| **Parity-lane cutover visual evidence: state-matched seed-versus-successor pair.** Whether a parity-lane cutover can textually pass while shipping a functionally-equivalent but visibly degraded reconstruction. | **Citation repaired 2026-08-20 — this cell rested on ignored paths, which this file's own reading rule forbids; the ruling itself is unchanged.** Rule stated inline, so the evidence resolves in a clone: the 2026-08-11 amendment requires that a parity-lane cutover record is incomplete without a typed, content-addressed, STATE-MATCHED seed-versus-successor visual pair — seed side drawn from the provenance-qualified seed visual corpus, successor side captured against the successor build at the same established interaction state through the isolated daemon+serve recipe (shared-daemon captures drift and are inadmissible), owner-judged evidence only and never an automated pixel-diff gate, N/A for the `greenfield-authorized-non-parity` surfaces. Tracked evidence: `apps/hypervisor/seed-ux-provenance.v1.json` and `apps/hypervisor/scripts/verify-hypervisor-seed-provenance.mjs` (provenance-qualified seed visual corpus, `greenfield-authorized-non-parity` typing, `seed_role` kind⇒role lock). Provenance only, explicitly NOT load-bearing because they do not resolve in a clone: the internal program guide's G-7 amendment, its W6.1 acceptance wording, and that checker's wording-presence rule. | **Ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** A parity-lane cutover record is incomplete without a typed, content-addressed, state-matched seed-versus-successor visual pair — seed side from the provenance-qualified seed visual corpus, successor side captured against the successor build via the isolated daemon+serve recipe at the same established interaction state; live-drift captures are inadmissible. The pair is owner-judged evidence only: never an automated pixel-diff gate, no numeric threshold — a passing pixel certificate still grants nothing. N/A for the five `greenfield-authorized-non-parity` surfaces (their record already types non-parity); the seed-role ruling is unchanged. **Deferral condition, filed by name:** the capture/compare mechanization lands with the FIRST parity-lane cutover packet, not before; until then the requirement binds the record's shape and no earlier unit may claim the pair satisfied or waived. |

### Journey verification VI (2026-08-12, next-legs VI run — dormant-proof canon + its machine gate, the verifier-family floor, W3.2 ProviderTransport, launch-chain file granularity)

**The verifier family had no floor, and now it does.** An independent peer filed
it during next-legs V: deleting assertions from a CI-gated verifier was silently
green — the deleted assertion stopped being checked, the survivors passed, CI
reported success. Sixteen gates ran and none of them watched the gates.
`check:verifier-floors` (#258) pins each verifier at the number of assertions it
actually EXECUTED, counted from the verifier's own completed run rather than a
source grep, so a commented-out assertion does not count. Its closed world is
re-derived from `.github/workflows/ci.yml` on every run and checked BOTH ways: a
CI-gated verifier with no floor row is RED, and a floor whose verifier CI does
not run is RED because it gates nothing. Pins are exact and ratchet up only —
above the pin is also RED, since slack is where a later deletion hides. Nine
mutations proven RED (deleted assertion, stale floor, stale census, missing
census, unset census dir, unregistered CI verifier, unknown script, non-gated
floor, out-of-world artifact), restore GREEN. 14 verifiers, 454 runtime
assertions. Three typed exclusions, each with its real reason: `seed-provenance`
is fail-ONLY (empty array on a green run, so a floor would read zero),
`ported-seed` keeps an integer counter but no named records, `owned-product-ui`
throws via `node:assert` and records nothing on success.

**W3.2 first cut: the first REGISTRY-driven model invocation** (#259). Before it,
the daemon's only real provider client was a boot-time singleton built from
process env, bypassing the CapabilityLease gateway entirely; no path let a
model-route record supply an endpoint or credential for a daemon-issued call. The
typed kernel `ModelInvocationReceipt` existed and had never been constructed
anywhere in `crates/node/`, and attempt lineage did not exist under any spelling.
`provider_transport.rs` adds the `ProviderTransport` trait (normalization,
streaming/cancellation, error and retryability CLASSIFICATION) with one native
`OllamaTransport`, behind `POST /v1/hypervisor/model-routes/:id/invoke` composing
identity-first rule E, INV-37, the registry's own `load_route_record`, the shared
write path, and the typed receipt. COMPOSED, NOT SHADOWED: no second census, no
second receipt shape, and a credentialed route REFUSES 501 rather than reading a
provider key from the environment. W4-F evidence is captured at the wire — token
mix, attempt lineage, latency incl. first-token, finish condition, verbatim
price-schedule ref — with every unreported field `null` AND named in
`unreported`, and `evidence_gaps` naming what the cut cannot fill at all.
Verified: `check:provider-transport` 13/13 (CI lane, deterministic, no provider
needed) and 28/28 live against real Ollama qwen2.5:7b — 36 input / 2 output
tokens observed, streaming first-token 86ms against 371ms total, idempotent
replay spending no second provider call, and a vanished provider producing an
honest `ProviderUnavailable` record rather than a fabricated completion.

**Launch-chain closes at FILE granularity** (#260), closing next-legs V's named
residual. The value scan already swept every persisted file, but the KEY-SET
close ran against exactly one record — the one found by this run's `launch_id` —
so a second-spine record minted under any other id was never key-set checked.
Four assertions now close existence and shape, at first-produce and again after
the daemon kill/restart over the complete family. Mutation-proven: a rogue
launch-family file under a different id drives both new assertions RED while the
PRE-EXISTING assertions do not notice it (51/53), which is the direct evidence
the residual was real. `check:launch-chain` 49 → 53.

**The dormant inference-computation-proof profile landed WITH a machine gate**
(#256). Dormant canon rots: nothing fails when an owner's block is stripped, and
nothing fails when a ninth file starts making proof claims outside the ownership
map. `check-architecture-docs` rule 9 derives its closed world from this file's
own lifecycle row and checks both directions, requires every carrying file to
type the profile `dormant`, and requires the posture owner to keep the exact
off/preferred/required triple. Five mutations RED, restore GREEN. Two gaps it
forced into the open: sas `service-marketplace.md` and `aiip.md` carried the
proof block WITHOUT typing it dormant, so an order block could have read as live;
both now carry an explicit lifecycle sentence.

**This run duplicated canon, and the tombstone above records how.** Its Leg 0b
re-filed two rulings that had landed the previous day in #255, on the strength of
an existence check that was wrong twice over: it grepped for a VENDOR NAME the
governing filing deliberately never used, and it ran against a LOCAL master that
had not been fetched and still pointed at #254, so even a correct subject-text
grep would have returned zero. The duplicate rows are collapsed to a tombstone;
the doctrine sections and map row that cut added are REAL canon and stay, because
#255 filed the ledger entry and Leg 0b filed the doctrine — each was half of one
correct filing. Two standing rules come out of it: **test canon existence by the
ruling's SUBJECT or disposition text, never a vendor or proper name**, and
**never make an absence claim against an unfetched local ref** — `git fetch`
before `git grep`, or the basis is an hour-old fiction.

**NAMED RESIDUALS carried out of this run (tracked, not waived):**
- **Economics join** — no `UsageRecord` is written by an invocation; the
  `model_tokens` meter class exists only in `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures. Named in
  every invocation record's `evidence_gaps`.
- **Credentialed model transports** — refuse 501 until a model-route
  CapabilityLease is minted and consumed. `openai_compatible` stays
  registry-admitted but transport-unimplemented.
- **One attempt per invocation.** Retry/fallback DECISIONS stay with the router.
- **CI has no model provider**, so the ProviderTransport live lane is not
  CI-gated — a named residual, never a silent skip.
- **Fork delegation-SUCCESS** stays unexercised: it needs a forkable source agent
  on the launch thread, which a fresh launch does not have. An assertion for it
  was written and DELETED before commit — the verifier already exercises the
  delegation-REQUESTED path more strictly, pinning the planner's exact refusal
  code, and a weaker duplicate that claims more than it checks is the
  decorative-assertion class this program keeps finding.
- **Three verifiers stay outside the floor** with typed reasons above.
- **Public copy** still carried a claim canon retired: ioi-ai `home.md` said
  "verifiable autonomous Workers" (repaired to `governed`); the technical
  whitepaper's "routine verifiable outcomes" / "generate verifiable outcomes" is
  a versioned artifact and its erratum-vs-next-revision disposition is an owner
  call. 31 files in that repo carry "verifiable"; most are a DIFFERENT and often
  defensible claim, so a grep sweep would retire true claims along with
  overclaims and the review is filed rather than performed.

### Ruling filed 2026-08-12 — fused outside-reader critique adoption (next-legs VII)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Whether a fused multi-frontier-model outside-reader critique of the public estate reorders the program.** The critique argued positioning, proof-sequencing, and machine-contract corrections: front-door overclaim, a public maturity table, a P0 multi-hop authority contract, prompt-injection release gating, a legal-principal boundary, a two-sovereign proof shortened past embodied gating, and an outsider-runnable conformance profile. | Load-bearing citations byte-verified before ruling: the README front door's retired-claim phrasing; the conformance ladder's honest admissions (no outside runner, no designated reference release, unasserted two-client axes); the typed portable-verifier gap in `authority-and-access.md`; H2B's embodied gating. The adoption plan and its amendments live at `internal-docs/prompts/architecture-relative-comparison/runs/2026-08-12-fused-outside-reader-critique-adoption-plan.md` (ignored internal registry). First executed legs: ioi-ai `c134c5a` (claim-verb retirement, telecom/6G disambiguation, retired-claims registry in the build parity audit — which caught live drift in both `llms.txt` machine summaries on its first run) and [#262](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/262) (H2B core/embodied split; `portable-authority-v3` criteria + negative-fixture contract, adversarially reviewed with four findings applied). | **Ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** Adopted as a course correction, not a redesign, via typed units through the program's own admission discipline. External critique is an advisory re-rating trigger — it never self-executes work, reorders the MVP graph, or displaces the code lane's standing NEXT (the economics join). The critique's demands are often priority-arguments over existing typed targets, not gap discoveries: the maturity table exists as the conformance ladder (the correction is to surface and GENERATE it — hand-written maturity tables are forbidden), and the successor authority contract was already the named `portable-authority-v3` target (its real contribution, the negative-fixture list, is absorbed there). Remaining legs: README front-door parity cut with a retired-claims registry; the legal-principal boundary filed as a dormant typed target through the generalized dormant-target gate (a terms-root acceptance is protocol evidence, not automatically an enforceable contract; a Worker is never presumed a legal person); the generated maturity surface. Non-schedules recorded, not scheduled: an outsider-runnable conformance profile only via owner pull backed by demand evidence AND born under the standing verifier discipline — a public runner that cannot fail certifies nothing; a second implementation and interop CI after that condition is defined; token/L1 doctrine unchanged; the whitepaper sweep stays an owner call. |

### Journey verification IX (2026-08-13, next-legs IX run — the economics join, credential custody, the second native transport, and the verifier-floor exclusions)

**W3.2 stopped being a single proven invocation and became a metered, custodied, multi-dialect
runtime.** Four legs, each with live-daemon proof and named residuals; the rulings and open defects
they produced are filed in their own sections below and above.

**Leg 1 — the economics join** ([#275](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/275)). An
invocation observed and receipted everything W4-F needs and wrote no `UsageRecord`;
`economics.usage_record` sat in every record's own `evidence_gaps`, and the `model_tokens` meter
class existed only in `#[cfg(test)]` rate-card fixtures — so `runtime_receipt_refs`, the registered
contract's binding from a charge back to the evidence that earned it, had no producer either. The
invocation now hands the ledger an OBSERVED `u64` and lets it price, sequence and hash-chain on its
own terms; the quantity crosses that boundary in a parameter that cannot carry caller JSON, and seven
charge-describing request fields refuse (the INV-37 idiom applied to HOW MUCH rather than WHO). ONE
resolver serves both a pre-execution preflight and the append, so the two can never disagree. Order
is load-bearing: the invocation is ADMITTED first so the charge cites a receipt that exists —
appending first could leave an orphan charge naming a record that never landed, which is exactly the
unauditable charge `economics_usage_evidence_required` refuses. **No branch writes a zero:** an
unreported mix mints no row and records `economics_join_token_mix_absent`, so W4-F can tell "not
metered" from "metered at zero". Live against real Ollama: 36 in / 2 out observed → 38 charged at
rate 5 → 190 micro work credits, sequence 1, join key resolving both ways, replay adding no row, and
a `FinalDebit` over the billed quote still refusing without a live grant (`require_spend_authority`
untouched).

**Leg 2 — model-route credential custody**
([#276](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/276)), ruled in its own section below. Two
CapabilityLease crossings; the kernel planner's always-unsatisfiable `provider_credential_lease_ref`
requirement finally satisfiable. **Its merge-blocking adversarial review found a key-exfiltration
path that a green 60/60 self-review had missed** — the crossing bound the route id but not the
DESTINATION, so an owner's already-approved grant survived a repoint. That, plus impossible key
rotation, a destructive rollback, a cross-tenant revoke, a forgeable custody label and a
reflected-bearer leak, are repaired and mutation-proven; the review's scope is recorded in that
section.

**Leg 3 — the second native transport**
([#280](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/280)), verified in its own section above.
`OpenAiCompatibleTransport` conforms to a contract it did not define, unchanged, and the economics
join written against the first dialect charges the second dialect's mix onto the same ledger with no
code change. **Adapted-transport absorption is now eligible** under the #255/#257 native-first gate.

**Leg 4 — the verifier-floor exclusions**
([#278](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/278)). `check:verifier-floors` shipped with three
typed exclusions; two named the exact work to close them and are now closed. `ported-seed` emits 11
named invariants over 413 raw assertions; `seed-provenance` converts from FAIL-ONLY (an empty
failures array on a green run, so a count read zero) to one positive CHECKPOINT per invariant group
with its 62 fail sites untouched. Both deliberately record per-INVARIANT rather than per-item: a
census scaled to the owned bundle's file count would duplicate `check:shipped-products`, which
already pins that count and tree hash, and would churn the floor on every asset change. Family: 14 →
16 verifiers, 465 → 490 assertions, 3 → 1 exclusions (`owned-product-ui` stays on its standing
reason). In one mutation the verifier itself still exited 0 reporting "10/10 passed" — the silent-
green hole the floor exists to close.

**WHAT THE STANDING DISCIPLINE ACTUALLY CAUGHT THIS RUN, recorded because the value is in the
specifics.** Seventeen mutations were proven RED across the four legs, and **three assertions were
decorative until re-aimed — each PASSED against the very mutation it existed to catch**: Leg 1's
billing-preflight ordering assertion (the refusal answers 404 whether the preflight runs before or
after execution, so it proved nothing about order — it now counts provider connections through a
forwarder); Leg 3's anti-exfiltration assertion (it checked only the reported posture and passed with
a real outbound request added to the probe — it now counts connections to a listener that answers
nothing); and Leg 1's pricing assertion (it folded in the expected quantity and so fired on a
quantity defect its label did not claim — it now prices against the quantity the row carries). Three
further assertions were decoupled from absolute chain length so each measures the delta it owns.
**`check:mutation-handlers` caught a defect in this run's own first rollback** — it discarded whether
the removal succeeded, so a refused bind could strand a secret while reporting a clean refusal; fixed
rather than exempted, which is the outcome that classifier exists for.

**A FALSE CLAIM THIS RUN PUT IN CANON AND THEN CORRECTED.** The Leg 2 ruling's first draft stated the
process-environment provider key and the leased path "are never live in the same deployment",
fencing the env key on `daemon_exposed()`. The adversarial review falsified it: the estate's own
documented exposed topology runs the daemon on loopback behind `serve`, so `daemon_exposed()` is
false exactly where the daemon is publicly reachable. Exposure cannot be inferred at boot, so the
fence no longer tries — it requires an explicit `IOI_HYPERVISOR_ALLOW_ENV_PROVIDER_KEY=1` opt-in and
fails closed. **The standing lesson is the one this program keeps relearning in new clothing: a claim
about DEPLOYMENT needs the same evidence discipline as a claim about code, and "I reasoned about the
bind address" is not that evidence.**

**NAMED RESIDUALS carried out of this run (tracked, not waived):**
- **The model-route registry mutation surface is unauthenticated** — filed as its own open defect
  section above, not fixed here. The credential-exfiltration consequence is fenced; registry
  integrity is not.
- **A transiently refused economics join is permanent for that invocation.** Replay is a pure read
  and does not retry it; the record says so rather than papering over it.
- **One `model_tokens` meter charges input+output at one rate.** Cache and reasoning classes are
  reported by no transport in this estate yet and are folded in nowhere.
- **`ModelRuntimeErrorClass` has no AUTHENTICATION class**, so 401/403 maps to `PolicyRefusal` with
  the detail naming it an auth refusal.
- **Caller-initiated mid-flight cancellation is not exposed**; the transports enforce a wall-clock
  ceiling and a stall ceiling only. **One attempt per invocation** still stands.
- **The `--live` and `--live-authority` lanes are not CI-gated** — they need a model provider and a
  real wallet.network cluster respectively. A named residual, never a silent skip.
- **The custody lease is not re-verified at invoke**; the per-call use lease is the invoke authority.
- **`owned-product-ui` remains the one floor exclusion**, on its standing reason.

### Ruling filed 2026-08-13 — model-route credential custody, and the disposition of the process-environment provider key (next-legs IX Leg 2)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A model route could not hold a credential, and the daemon's one credentialed model call held its key in the process environment.** Compute `ProviderAccount`s have had real sealed per-kind BYOK across nine provider kinds, resolved at the CapabilityLease gateway, for far longer; a model route got a `credential_binding` of `{kind: "env_key_report", env_key_name}` — a note about which environment variable a human was supposed to have exported. Meanwhile `resolve_inference` (`crates/node/src/bin/hypervisor-daemon.rs`) read `OPENAI_API_KEY` at boot and handed it to the `InferenceRuntime` the session harness and agentops consume: a real provider crossing with no lease, no wallet authority, no receipt, and no revocation surface. | Byte-verified at `c9569751f`: the model-route registry's create handler rejects plaintext secrets and stores only an env key NAME; `provider_transport.rs` refused every credentialed route with `provider_credential_lease_unimplemented` (501) rather than reproduce the env bypass; `resolve_inference` was the only credentialed model call in the daemon. **A second, unreachable-refusal defect surfaced while building the fix:** `runtime_model_route_mutation_admission` has always refused to enable or bind a route whose credential posture is `provider_vault_token` / `wallet_credential_lease` unless a `provider_credential_lease_ref` with a `lease:` prefix is present, and `compose_mutation_admission` reads that field from `credential_binding` — which nothing ever wrote. The gate was correct and unsatisfiable: a credentialed model route could not be enabled at all. | **Ruled (owner, owner-reversible) and executed.** (1) **Custody exists.** A model route seals a provider key into `model-route-credentials` through `POST /v1/hypervisor/model-routes/:id/credential`, with `DELETE` as the revocation surface the lease descriptor names. The store is separate from `connector-credentials` because the two families are revoked by different surfaces and audited under different backing providers. (2) **TWO crossings, both at `authorize_capability_lease`.** Establishing custody is one authority (`model.credential.bind`); USING it is another, minted per invocation (`model.invoke`). The planner's `lease:` prefix settles the reading — the route records the custody lease's id, not a pointer at a vault. Satisfying that prefix with a custody-shaped ref would have been label-fitting. (3) **Ordering is a correctness property.** Every refusal a pure read can discover — unknown route, unimplemented transport, unprobed availability, an unresolvable billing quote — precedes the crossing, because the crossing atomically consumes one wallet-owned grant use and a caller's own request error must not spend owner authority. (4) **A refused bind rolls back and SAYS SO.** The secret is sealed before the crossing (the gateway resolves it as part of deciding), so a refusal removes it and verifies the removal; a removal that fails answers `model_route_credential_rollback_failed` naming the stranded key, because reporting a clean refusal over surviving key material is the defect class `check:mutation-handlers` exists to find — and that gate caught exactly this in the first cut of this work. (5) **The env path is PINNED as a typed development-only posture, not retired, and FAILS CLOSED.** `resolve_inference` still feeds consumers that have no route record yet, so deleting it would break them rather than move them onto custody; instead the process-environment key is refused unless the operator explicitly opts in with `IOI_HYPERVISOR_ALLOW_ENV_PROVIDER_KEY=1`. The first cut fenced this on `daemon_exposed()`, which an adversarial review showed is the wrong signal — the estate's own exposed topology runs the daemon on loopback behind `serve`, so `daemon_exposed()` is false exactly where the daemon is publicly reachable, and an earlier draft of THIS row wrongly claimed "both paths are never live in the same deployment." Exposure cannot be inferred at boot, so the fence no longer tries: it requires a deliberate flag, which makes the disposition accurate — the env path is off by default and the operator turns it on knowing it bypasses custody. **Named target:** move the remaining `InferenceRuntime` consumers onto route records with sealed credentials, after which that branch deletes rather than narrows.

**Merge-blocking adversarial from-source review of the credential seam (Leg 2d) found a key-exfiltration path and this cut does not merge without its repairs — a green 60/60 self-review would have shipped it.** (a) The invoke crossing's grant hash bound only the route id, not the DESTINATION, so a grant approved for "invoke route R" stayed valid after R's `base_url` was repointed — an owner's ordinary grant would ship the sealed key to an attacker-chosen host. Fixed by folding `base_url`/`model_id` into the crossing facets AND freezing a credentialed route's `base_url`/posture against `PATCH` while custody exists (`model_route_credentialed_config_frozen`) — a credentialed route cannot be repointed without first revoking, which is owner-scoped. (b) The bind was genesis-only, so a route could hold a credential exactly once — a compromised key was unrotatable; now a successor CAS chains rotations and revoke leaves a tombstone that keeps the stream head. (c) The vault write preceded the crossing and the rollback DELETED, so a wrong-owner or failed rebind destroyed the incumbent; now an owner-scope check precedes any write and rollback RESTORES the prior bytes. (d) Revoke admitted under a different resource ref than bind, leaving revocation ownership unclaimed; now both share `model-route-credential://{id}` so the bind's owner pin governs revoke. (e) `key_source` was caller-settable (a forgeable durable custody label); now server-derived via `scm_key_source()`. (f) The upstream error body was recorded verbatim, so a reflecting host could echo the `Authorization` header into the hash-chained event stream; now hashed, never stored raw. All verified in the `--live-authority` lane against a real one-validator wallet.network cluster (64/64: rotation, revoke→rebind, PATCH-freeze, forged-key_source-absent, bearer-never-durable) and mutation-proven RED on each finding. |

### Journey verification 2026-08-13 — the second native transport conforms; adapted-transport work becomes eligible (next-legs IX Leg 3)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **`ProviderTransport` had exactly one implementation, so nothing showed whether the contract GENERALIZES or had simply been fitted to Ollama.** The absorption ruling gates adapted-transport work on "ONE native provider proven end-to-end first, so the native path defines the transport contract rather than ported adapters defining it by default" — one implementation cannot demonstrate that. `openai_compatible` was registry-admitted but transport-unimplemented and refused 501. | `OpenAiCompatibleTransport` conforms to the trait **unchanged** — no change to `ProviderTransport`, the typed `ModelInvocationReceipt`, attempt lineage, the credential crossing, or the economics join — while differing from the first dialect in every mechanical respect: SSE frames with a non-JSON `[DONE]` sentinel rather than NDJSON, `usage` rather than `prompt_eval_count`/`eval_count`, an HTTP error envelope with a typed `error.code`, and cache/reasoning token classes Ollama has no concept of. Proven live against Ollama's REAL OpenAI-compatible endpoint (`/v1/chat/completions`) — a genuine second dialect against a real server, no fixture: 62/62 `--live`, including SSE streaming with separated first-token latency, real `usage`-derived counts, and **the economics join charging the second dialect's observed mix onto the same hash-chained ledger with no code change**. CI lane 31/31 (floor 24 → 31, raised in-commit). Three mutations RED. | **Executed.** (1) **Dispatch is an enum, not `dyn`** — the trait's `invoke` is an async fn and not object-safe; the one match that knows a route's dialect is also the only thing a third conformer touches. (2) **Executability is per-transport, because what a PROBE can honestly learn is per-transport.** Ollama's probe reads a real catalog so `available` means served; the openai_compatible probe is POSTURE-ONLY under the standing anti-exfiltration ruling — the daemon never sends a credential to a caller-supplied `base_url` to discover a catalog — so `credentials_present` is the strongest state it can reach, and requiring `available` there would make every such route permanently unexecutable on a category error. **The probe's no-contact property is now OBSERVED**, not asserted: the verifier points the route at a connection-counting listener and requires zero connections. An earlier revision checked only the reported posture and PASSED with a real outbound request added to the probe. (3) **Sealed custody counts as credential presence** — a route whose key is in `model-route-credentials` has a strictly better credential than an env-key report, and reporting it `credentials_missing` would have left every properly-custodied route unexecutable. Still posture-only: it reads a local record and sends nothing. (4) **The legacy env-key posture is dead upstream** — a `provider_vault_token` route whose only credential is an env-key REPORT cannot be ACTIVATED at all, because the kernel planner demands a custody lease ref; verified with the declared variable really set in the daemon's environment and absent from every durable byte. (5) **A provider-reported `content_filter` finish is receipted as a typed FAILURE with its observed mix**, never as a short success — the tokens it consumed are real and remain billable. **Named residuals:** the kernel's `ModelRuntimeErrorClass` has no AUTHENTICATION class, so 401/403 maps to `PolicyRefusal` with the detail naming it an auth refusal — adding a kernel variant is wider than this cut; caller-initiated mid-flight cancellation is not exposed by the route (the transport enforces a wall-clock ceiling and a stall ceiling only); one attempt per invocation still stands. **Adapted-transport absorption is now ELIGIBLE** under the #255/#257 ruling — provider-protocol knowledge only, behind this contract, with a LICENSE-MANIFEST entry per ADR 0033 for anything transcribed. |

### Journey verification X (2026-08-13, next-legs X Leg 2 — the router decides, and refuses what a deterministic crossing cannot honestly do)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Transports CLASSIFIED retryability and nothing DECIDED.** `retryable` was a field no code read; attempt lineage was a one-element list under every spelling; and W4-F's cost-per-successful-unit and failure-rate fields had no data to compute from, because no invocation could record more than one attempt. **The first cut of this leg then hit the exact hazard the resume-checkpoint names.** A credential crossing is deterministic by construction: `capability_lease_request_hash` folds `{domain, allowed_tools, resource_refs, scopes, facets}` and nothing else — no nonce, no attempt index — and the consumption commitment built from it is keyed on the same fields plus the grant. A SECOND crossing for one route under one grant is therefore byte-identical to the first and is refused `authority_operation_already_admitted`. A retry on a credentialed route could never have succeeded; it would have spent a grant use, contacted the provider, and returned a bare 409 with no record, no receipt and no ledger row, because every durable write sits below that return. The same determinism defeats a credentialed FALLBACK from the other side: the facets bind `base_url` and `model_id` (the IX anti-exfiltration hardening), so one request's single `wallet_approval_grant` cannot satisfy a second route. | **A merge-blocking adversarial from-source review found all of it; the green 29/29 self-review did not**, because every route in that lane was `no_credentials_required` and short-circuits before any crossing — the verifier named a MEASUREMENT gap where the truth was an IMPOSSIBILITY. Live daemon throughout, against controllable local providers speaking the real Ollama and OpenAI-compatible wire shapes: 38/38, **twelve mutations proven RED**, each on the assertion that owns its finding. Two of those assertions were decorative until re-aimed: the accumulation proof passed against charging only the final attempt (ollama failures carry no token mix, so the two quantities were equal — it now runs a metered `content_filter` failure where they differ 9410 vs 10, and reads the durable LEDGER ROW rather than the daemon's account of itself), and the spend-gate proof was answered entirely by "quote not found" and would have passed with `require_spend_authority` deleted (it now posts against a real held quote and pins `403` + `economics_spend_authority_required`). Floored in the same commit: 17 → 18 verifiers, 533 → 571 assertions. `check:provider-transport` 31/31 and `check:model-route-authority` 43/43 against the new loop. | **Executed and ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** (1) **The router decides; the transport still only classifies.** `decide_after_failure` reads the transport's `retryable`, the caller's authorized policy, and its position in the target list — never the provider's body, and it never re-classifies. (2) **RETRIES ARE OFF BY DEFAULT and bounded 1..=4.** Each attempt against a credentialed route is its own crossing and so consumes another wallet-owned grant use; a router that retried by default would spend owner authority nobody authorized. An out-of-range OR unreadable budget REFUSES rather than clamping — silently clamping is the same defect arriving through the type system. (3) **RETRY AND FALLBACK ARE REFUSED ON CREDENTIALED ROUTES**, typed (`model_invocation_multi_attempt_credentialed_unsupported`) and checked on the DECLARED records before admission, because it is a refusal about request shape. The SPEND SEMANTICS are RULED (owner, 2026-08-13), so what the residual needs is BUILD, not a decision — and the two cases are NOT the same authority boundary. A RETRY on one credentialed route is ONE grant carrying `max_usages: N`, the field the substrate already enforces (`governed_authority.rs`), because every attempt crosses the same boundary (same owner, destination and model); three separately signed grants would duplicate one bounded approval and ignore what `max_usages` exists to express. The build it needs is a per-attempt EFFECT identity — an attempt ordinal folded into the facets — so consumption N+1 is a distinct operation rather than a replay of N, plus idempotent recovery of an already-consumed attempt that spends no further use. A FALLBACK to a different route is a DIFFERENT boundary — a destination the primary's grant does not cover — so it genuinely needs its own grant per credentialed hop; borrowing the primary's grant for another destination would be exactly the weakening `require_spend_authority` exists to prevent, which is why this cut refuses that case rather than reaching across it. (4) **Fallbacks are caller-DECLARED, never inferred.** A router choosing its own alternates would mint a placement policy beside the venue picker and W4-F's advisory economics — a second switching mechanism — and W4-F's ruling is explicit that economic comparison "neither grants route rights nor independently authorizes placement". Every hop is owner-checked and passes the SAME admission as the primary, so a fallback is not a privileged path to an unimplemented transport or an unprobed provider. (5) **THE CHARGE FOLLOWS THE WHOLE LINEAGE.** A failed attempt the provider metered is real usage the supplier bills; charging only the final attempt undercharged silently and left `evidence_gaps` empty, which is worse than an absent measurement. `TokenMix::accumulate` sums what every attempt OBSERVED while keeping absent absent — `None` is unreported, never zero — with a unit test pinning the distinction. (6) **The record names the route that ANSWERED**, with `requested_route_id` beside it; the first cut attributed a fallback's work to the primary. (7) **Bounded in wall-clock and in aggression:** a router-level deadline stops new attempts (a per-attempt ceiling alone allowed ~32 minutes on one request), and a retry backs off before re-hitting a provider that may have rate-limited it. **NAMED RESIDUALS (tracked, not waived):** multi-attempt and multi-hop invocation of CREDENTIALED routes is unavailable — the largest named gap this cut leaves — but its SPEND SEMANTICS are ruled (retry = one grant with `max_usages: N`; fallback = a grant per destination), so what remains is the build named in (3), not an open decision; a mid-loop credential refusal still returns without recording, which is unreachable while (3) stands and becomes live the moment it is lifted; the per-attempt crossing itself is countable only in the `--live-authority` lane, which is not CI-gated; an upstream 401 sweeps a declared chain, spending one grant use per hop of the caller's OWN budget; and a deployment ADMINISTRATOR may name any principal's route as a fallback, resolving that principal's sealed key under the admin's invocation record — no escalation, since an admin may invoke it directly, but stated here rather than only in a verifier comment. |

### Journey verification X (2026-08-13, next-legs X Leg 1 — the model-route registry gets an owner, and the ownership boundary turns out not to be the tenant)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The open defect below is CLOSED, and closing it falsified the first shape of its own fix.** The registry surface is wider than the filed row said: censusing the router rather than trusting the row found **eleven** mutating endpoints, not seven — `session-bindings` (a durable write plus an inline outbound probe), `invoke`, and both credential handlers were never named. Worse, the first cut scoped ownership to the caller's TENANT, passed its own 42/42 verifier, and isolated **nothing**: `validate_membership_tenant` ([`lifecycle_routes.rs`](../../../crates/node/src/bin/hypervisor_daemon_routes/lifecycle_routes.rs)) admits the literal `org://local` and 404s every other `org://` ref, and every product onboarding lane that is not the bootstrap operator — OIDC/SSO auto-join, SCIM provisioning, org-invite accept — grants that same ref through `ensure_provisioned_org_membership`. In the multi-user product shape every principal's admitted scope is exactly `["org://local"]`, so `authorizes_tenant(owner)` was true for **every** principal in the deployment. The gate would have refused an anonymous caller and admitted every authenticated one: the filed exploit chain intact, merely re-labelled. | **A merge-blocking adversarial from-source review found it; the green fixture could not, because the fixture was built in a shape the product never produces** — its second principal was hand-placed in a `project://` tenant no onboarding lane creates. Repaired and re-verified at 43/43 with the fixture rebuilt to the PRODUCT shape: two ordinary members both in `org://local`, with their tenant sameness asserted as a precondition so every isolation assertion below would go red if ownership drifted back to the tenant. **Ten mutations proven RED**, each on the assertion that owns its finding — including `M7`, which restores the exact tenant-scoped check that shipped green, and turns twelve assertions red. Live daemon throughout; no fixture corpus. CI lane added (`check:model-route-authority`) and floored in the same commit (family 16 → 17 verifiers, 490 → 533 runtime assertions). `check:provider-transport` re-run 31/31 against the new invoke gate; the registry verifier was converted from an unrunnable anonymous script to a hermetic authenticated one, 38/38. | **Executed and ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** (1) **OWNERSHIP IS PER-PRINCIPAL, NOT PER-TENANT**, and the check reads the substrate's own pin: `bind_request_resource_scope` at create binds `model-route://{id}` to the creating principal, genesis-CAS'd so it cannot be re-claimed, and `authorize_request_resource_scope` reads it on every mutation. The record's `owner_ref` field is descriptive; it is never the thing that authorizes. Until this deployment can construct more than one organization, the principal is the only isolation boundary that exists — a tenant check is decorative by construction. (2) **THE WHOLE SURFACE, derived not listed.** All eleven mutating endpoints refuse anonymously, and the verifier re-derives that list from the router source on every run, so an endpoint added later is covered or the gate goes red. Three of them were doors the first cut left open: `POST /v1/hypervisor/sessions` bound any principal's route into a session (and because a binding blocks its route's deletion and **no binding-revocation surface exists**, that was an unremovable denial of service on another principal's record); the credential slot's first-binder-wins pin let a stranger freeze a route's `base_url` and posture against PATCH via the IX defence itself; and `invoke` — the operation that actually spends a route — checked nothing. (3) **USE is gated with CONFIGURATION.** A registry that decides who may configure a route but not who may use it has drawn the boundary in the wrong place. (4) **Ordering is a security property twice over.** Authentication precedes every record read (rule E), and the credential gate precedes the posture check, because a refusal naming `credential_posture` is an answer about someone else's record. (5) **The seed is typed `system://hypervisor`**, and an ownerless legacy record is deployment-held rather than free — absence of an owner is not permission. The typed backfill happens in `save_route`, under the registry lock every mutation already holds, rather than from a read handler that holds none. (6) **`select-default` is deployment-scoped regardless of route ownership**, because the default is a global singleton and selecting it demotes a record the caller may not own. **NAMED RESIDUALS (tracked, not waived):** registry **reads** stay unauthenticated, so an authenticated caller still learns route existence via 404-vs-403 and anyone learns the route list — this leg closed mutation and use, not reads; **route mutations record no acting principal** (`route_receipt` writes no `principal_ref`, unlike the credential family's admitted events), so the registry now decides who *may* act while storing no answer to who *did*; **`create` mints a wall-clock route id**, so its idempotency key cannot make a retried create idempotent and duplicate routes are mintable; **no session-binding revocation surface exists**, so a legitimate binding still pins its route against deletion; **`verify-m4-outcome-room-system-spine.mjs` is left BLOCKED by this change** — it drives the daemon wholly anonymously, and the repair it needs (an authenticated operator session) flips the auth posture the rest of that verifier assumes, which is a change to that verifier's own design and not this leg's; it is not CI-gated. |

### Open defect filed 2026-08-13 — the model-route registry mutation surface is unauthenticated (next-legs IX Leg 2, spun out) — **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by next-legs X Leg 1; see Journey verification X above**

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Every model-route mutation handler authenticates only at the global auth gate, not per-request, because a model route carries no owner.** `handle_model_route_patch`, `_probe`, `_enable`, `_disable`, `_select_default`, `_create`, and `_delete` take `State` + `AxumPath` (+ `Json`) and NO `HeaderMap`; none resolves a caller or an owner scope. The record has no `owner_ref`/`tenant` field at all. Under the default `auto` auth policy the only gate is `daemon_exposed() || request_exposed(headers)`, which is false for the loopback-behind-`serve` topology — so on the estate's documented exposed deployment these mutations are reachable unauthenticated. | Byte-verified at HEAD on `w32/model-route-lease`: the seven handler signatures; `read_record_dir("model-route-registry")` records carry no owner; the adversarial review's finding #1 exploit chain (unauthenticated `PATCH base_url` → `probe` → repoint). The **credential-exfiltration consequence is fenced in the Leg-2 cut above** (destination bound into the invoke grant hash + a credentialed route's config frozen against PATCH), so a bound key cannot be re-aimed. What remains is registry integrity: an unauthenticated party can still repoint an UNCREDENTIALED route, flip enable/disable, or vandalize display fields, and an env-var existence oracle (`credential_binding.env_key_name` → `std::env::var`) is settable the same way. | **Filed OPEN as its own leg, not fixed in Leg 2 (owner-reversible).** Authenticating the whole surface needs a route OWNERSHIP model — capture the owner at `create`, migrate the seed route, and owner-scope every mutation — which is a registry-wide change with its own verifier, well outside a credential-custody cut. Fixing one handler would falsely imply the surface is closed. **Residual named in Leg 2:** even with the destination fenced, a party who repoints an uncredentialed route and then persuades the owner to bind + approve a grant against it exfiltrates on the owner's own approval; the grant facets now NAME the destination, so an owner who inspects them sees the host, but the approval surface decoding those facets is part of this leg. The public "stronger than incumbents" claim stays gated on this and the standing Journeys B/C/D/G bar. |

### Journey verification XII (2026-08-13, next-legs XII — SURF-ontology: the governed controls that were neither real nor named)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Seven of this surface's thirteen governed controls were reachable by no path at all, and were not shown as disabled named gaps either.** `SURF-ontology` holds 13 of the estate's 24 `governed_receipted_action` controls — the highest concentration of the twenty. Six were real (create-ontology plus five inspector EDIT forms). The other seven — the five New-menu create entries, the section New-object-type button, and the 5-step wizard — had daemon authority that SHIPPED (`upsert-*` creates on a fresh id) and module actions that were DECLARED, but the Manager rendered no authoring form at all unless a definition was already selected, and the gaps note never named them. Silently absent is the one state the read-truth · local-UI-state · receipted-authority · disabled-named-gap ladder does not allow, and no assertion could see it because every authoring assertion drove the action lane directly instead of asking the page whether a control existed. | Live against an isolated daemon + serve throughout. `check:ontology-journey` **46/46** (was 36), floored in the same commit (36 → 46; family 654 → 657). **Eight mutations proven RED-ON-TARGET.** The seed gate was RE-CONFIRMED on ground rather than trusted from its dated row: `--require-ready` green, and both protected graphs replay `complete_interaction_route_graph=true` (schema 32 edges, explorer 3, zero problems). **A merge-blocking adversarial from-source review found a SHIP-BLOCKING defect in the first cut of this very fix** — see the paragraph below — plus six more, all applied. | **Executed and ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** (1) **12 of 13 controls are now REAL**; the 5-step wizard is the one that stays a gap and is rendered as a DISABLED NAMED CONTROL with its reason, not as prose. (2) **CREATE MEANS CREATE.** `upsert-*` merges onto an existing id — correct for the edit lane, catastrophic for a create entry, which would silently rewrite another author's definition and still return a receipt. Create entries carry an explicit intent and refuse a duplicate typed (`ontology_definition_exists`); the edit lane is unchanged. (3) **A CONTROL THAT CANNOT SUCCEED IS NOT REAL.** Entries whose required selects would render empty (a link or action type on an ontology with no object types) render as disabled named gaps naming their own precondition. (4) **The frozen census is updated to match** — 12 of 13 `implemented: true`. Only `implemented` moved; `outcome` describes the REFERENCE control's class and is not ours to rewrite, so the estate total stays 24. |

**THE AUTHORITY RULING, AND WHY THE BRIEF WAS WRONG.** `surfaces/ontology.md` §4 targeted the seven
authoring actions at "re-plumb through the W0.3 CapabilityLease client (this plane today mutates
without a wallet challenge)". That target is **withdrawn as stale**. Canon rules ontology authoring
an ordinary governed mutation, not an authority crossing:
`domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md` — *"Local canonicality is the default. An organization or
autonomous-system domain may canonically define its own objects, actions, assertions, and
policies… wallet.network supplies that path when portable delegated authority, secrets, decryption
leases, external account access, or high-risk approval is required."* Authoring your own ontology is
none of those. Ruling OQ-1 reached the same conclusion for the sibling object
(`OntologySurfaceDescriptor`), OQ-11 reaffirmed it and forbade stale contradicting wording, and the
frozen census binds all 13 of these controls to plain receipted POST/PATCH with **zero** lease
bindings. The built shape — identity-first (rule E), `expected_revision` CAS,
`ontology-receipt.v1`, fail-closed on a missing receipt, `acting_principal_ref` per INV-37 — IS the
target. **Re-plumbing would have invented authority canon does not require and made every schema
edit demand a wallet approval.** The brief row is corrected in place. Two caveats recorded rather
than buried: the module already routes through the authority client as a no-lease pass-through, so
a future 428/403 would be legible without re-plumbing; and canon separately forbids an ontology
redefining constitutional purpose, prohibitions, authority ceilings or terminal obligations, and
names constitution-bound roots as protected refs — a GOVERNANCE check that **does not exist on this
lane** and is named below as a residual.

**THE SHIP-BLOCKING DEFECT THIS RUN SHIPPED INTO ITS OWN FIX, AND WHY THE GATE COULD NOT SEE IT.**
The first cut's `New → Action type` entry sent a hardcoded `kind="action"`. The daemon enumerates
`create_object | modify_object | delete_object | function`, so **every submit refused** — a control
that renders, claims to be real, and can never succeed. It was the SAME defect this cut had already
made and fixed once (a `one-to-many` placeholder where the daemon enumerates `one_to_many`),
re-shipped in the sibling field, *under a comment claiming the create forms reuse the edit lane's
vocab-driven selects*. It survived because **the new assertions hand-picked their field values**:
they proved the ACTION worked and said nothing about the FORM, so no assertion ever POSTed
`upsert-action-type` at all. The assertions now **scrape each rendered form's own fields and submit
that**, which is what made the mutation catchable. The same weakness recurred a third time in the
property assertion and was caught only because its mutation came back GREEN twice.

**WHAT THE STANDING DISCIPLINE CAUGHT THAT THE REVIEW DID NOT.** Two mutations returned
GREEN-NOT-CAUGHT and forced repairs. The second of them — stripping the create intent from the one
form labelled "Add a property" — exposed that **the property inspector branch has been unreachable
since before this leg**: `resolveDef()` returns `{ot, p}` for a property while the render chain
tested `if (def.ot)` first, so selecting a property always rendered its owning object type and the
property inspector had never displayed. That mattered the moment "Add" became create-only, because
the property EDIT form lives in that branch and editing would have been stranded. Branch order
fixed; the inspector renders for the first time. Separately,
`verify-hypervisor-operational-depth.mjs` **crashes on the committed tree and is not CI-gated at
all** — which is precisely how the depth ledger drifted into claiming "create entry not rendered"
for controls that render. Both are named as residuals below, not fixed here.

**A FALSE FINDING THIS RUN ALMOST FILED.** The first seed-gate replay reported
`complete_interaction_route_graph=false` on all three graphs. That was a BROKEN SHARED RUNTIME — a
`serve` process up with its daemon down — not a broken gate; the isolated recipe replays green. The
bad run also **overwrote a tracked seed graph**, replacing eight specific per-control problem
records with two "cannot re-establish node" lines; that churn was reverted. A verification pass must
not restamp content-addressed artifacts, and a gate is not failing merely because the thing you
pointed it at was not running.

**SURF-ontology REMAINS OPEN — it is not closed by this cut and must not be read as closed.** ACC-A
requires create/version/**propose**/read/**search**/**saved-set** journeys to close. Derived from
the router rather than from guessed URLs: there is **no ontology proposal/branch family** (the only
`proposal` routes are Foundry qualification and Intelligence improvement), **no** saved
object-set/exploration family, and **no** object-instance search family (the `instances` matches are
`managed-worker-instances`, a different plane). Three of the six named journeys have no daemon
family at all — they are the W3 backend work in the brief's §5 PRs 6–7 — and the per-owner cutover
with typed 410s is W6.1. **NAMED RESIDUALS (tracked, not waived):** the constitutional-ceiling
governance check canon requires of ontology evolution does not exist on this lane; the
operational-depth verifier crashes and gates nothing, so the depth ledger has no machine guard; the
5-step wizard is a disabled named gap; object-instance execution, proposals, saved sets and search
remain typed absences; and the legacy `/__ioi/ontology/*` mounts keep serving until that cutover.

### Journey verification XI (2026-08-13, next-legs XI — W3.3 durable custody: a backup crosses to a fresh daemon, and what was deleted stays deleted)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **W3.3's acceptance sentence — "a fresh-daemon restore with custody, retention, and deletion proof" — was unreachable in all three of its parts, and the substrate hid how close it already was.** The managed-runtime plane had a real capture path, a manifest-complete `HypervisorEnvironmentBackup` admission, and a staged restore with rollback and interruption-resume. But a backup could not LEAVE the daemon that captured it: the export lane handed back the raw workspace tar — payload bytes with no record, no manifest, no provenance — and there was no import at all. RETENTION was canon nothing enforced: `compile_backup_record` wrote `expires_at: Value::Null` into every record while the registered contract declared it required. DELETION already had an owner — the W1.5 data-retention disposition plane, which blocks on legal hold and destroys payload bytes — but the backup plane could not READ it: a deleted backup answered `managed_backup_material_unavailable`, the same observable a lost disk produces, and nothing stopped a previously exported bundle from putting the bytes back. | Two REAL daemons over separate data directories, live throughout, no fixture corpus: daemon A captures through the product's own `POST /environments/:id/backups` route and exports; daemon B starts from an EMPTY data directory, imports, restores, is KILLED AND RELAUNCHED, then deletes. **76/76** on `check:backup-restore`, floored in the same commit (family 18 → 19 verifiers, 571 → 647 runtime assertions). Every byte daemon B restores was minted by daemon A's capture path during the run. **The mutation battery over this gate is SEVENTEEN mutations and its verdicts are recorded in the PR, not asserted here** — each must go RED on the assertion that owns its finding, and this leg has already had two mutations come back GREEN-NOT-CAUGHT and force a repair, so a count written before the battery completes is exactly the claim this file must not carry. **TWO independent merge-blocking adversarial from-source reviews ran, and BOTH found the same ship-blocking defect** — see the row below, which is the substance of this leg's evidence. | **Executed and ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** (1) **NO SECOND DELETE ROUTE.** A `DELETE /backups/:id` beside `retention/dispositions/:id/delete` would have been a second admission path for one act, with its own answer to legal holds; the substrate had already ruled it, and reading that rather than escalating it is next-legs X's standing lesson applied. The retention plane's executed deletion now admits the subject's tombstone **before it destroys a byte**. (2) **A BACKUP HAS A LIFECYCLE HEAD**, because the resume checkpoint's genesis-only trap is live in exactly this design: `expected_head: None` sets expected-absent, so an object a restore may RE-ESTABLISH needs a head to compare-and-swap against and a deletion that PRESERVES it. The stream is established lazily from the already-admitted record, so one code path serves captured, imported and legacy records without a migration pass. (3) **THE RECORD TRAVELS VERBATIM.** A backup's whole bytes are its content address and the family is keyed on that digest, so the import re-derives the key from the record it received. The importing daemon never rewrites the record to claim local custody; where the bytes now live, and that they arrived by import, are LOCAL truth on the lifecycle stream. (4) **RETENTION IS SERVER-DERIVED AND NEVER RESTAMPED.** The storage profile declares a duty in seconds; capture turns it into `expires_at` through a parameter that cannot carry caller JSON; every use site evaluates it as a pure read of durable bytes, so there is no sweeper to go stale; and an import carries the source's expiry byte-for-byte, because an import that reset the clock would launder an expiring backup into a fresh one every time it crossed a daemon boundary. (5) **A RE-CAPTURE OVER A DELETION REFUSES.** A backup's coordinate is content-addressed, so re-capturing an unchanged workspace lands on the exact object an owner deleted. The deletion wins. (6) **A READ STATES THE VERDICT** — `GET /backups/:id` carries the lifecycle head and an explicit restorability verdict beside a record whose own `status` reads `complete` forever. (7) **WHAT AN IMPORT CANNOT VERIFY IS TYPED.** It verifies internal consistency completely but cannot verify ISSUER, because that needs a signature over the record by the source daemon's key and this estate has no cross-daemon issuer identity; the imported record is typed `imported` with `issuer_verified: false`. **NAMED RESIDUALS (tracked, not waived):** cross-daemon bundle AUTHENTICITY is unbuilt, so a forged-but-internally-consistent bundle imports as one, and an import therefore leaks coordinate EXISTENCE (403 where a scope exists, 201 where none does); a retention deletion executed by a principal who does not hold the backup's own resource scope REFUSES the whole deletion rather than tombstoning — fail-closed, but a compliance operator who did not capture the backup cannot discharge the duty, and the disposition can only be DECLARED by the scope holder in the first place; the bundle quarantine directory is removed on every refusal path by a `Drop` guard but NOT on SIGKILL, and nothing sweeps it at boot; `hold_refs` stays `[]` — legal hold lives on the disposition, not on the backup record; there is no expiry SWEEPER, so expiry refuses USE while the bytes stay on disk until an owner deletes them; `managed_backup_retention_unreadable` is DORMANT on the import path because the registered contract validates the instant first, and is retained as defence in depth for locally compiled records; an import is bounded at 8 MiB and REFUSES above it, and the router's body limit is spent on UNAUTHENTICATED material because axum runs the `Json` extractor before the handler resolves identity; remote storage profiles still answer 501; a storage profile is admitted GENESIS-ONLY so a profile predating retention cannot be amended and a NEW ref must be declared; and deleting a backup does not delete the LIVE SOURCE the environment still holds. **W3.3 IS NOT CLAIMED CLOSED.** Its substrate acceptance is built and gated; the AUD-W3.3 scope row's SURFACE half — owner surfaces showing stale/restoring/recovered states, provenance verifying state roots — belongs to `SURF-environments`, `SURF-operations` and `SURF-provenance`, each with its own acceptance, and none is opened by this. |

**THREE SHIP-BLOCKING DEFECTS WERE FOUND AND CLOSED IN THIS LEG, NONE OF THEM BY A GREEN GATE.** The
record is kept in specifics because the value is in what the discipline actually caught, and in what
it did not.

**1. The deletion gate was keyed on a name the caller supplies.** Found INDEPENDENTLY BY BOTH
adversarial reviews, each demonstrating it end to end against live daemons. The lifecycle tombstone
keyed on `backup_ref` — which travels INSIDE the bundle — and every tamper-evidence check an import
applies is a *self-consistency* check over caller-supplied bytes. So a caller who had legitimately
downloaded a bundle could rewrite `backup_ref`, honestly recompute `manifest_root` and
`record_artifact_root` (both UNKEYED, both pure functions of the record), re-import, and the
destroyed payload was written back to **the exact content-addressed path the deletion had emptied** —
a fresh restorable record admitted, a restore plan reaching `prepared`, while the audit trail still
said the backup was deleted. The gate protected a *name*; what an owner deletes is *content*. **A
deletion now records what was DESTROYED**, keyed on the state root — the one coordinate a caller
cannot rename, because the import verifies it against the payload bytes themselves. That single
change also closes two paths neither the name-gate nor any assertion covered: re-creating an
environment under a different id to re-capture identical content, and the DownloadIntent egress
lane, which reads the same content-addressed store and had **no deletion gate of any kind** — while
the bytes were gone it answered `download_intent_payload_unavailable`, the very lost-disk observable
this leg exists to replace. **The assertion that should have caught it named the defect and passed
against it**: its forger edited a field and did not recompute the hash. The verifier now computes
both roots itself, the way a forger would.

**2. Carrying a retention duty made capture non-idempotent, and that broke deletion.** `expires_at`
is wall-clock-derived and the family is keyed on a hash of the WHOLE record, so the same capture
retried a second later compiled different bytes, landed at a different key, and admitted a SECOND
record at one `backup_ref`. `backup_by_id` requires a coordinate to resolve exactly once, so read,
verify, export, restore — and the retention deletion itself, which resolves its subject the same
way — then answered `managed_backup_identity_ambiguous` forever, while both captures had returned
201. Before this leg the two records were byte-identical and collapsed onto one key: **a regression
this cut introduced, on the most ordinary operation there is.** Fixed at the coordinate's own
meaning — identical content at one environment IS the same backup, so a second capture replays and
admits nothing.

**3. A restore plan prepared BEFORE a deletion could apply it afterwards.** Found in this run's own
second pass over its own source. Prepare stages a COPY beside the target workspace, out of the
retention plane's reach, and `act_on_restore_plan` decided everything from the plan's admitted
status plus the staging directory's existence — inheriting restorability from prepare. Restorability
is now re-decided at APPLY on both the initial and the resume branch, and a refusal DISCHARGES the
staged copy, because those bytes are the deleted content and leaving them beside the workspace keeps
the resurrection one rename away.

**WHAT THE GATE ITSELF GOT WRONG, recorded because a verifier is a claim like any other.** A
mutation stripping authentication from `POST /snapshots/:id/restore` — a handler that OVERWRITES a
workspace — passed 62/62, because the closed-world census derived its endpoints from a hand-written
FAMILY LIST that never named `/snapshots`; the label "every censused custody mutation" was true and
the census was the lie. A review then found the repaired predicate still omitted the retention plane
— the estate's ONE deletion path, and a handler this cut edits — and the DownloadIntent lane.
Coverage went 8 → 13 → 17 endpoints, and the family list is now itself asserted against the router's
own custody-route inventory. Separately, an "anonymous" probe built a spread copy of the daemon
object and called its BOUND request method, so it went out fully authenticated and actually
performed the import it existed to prove refused — the same defect class as a deleted assertion. And
a review found the retention-unreadable assertion was a two-code DISJUNCTION whose fixture died at
the contract check and never reached the branch it named. All replaced.

**A NEAR-MISS THAT DESERVES ITS OWN LINE.** A `pkill -f` pattern used to stop the mutation harness
matched its own shell (exit 144 — the trap the resume checkpoint documents for `pgrep/pkill -f`) and
killed the harness mid-mutation, so its revert never ran and `handle_snapshot_restore` sat in the
tree with authentication stripped: the exact defect this leg had just closed, staged and ready to
commit. It was caught by diffing the file's line count against its earlier value rather than by
trusting the harness's `finally`, and the tree was then re-verified by replaying every mutation
anchor against it. **A mutation harness rewrites the source it certifies; a kill that skips its
cleanup leaves a defect wearing your own commit.**

**MUTATION COUNTS AND `cargo test` TOTALS IN THE EVIDENCE COLUMN ARE COMMIT CLAIMS EXCEPT WHERE A FLOOR PINS THEM.** This repository pins ASSERTION counts (`verifier-floors.v1.json`, CI-enforced) and tracks no mutation ledger. The difference this run: 3a's and 4's batteries are COMMITTED, REPLAYABLE artifacts wired into CI (`mutate:ontology-admission-census`; `check:env-lease-authority` exercises its exploit as its finding), so those two counts reproduce by running — the falsified-count class, retired for the gates that carry a battery. `cargo test -p ioi-node` 726/726 still has no floor row.

**THE SCARS THIS RUN PAID FOR, bound here and in the checkpoint.** (1) **REACH ≠ AUTHORITY** — a closed world derived over BYTE REACH cannot see a minter, which touches no bytes and transfers authority over everything downstream; an authority census derives over authority, not reach (Leg 4's eighth failure, and it retroactively scopes any no-second-spine census). (2) **OWNERSHIP FROM A RECORD FIELD IS OWNERSHIP FORGED** — `new_env` writes `tenant_posture: single_user` with no `owner_ref`, so every legacy-branch ownership check ships FALSE; the same root appears three times (1a, the env-lease escalation, `bind_env_workspace`), and the fix is one shape: resolve ownership from the substrate scope pin. (3) **A TOTAL POPULATION CLOSES NOTHING UNLESS RESOLUTION OVER IT IS TOTAL** — the token walk was total and the resolver was not; count the resolver's failures in named buckets and stop, because a twice-falsified design earns no third edge. (4) **A GATE MAY NOT BE ITS OWN ORACLE** — an expectation is a committed pin compared against a fresh computation; a digest a gate computes from the source it then builds certifies nothing. (5) **A DENIAL IS METHOD-PRECISE** — a field's polarity is not every mention's; three strikes made it structural. (6) **A FALSE "CLOSED" IS WORSE THAN AN HONEST OPEN DEFECT** — it suppresses the work that would close it; prove a security claim LIVE and correct canon BEFORE the fix, and beware reintroducing the class inside its own correction. (7) **A REPAIR IS WHERE THE NEXT DEFECT HIDES** — every review round this run, including of the repairs, found something true; a repair re-opens the review for its delta.



**LANDED** as four PRs, squash-merged in leg order, each rebuilt onto the freshly-synced master by cherry-pick or explicit tree composition and byte-verified against its reviewed tree, with its floors pin re-derived from a fresh RUNTIME census: [#286](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/286) `22f5dd3f8` (legacy capture custody + retention reach), [#287](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/287) `db1f091ec` (the operational-depth gate), [#288](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/288) `45251d717` (the three ontology backend families), and this block. **Leg 3's CI failed twice before it landed, and both failures were the run's own lesson turned on the run:** `check:admission-evidence` could not see identity resolved through a module-local wrapper — a canonical call hidden behind a name only one file knows — and `cargo fmt --check` failed while the local check reported success, because `cmd | tail -1; echo $?` reports TAIL's status and printed a zero over a real diff. The wrapper is gone and all eleven call sites name the canonical resolver; the pre-push set is now every gate the change can touch, run with real exit codes. The five bundled gates are not CI's set — CI runs about thirty-eight.

The claim that no second writer exists is entailed by THE CALLER'S OWN REQUEST — no refused request wrote, and every field of every write is explained by what the caller asked for: named fields hold the value the REQUEST sent, unnamed fields are byte-unchanged, only declared bookkeeping moves on its own, and a receipt appears only beside the write it attests. Binding it to the RESPONSE was circular and was defeated live at 81/81 — a response is the writer's own product, so comparing a writer to it can only catch a write that escapes the response, never one composed into it — because a source-derived census was defeated by a new spelling in five successive rounds, and because the first behavioural version keyed on the ROUTE was itself defeated live: a second spine running beside the one writer moves the fingerprint once, under an allowed route name, and a route allowlist cannot tell that from the write that belongs there. Search types `object_instance_corpus_absent` apart from `object_instance_query_unmatched`, because "nothing has been materialized here" and "your query matched nothing" are different facts and a surface that cannot tell them apart lets a user conclude the wrong one. **XII's authority ruling binds and is not re-litigated:** ordinary governed mutation, not a CapabilityLease crossing. |
