**MUTATION COUNTS IN THE EVIDENCE COLUMN ARE COMMIT CLAIMS, NOT GATE OUTPUT.** This repository
tracks assertion counts — `verifier-floors.v1.json` pins them and CI enforces them — and tracks no
mutation ledger at all. The three mutation counts above sit in cells beside floor-pinned,
CI-enforced numbers and are typographically indistinguishable from them; they are not the same kind
of fact, and this document has already recorded that "a published verifier pass count is a
commit-message claim rather than a CI-enforced fact" as a systemic finding. The floors gate closed
that hole for assertions. It is still open for mutations, and naming it here is not closing it
either. The same applies to `cargo test -p ioi-node` 720/720, which has no floor row.

**SURF-ontology REMAINS OPEN, and this run does not close it.** The seed gate is green on ground (`--require-ready` ontology, 58 sources, fail-closed graph gate armed) and `check:ontology-journey` is 46/46, but ACC-A names six journeys and three of them now have **a backend and no surface control**. Derived estate-wide rather than from one module, because the first draft of this paragraph read only the Ontology Manager and two of the three journeys do not live there — saved object sets and object-instance search belong to the **Object Explorer**, and reading the Manager's action list proves nothing about it. The derivation that does hold, restated after an audit falsified the first attempt AT ITS OWN LANDING TIP — it claimed only the atlas and the new verifier reference these families, while the sibling hunk two paragraphs above had just put their route names into the Explorer's own gap strings, so the sentence was refuted by the commit it shipped in: every reference to `ontology-proposals`, `saved-object-sets` or `object-instance-search` under `apps/` or `product-ui/` is either the depth atlas, the new verifier, or a NAMED-GAP STRING whose whole content is that no control is bound — not one of them is a control. Read from the bound modules rather than by grep: the Ontology Manager declares `create-ontology`, `update-metadata` and five `upsert-*` actions; and the Object Explorer declares `actions = []`. **Zero** controls bound to any of the three. A journey whose daemon family exists and whose surface offers no way to reach it is not a closed journey — that is the same "silently absent" state XII refused to allow, one layer down. The depth ledger's own named-gap reasons for `rail.proposals` and `hdr.branch-main` said *no proposal/branch plane exists*; that became false the moment the plane landed. Correcting the REASON was not enough and the first attempt shipped it half-executed: `rail.proposals` kept the machine-readable `outcome` `unsupported_reference_session`, whose taxonomy reads *"with no IOI product plane"* — so the classification every consumer keys on still asserted the falsehood two lines above the corrected prose — and the PRODUCT went on rendering *"Proposals is a reference-only lane — no authority contract yet"* to the user. **AND THE CORRECTION REACHED ONE JOURNEY OF THREE, which is this block's own lesson failing on its own subject.** Leg 3 landed three families; the first draft corrected the named gaps for the proposal plane and left the Object Explorer's SIX atlas rows and FOUR rendered product strings asserting *"missing daemon contract: object-instance search plane"* and *"objects exist only as materialized sets"* — the second of which now names precisely the corpus the new route walks. All six rows and all four strings are corrected; they already carried the `disabled_missing_authority` outcome, so the classification did not move and the estate's `governed_receipted_action` total is untouched, but every one of their REASONS had gone false. The `rail.proposals` row is reclassified `disabled_missing_authority` (the estate's `governed_receipted_action` total stays 24, so XII's ruling on that axis is intact; the taxonomy's prose says "missing daemon contract" while this gap is a missing SURFACE binding, and that imprecision is named rather than smoothed over), and the Manager now carries a per-item gap reason where the shared sentence had gone false — the MECHANISM is per-item, and exactly one item uses it so far; the shared sentence survives as the fallback for the gaps where it is still true, which is not the same as a surface-wide conversion. A gap whose stated reason has gone false is worse than an unnamed one — it reads as evidence.

**NAMED RESIDUALS (tracked, not waived).**
*Custody — 1a CLOSED 2026-08-24.* The previously recorded ownerless environment and
first-touch defects are superseded by the implemented ADR 0035 boundary described in Leg 2
above. Pre-existing unpinned environments remain deliberately **unadopted**: ordinary principals
cannot claim or use them, while the deployment administrator can dispose of them through a
receipt-bearing path without gaining read or write authority. This is administration, not a
retroactive ownership migration.
*The depth gate.* `verify-hypervisor-action-runtime.mjs` has no npm script, no CI job and no floor row, so the estate's proof of MODULE-ACTION containment and receipt-fail-closed is ungated — the repaired depth gate pins the one registry row that proof depends on, which is a guard, not a substitute. The flat serve branches beneath read-tier mounts are governed by no module registry: `listings`, a `browse` row, really does have reachable mutations — `/__ioi/marketplace/listings/:id/{delete,candidates,offers,patch}` and a POST on the collection itself, five of them, not the three an earlier count of this named. A POST to a now-forbidden action route answers 200 from the SPA catch-all rather than a typed refusal. The reference-less list catches a false `greenfield` claim but `post-atlas` means only "not greenfield", so it remains an allowlist wearing a derivation; the promotion-claim guard is a DENYLIST and is labelled as one.
*The no-second-spine property is NOT entailed here, and this is a RULING, not an omission.* **"No second admission path writes ontology truth" is a whole-program property, and a black-box journey verifier cannot entail it.** Five review rounds moved this gate's reference — the route name, then the file name, then the response bytes, then the caller's request — and each round a review defeated the PROOF while the code under test stayed correct and green. That pattern is the diagnosis: the observer sits inside the system it is trying to bound. The owner terminated the iteration at round ten and ruled the claim RE-SCOPED. Every assertion that was phrased as the whole-program property now claims the journey-scoped fact it checks — that the ontology truth admitted through the watched path equals what the CALLER REQUESTED, on the journeys walked — and the headline one says so in its own text. What the final round's review named as still evadable, recorded because it is the specification of the real proof: the request is SILENT ABOUT DEFAULTS, so a create may mint any value for a field the caller did not name; only two of the plane's four families are fingerprinted (`odk-ontology-proposals` and `odk-saved-object-sets` are not, so a fabricated proposal record reading `status: "applied"` by a principal who never acted is the identical attack one family over); a receipt OVERWRITE keeps the count at one; a write-then-revert inside one request is invisible; a deferred write is attributed to the next request; a caller-named field the plane silently DROPS is skipped; and the static half remains regexes over one file, which six successive rounds each defeated with a new spelling. **Commissioned in next-legs XIV Leg 3a**: derive the admission census from the router/module SOURCE and assert that exactly ONE module admits ontology truth — the move that fixed the custody census — with the census's own filter asserted. The journey gate's re-scoped label then composes with that proof instead of impersonating it.
*The falsified-reason class.* Filed in the same spirit and for the same reason: six rounds of hand-correction did not converge, and two survivors were invisible to grep — one named URLs that were never routes, one lived inside a verifier's expected string. **Commissioned in next-legs XIV Leg 3b**: reasons and user-reaching strings asserted at their EMISSION POINT, derived from source, closed-world over emission sites.
*The ontology families.* Search's MATCHING over a product-produced corpus, the provenance on its rows, its truncation arithmetic and its scope FILTER are proven NOWHERE. Producing a corpus requires the wallet-gated materialization crossing and a real one-validator wallet.network principal-authority fixture, started before the daemon because the authority ref is read at boot. An opt-in lane claiming to prove it was written, **could never pass, and was deleted rather than defended.** `object_instance_query_unmatched` is constructed by nothing reachable without that corpus, so the two-empties distinction is proven in ONE direction. Any authenticated principal can WITHDRAW another principal's proposal — shared apply is deliberate and asserted, shared withdraw is not, and the proposal family carries no scope term. The per-owner cutover with typed 410s is W6.1 and OQ-2 blocks W6.1.

**WHAT THE MERGE-BLOCKING REVIEWS CAUGHT, because the value of this run is in what a green self-review shipped.** FORTY-THREE ship-blocking defects across four legs and TEN review rounds, every one demonstrated end to end against live daemons, and **not one caught by a green gate**. Two were gates themselves: one asserted two planes ABSENT while probing a URL that was never a route, so it could never have failed; the other had not executed at all since 2026-08-09, because it writes an ontology without a session and ODK writes became identity-first that day. THIRTY-FIVE of them were introduced by REPAIRS of earlier ones. Both numbers are COMMIT CLAIMS on exactly the footing as the mutation counts disclaimed above — this repository tracks no defect ledger either, and hedging one while stating the other in bold was the same asymmetry twice in one document — the single most useful thing this run learned, and the reason every repair delta was re-reviewed as unreviewed new code until a round came back clean. The last six arrived after the ledger block was first written, every one of them in a repair: the partition audit found two verifiers left BLOCKED on an ownership precondition this run had REMOVED; one review round found the ontology census had been made strictly BLINDER by its own repair, plus a second writer exported by an allowlisted module; the NEXT round defeated the behavioural replacement LIVE AT 78/78, because keying it on the ROUTE cannot tell a second spine running beside the one writer from the write that belongs there, and the write it used rode a 409-REFUSED apply; and the same round found this block's own falsified-reason correction had missed the atlas's `missing_authority_contracts` field entirely and shipped a derivation sentence its own sibling hunk falsified.

**AND THEN, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS RUN, THE GATES CAUGHT THE REPAIR'S OWN DEFECTS.** Fixing the census surfaced two more, and neither needed a reviewer: a mutation showed the new runtime observation watched the legacy record directory while `persist_promoted` admits into the SUBSTRATE LOG, so the precondition is now asserted rather than assumed; and a census run came back 48/78 because the mutation battery's restore set was a hand-written list beside a derived one — `[WORKBENCH, ODK]` while a mutation targeted `substrate_store.rs`, so that file was never restored and the digest check REPORTED CLEAN OVER A POISONED TREE. Both are the same shape as everything else here, and both were caught by machinery rather than by reading. That is the only direction this gets better.

**A GATE IS ONLY AS STRONG AS THE COORDINATE IT KEYS ON — again, in a third costume.** The owner pin was bound on the RAW `environment_id` while the record file and the workspace directory key on `safe_id(id)`, which is MANY-TO-ONE. `env.18cb` and `env_18cb` were two pin coordinates over ONE record and ONE workspace, and every daemon-minted id contains `_`, so a colliding alias always existed. A review created the alias, took a fresh pin at it, clobbered the victim's record, started onto the victim's own workspace directory, captured the victim's bytes and restored over them: an ordinary member with read AND write on any principal's workspace. Every caller-supplied id now becomes a scope coordinate through one function. The same mixed convention then reappeared HALF-FIXED: normalized where the scope is authorized, raw everywhere downstream, so a retention duty declared at an alias was ADMITTED and could never be executed, refusing forever with "retry to converge". An erasure duty that can be declared and never discharged is worse than one that refuses at declaration.

**AN OWNERSHIP DECISION MAY NOT DEPEND ON A FIELD AN UNAUTHENTICATED ROUTE CONTROLS.** Two successive repairs of mine failed in opposite directions, and that is what named the real mistake. Gating adoption on `status.workspace_root` was defeated by one anonymous `POST /environments/:id/delete`, which nulls the field — two requests, one unauthenticated, and the seizure was permanent because the pin is genesis-only with no unbind. Refusing every unpinned record stranded the other way: one anonymous GET established a coordinate no principal could ever own, capture, back up or place under a retention duty. Binding at record ESTABLISHMENT made custody FIRST-TOUCH over a COORDINATE — a member could touch `session_workspace`, let the administrator start it and write secrets, then capture them — and a REFUSED action staked the claim before returning 400, an invisible squat. **A record is a name; a workspace is bytes.** Ownership binds where the bytes come into existence, and everything else follows.

**A REPAIR IS WHERE THE NEXT DEFECT HIDES, and the tier gate proves it.** Repairing the depth gate closed a real hole and silently killed the estate's only proof of MODULE-ACTION fault containment and receipt-fail-closed (`verify-hypervisor-app-runtime-safety.mjs` still proves route-local RENDERER containment, so the earlier phrasing here overstated it): the action-runtime test surface is declared `browse` and carries two receipted POST actions, the new gate skipped it, and both POSTs fell through to the SPA catch-all as 200 — CI green, because that verifier has no npm script, no CI job and no floor row.

**A LANE NOBODY HAS DEMONSTRATED PASSING IS NOT EVIDENCE.** This run wrote a `--live-authority` lane for the ontology search corpus and cited it in a commit message as proof of matching. It could never pass — it never started the authority fixture and re-minted a grant from a 501 that carries no approval — and a review ran it and found four of its five assertions RED and the fifth vacuous. It is deleted, and the residual is stated unhedged rather than covered by a lane that does not run.

**AGREEMENT IS NOT A CONTRACT, AND A SHARED CHECK CANNOT PROVE ITSELF.** This run closed a
propose/patch divergence by extracting one shared validator, then added a parity journey to gate it —
and a review flipped a single guard key so an empty `domain` became admissible on BOTH planes, leaving
the journey green printing "ACCEPTED / ACCEPTED". A shared validator preserves parity under ANY
weakening; parity can only ever see a SECOND, non-shared check. Worse, the two absolute assertions that
would have caught it were deleted in the same commit, undeclared, under a RISING assertion count —
which is exactly the case `check:verifier-floors` documents it cannot judge and defers to a human. A
gate over a shared component must assert the ABSOLUTE answer, not the agreement.

**AND A VERIFIER HAD NOT RUN SINCE 2026-08-09, WHICH IS WORSE THAN THE DEFECT THIS BLOCK FIRST CLAIMED IT HAD.** An earlier draft of this paragraph said `verify-hypervisor-app-parity-ontology-manager.mjs` had been PINNING the old wording and "turned RED" when the Proposals reason was corrected — and a merge-blocking audit ran the file and found it cannot reach that assertion at all. It POSTs its ontology fixture with NO SESSION; ODK writes became identity-first in next-legs II (#236, 2026-08-09), so the daemon has answered 401 ever since and the next line dereferences `undefined` and throws. Every assertion below that point — including the named-gap rail checks this run "repaired" — has been DEAD CODE for months, in a file with no npm script, no CI job and no floor row, so no green ever covered it and no red ever reported it. The wording-pin defect was real but LATENT; the claim that it fired was false, and this block's own bar two rows above — *a check that cannot run gates nothing and may not pretend to* — was being violated by the paragraph shipping beside it. The file is now runnable (it takes the operator session this manually-launched estate is driven with, and BLOCKS when it is absent rather than failing downstream on an unrelated symptom), its lane assertions are bound PER LANE rather than per page, and its Proposals assertion no longer admits the negation of its own claim — a `[^"]*` gap let *"no ontology proposal plane exists"* satisfy a check named for the opposite. Run against a live estate it is **24/24**, and the six failure modes were each shown to go red. IT REMAINS UNGATED: nothing runs it automatically, and that is a residual, not a fix.

**AND A CORRECTION THAT CANNOT RENDER IS NOT A CORRECTION — TWICE, IN OPPOSITE WAYS.** The Manager's health note was first fixed in the surface's FALLBACK string, which is unreachable because the daemon emits `object_data_note` unconditionally. It was then fixed where that note is COMPUTED — and an audit showed the falsehood still rendering on three user-facing pages, because the note is computed at write time and PERSISTED INTO THE RECORD, and reads return the stored record verbatim. Every ontology written before a capability lands keeps the sentence that was true when it was written, for ever, with no backfill anywhere in the stack. Prose about what the estate can do is now PROJECTED ON READ from one pinned constant, on both the get and the list path, and the gate plants a stale record and reads it back to prove it. Four sibling strings on the serve lane were corrected the same way. The atlas's own outcome TAXONOMY was falsified in both directions and both are fixed: `unsupported_reference_session` still defined itself as covering "proposal" machinery "with no IOI product plane" — the definition the `rail.proposals` reclassification was argued against — and its sibling `disabled_missing_authority` still read "the reason names the exact missing daemon CONTRACT" while eight rows in that bucket now name a contract that EXISTS and a surface binding that does not.

**AND THE SHARPEST COPY WAS A GREEN GATE WHOSE PROBES WERE NEVER REAL.** `check:ontology-journey` asserted "proposal family absent" and "saved-set authoring absent" by probing `/domain-ontologies/:id/proposals` — never a route in ANY build — and `POST /materialized-object-sets`, which is registered GET-only. Both answered 404/405 for reasons having nothing to do with their subject, so NEITHER COULD EVER HAVE GONE RED. This run landed both families and the gate stayed green; it is CI-wired and floor-pinned, so two false assertion NAMES were hashed into the floors digest as certified estate truth, and this very block cited it as corroboration — "46/46 unchanged" read as "still valid". It is corrected to assert PRESENCE at the registered routes, with a mutation that withdraws one of them to prove the gate can now fail. **And it is the argument for the XIV gate that this block could not make for itself:** a survivor that names URLs which were never routes is INVISIBLE to grepping the family names — the sweep this run performed five times could not have found it, and the sixth would not have either.

**A CORRECTION THAT OVERCLAIMS IS THE SAME DEFECT FACING THE OTHER WAY.** Correcting the Explorer's saved-object-set gap, this block asserted 'Your object sets' and 'Created by me' were now backed. They are lanes over the MATERIALIZED set catalog — a different store, with no per-user ownership recorded anywhere — so the correction traded a false absence for a false presence, and the atlas contradicted itself two hundred lines apart. Likewise the blanket "explorations have no IOI plane" went false when a saved set (a named, bounded selection) landed, while exploration TABS genuinely have no plane; both are now qualified to name which is which.

**A FALSIFIED NAMED REASON IS A CLASS, IT RECURRED FOUR TIMES IN THIS RUN, AND PROSE IS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT — FILED FOR XIV.** Round after round, a merge-blocking review found another copy: the depth ledger's prose, then its machine-readable `outcome`, then the string the product renders, then the SIX Object Explorer rows and FOUR rendered strings this stack's own backends falsified, then — in the very commit that corrected those — three `missing_authority_contracts` entries, a `primary_workflow` line, a `landing_vs_workflow_note`, a `security_credential_implications` paragraph keyed on the plane NOT existing, and two `aria-label`s left carrying the replaced word on the same source lines whose `title` had just been fixed. The accessible name is the only text an assistive-technology user receives, so those two were rendering the falsehood to precisely the readers least able to route around it. Every survivor was reachable by one `git grep` — the same grep this block asserted in prose that it had run. **The instrument is wrong.** A claim that no surface control is bound to a landed plane belongs in a GATE that derives it, failing closed when a plane lands and a named gap still says it did not; and the depth verifier reads `reason` for presence and vagueness while never reading `missing_authority_contracts` at all, which is why nothing green ever objected. That gate is NOT built here — this run's landing carries no new scope — and it is filed as the first thing XIV should build, because five rounds of correcting copies by hand is the evidence that hand-correction does not converge.

**AND FIXING A FALSEHOOD IN ONE PLACE IS NOT FIXING IT.** A named gap's reason lived in three places —
the depth ledger's prose, its machine-readable `outcome`, and the string the product renders to a user.
Correcting the prose left the classification every consumer keys on still asserting "no IOI product
plane" and left the Manager still telling users "no authority contract yet", months after the contract
landed. Correct every copy, or the ones left behind go on reading as evidence.

**AND THE LEG'S OWN IMPLEMENTATION WAS IN THE WRONG COMMIT.** A review found the entire ontology module — 1028 lines — riding inside the custody leg's commit, inert because its `mod` declaration was absent, together with a live refactor of the ODK ontology writer that the custody review never looked at. One PR per leg is not a filing convention: it is what makes a per-leg merge-blocking review mean anything. The branches were rebuilt so each leg's work is in its own commit, and each leg's review re-run against it.

**FOUR ASSERTIONS OF THIS RUN'S OWN WERE DECORATIVE, and mutations or reviews said so.** A census family list that was hand-written and wrong in seven of twelve entries. A "no second spine" check that was a DENYLIST and sailed past the two most obvious second spines. A "the scope filter is applied" claim that is unobservable with no corpus in either scope — the whole predicate can be replaced by `true` and the assertion stays green. And a complement list that claimed it "cannot absorb a surface that does have reference evidence" while implementing nothing of the kind, with eight of eleven entries contradicted by the estate's own provenance ledger. Each is now either derived positively or labelled to claim only what it checks.

### AFT Common-Boundary program opened (2026-08-16, AFT-CB P0 wave — every surveyed consensus quirk gets a fix PR or a registered obligation, and the claim ladder becomes conditional and checker-gated)

**The AFT consensus corpus now executes under a ratified research program (AFT-CB), and this row is its tracked registration.** The program's authority chain, leg DAG, per-leg gate evidence, and append-only status ledger deliberately live in the ignored internal research registry (per that ledger's own LEG 0 ruling: ignored program state carries no PR), and the program is registered as the single `parallel_research` unit `VERT-aft-common-boundary-program` in the ignored implementation registry — none of which is load-bearing here. What is load-bearing and tracked: the five P0 claim-hygiene PRs below, the checkers they add under `.github/scripts/`, and this section. The lane is non-MVP and non-gating; nothing in it delays release, and no unconditional quantitative security claim prints anywhere in the corpus while the program's own checkers are the gate.

**Measurement basis:** 2026-08-16 against master `d0fa28d0b`, with all five P0 PRs OPEN and fully green (per-PR CI rollup checked at heads `4b80b7323`, `28edf3acb`, `266ba0769`, `761dfe64c`, `779e94088`). This row asserts what each PR's tree contains and what its CI run proved — it does not assert that any of them merged; their merge is a pending owner action, and code anchors below cite the basis commit, not a post-merge tree.

| Quirk (at basis `d0fa28d0b`) | What it was | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Q1** — `canonical_order.rs` built the bulletin from the candidate block's own transactions | the bulletin proved internal consistency, never completeness — a censor omitted a transaction and then committed a bulletin that never contained it | **KILLED — R1 landed**: the committed-surface builder CONSUMES a previously closed boundary (bulletin-from-block deleted); certificates carry boundary + typed omission justifications, verification is self-contained (included = boundary ∖ justified must reproduce the header root), and the baseline commit records the pre-cut code verifying a censoring block GREEN. Reference-single-member closer (n=1, honestly labeled) until R5 |
| **Q2** — `internal-docs/architecture/protocols/aft/formal/canonical_ordering/CanonicalOrderingProof.tla:29` defines `CertifiedSoundness` as admitted-equals-canonical | uniqueness followed from the definition, not from a protocol | **registered obligation — leg P2.1** (uniqueness derived from an inductive invariant over an unconstrained Byzantine adversary; admitted-equals-canonical definitions are refused by the leg spec) |
| **Q3** — `.../formal/nested_guardian/NestedGuardianRecoveryRecurringInductionCore.tla:86` takes `<>RecoveryClosedPrefix(1)` as a premise | eventual prefix closure was a premise contract wearing a theorem's clothes | **registered obligation — leg P2.2** (the successor derives the premise; the kernels are requalified as premise-contract kernels, never claimed as proofs) |
| **Q4** — `crates/types/src/app/consensus/collapse/proofs/continuity.rs:30` routes the "succinct" backend to hash-derived mock bytes | mock bytes presented as a succinct proof | **fenced by PR [#298](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/298)** (release verifiers reject `SuccinctSp1V1` in both build and verify directions, typed error naming the completion leg); **kill registered at P2.5/R4c** (real recursive backend, pinned vkey) |
| **Q5** — `crates/services/src/guardian_registry/materialize.rs` deleted close keys and rewrote `sealing` in place | post-close history was rewritable | **KILLED — R3 landed**: `materialize_observer_abort` deletes nothing and rewrites nothing; post-close evidence lands in the append-only `aft_resolution_log` (PostCloseAbortEvidence + DescendantFence records); the sealed collapse is byte-identical after an abort attempt (mutation-drilled) |
| **Q6** — `continuity.rs:41` selects the continuity proof system from a process environment variable | consensus-critical behavior varied per process, outside protocol state | **fix PR [#298](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/298)** (env selector deleted; the proof system is an explicit protocol parameter; a source-scan test holds the env-read count at zero) |
| **Q7** — `materialize.rs` exempted abort-carrying collapses from the conflict check | abort silently superseded a sealed close | **KILLED — R3 landed**: abort supersedes only PRE-SEAL; an abort against a sealed close is a typed rejection whose attempt is itself logged as post-close evidence (mutation-drilled) |
| **Q8** — `crates/consensus/src/aft/experimental/vrf.rs` computes `proof = output` | a VRF with no soundness, stranded but promotable by accident | **fix PR [#301](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/301)** (deleted outright — it was held as an engine field and invoked nowhere; a pin test blocks silent reappearance and names the real successor path, VDF-beacon sortition at R11/R5) |
| **Q9** — `.github/scripts/run_aft_formal_checks.sh` was invoked by no workflow | worse than surveyed: the invoking workflow had been deleted, so the formal corpus had **zero** CI presence, not a partial floor | **fix PR [#297](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/297)** (module census: every `.tla` is executed or manifest-marked manual-discharge, orphans fail; new `aft_formal_floor` CI job; that PR's own run is the first green TLAPS+TLC discharge in CI in repo history) |
| **Q10** — the internal yellow paper asserted a discharged "deterministic 99% Byzantine Tolerance" while the whitepaper claimed nothing quantitative | claim divergence between two owned surfaces, and a rounded figure that is neither exact nor maximal | **fix PRs [#300](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/300)** (all-but-one (n−1 of n) language, model-delta conditionality, claim-discipline checker: 49 baseline violations → 0) **and [#299](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/299)** (whitepaper §5.3 A1–A9 assumption ledger, verbatim interim conditional claim, exact q-of-n bounds, no rounded tolerance figures — its own checker gates the section) |

**What the wave adds as machine gates rather than prose:** `check_aft_claim_discipline.sh` (no unconditional or rounded tolerance claim survives in the AFT corpus; extended at P4.4 so no flagship rung prints as a bare assertion), `check_aft_whitepaper_claims.sh` (the A1–A10 ledger and interim claim cannot silently erode), `check_aft_theorem_assumes.sh` (the theorem corpus's Assumes-line discipline + pairing-table completeness), `check_aft_no_mock_proof.sh` (no mock succinct constructor survives — R4c), and the `aft_formal_floor` job (an unlisted formal module is a red build; the R13 trace-conformance replay runs here). Every leg ran a mutation drill against its own gate before its PR opened (defect reintroduced → named RED → reverted GREEN), recorded per leg in the program ledger and PR bodies. The interim claim is deliberately conditional on the common-boundary model delta, and the program's standing refusals bind every leg: no mock presented as proof, no strong-ring alteration from non-response evidence, no selection probability composed into a deterministic tolerance figure.

### Ruling filed 2026-08-12 — the conformance tree retires (owner re-ruling)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Whether `docs/conformance` stays as a separate conformance-document tree.** The owner ruled it noise as a category — a document family no comparable project carries — and ordered full deletion, superseding the same-day maturity-surface leg (the generated claims table) that had built on it. | The tree at deletion: 35 typed claims (4 active invariants, 7 target_runnable, 21 targets, 2 nonclaims, 1 deprecated stub), the hypervisor-core target files, the agentic-runtime CIRC/CEC stubs, and three matrix JSONs. No code consumer existed outside the docs gate's ROOTS entry (verified: no script, verifier, or crate reads a `docs/conformance` path). Inbound: ~30 markdown links across 16 canonical files, all unlinked in this cut; the docs gate ROOTS narrowed to `docs/architecture` + `docs/decisions`; PR #266 (the claims-table generator + `check:conformance-claims`) was CLOSED unmerged — its purpose deleted with its source. | **Ruled (owner, owner-reversible) and executed.** The DOCTRINE the tree carried is not erased where it was load-bearing: the portable-authority v3 acceptance bar and negative corpus fold into `objects/authority-and-access.md` (the owner that always defined the semantics); per-claim maturity typing continues where each claim's owner lives — `Implementation status:` headers, typed absences, and the delta ledger — which the README now points at instead. The maturity-surface leg of the outside-reader adoption ruling is SUPERSEDED by this re-ruling; the adoption ruling's other legs stand. History stays legible: this row records what the tree was, why it went, and where its living content moved. |

### Rulings filed 2026-08-12 — TOMBSTONE (refiling of the 2026-08-11 forward-scope rulings)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|

Both halves of that check were wrong: the governing filing ([`#255`](https://github.com/ioi-foundation/ioi/pull/255), `8d7555e74`, 2026-08-11) is deliberately vendor-free, and it had already landed on `origin/master` at 01:43 that morning. A subject-text grep (`cost per successful workload unit`) hits three tracked files — but it would also have returned zero against the stale tree. | **Superseded by the 2026-08-11 section below, which governs.** The rows are collapsed here rather than deleted so the duplication and its cause stay legible. What that run added and #255 never filed — [`doctrine.md`](../components/model-router/doctrine.md) §Provider Transport Boundary and §Route Economic Comparison, and the [`source-of-truth-map.md`](./source-of-truth-map.md) model-router row — is REAL canon and STAYS: a ruling about the model router belongs in the owner's doctrine, not only in this ledger. The vendor name leaves tracked canon entirely and re-enters only through `LICENSE-MANIFEST.json` if bytes are ever transcribed ([ADR 0033](../../decisions/0033-licensing-split-surface-and-license-manifest.md)). **Standing rule minted here: test whether canon already says something by its SUBJECT or disposition text, never a vendor or proper name — and never against an unfetched local ref.** |

### Forward-scope rulings filed 2026-08-11 (provider-adapter absorption and cost-shape comparison; owner-ruled)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Provider-adapter absorption boundary (W3.2).** Whether IOI absorbs an external provider-gateway project wholesale, or only its protocol-adaptation layer. | Owner-originated proposal plus four owner-supplied refinements, ruled 2026-08-11. Tracked artifacts the ruling composes with: [`0033-licensing-split-surface-and-license-manifest.md`](../../decisions/0033-licensing-split-surface-and-license-manifest.md) and `LICENSE-MANIFEST.json` (repo root); the CapabilityLease authority-crossing gateway (`apps/hypervisor/scripts/verify-hypervisor-capability-lease-plan.mjs`); the no-second-spine structural law as enforced through the W3.1 launch chain (`apps/hypervisor/scripts/verify-hypervisor-launch-chain.mjs`, Journey verification V above). | **Ruled (owner, owner-reversible).** IOI absorbs ONLY the provider-protocol adaptation layer — request/response normalization, streaming and cancellation, tool-call and structured-output translation, provider headers/endpoints/auth shapes, error normalization and retryability *classification*, capability metadata, and conformance test vectors — as knowledge and test vectors transcribed behind an IOI `ProviderTransport` contract. The control plane is never imported and stays kernel-owned: route selection and admission, credential custody, privacy/IFC enforcement, retry and fallback *decisions*, budgets/billing/reconciliation, invocation receipts and attempt lineage, and logging/caching/policy/authorization. An imported gateway's control plane would mint a second spine beside each of those kernel owners. Timing is early W3.2, gated on W3.1 plus ONE native provider proven end-to-end first, so the native path defines the transport contract rather than ported adapters defining it by default. Anything transcribed closely enough to count as copied takes a `LICENSE-MANIFEST.json` entry per ADR 0033, with upstream `enterprise/` code excluded. Imported capability and price maps enter as candidate evidence, never authority. Differential testing against an upstream implementation is a gated harness lane, never a default CI gate. |
| **Economic comparison across cost shapes (W4-F).** Whether, and on what evidence, IOI may tell an operator that per-token routes or rented GPU capacity is cheaper for their workload. | Owner-ratified 2026-08-11. **Pre-existing canon anchor:** the model-invocation receipt in [`events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md`](../components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md) already carries `price_schedule_ref` and already states that this receipt is a development projection, not invoice-grade Work Credit truth — so the extensible price schedule and the estimates-are-never-spend constraint extend existing canon rather than introduce it. Composes existing tracked planes, each with a live verifier under `apps/hypervisor/scripts/`: expiring candidate evidence (`verify-hypervisor-cloud-candidate-plane.mjs`), probed availability (`verify-hypervisor-model-route-registry.mjs`), reconciled spend (`verify-hypervisor-provider-spend-reconciliation.mjs`), receipted placement decisions (`verify-hypervisor-placement-venue-picker.mjs`), governed change gates (`verify-hypervisor-improvement-governance-gates.mjs`, `verify-hypervisor-policy-canary-rollback.mjs`), and the attempt/outcome planes that carry a success signal (`verify-hypervisor-attempt-finding-plane.mjs`, `verify-hypervisor-work-result-plane.mjs`, `verify-hypervisor-outcome-learning-proposals.mjs`). | **Ruled (owner, ratified).** W4-F may expose an advisory economic comparison across eligible model routes, projecting workload cost from provider price schedules or reconciled capacity spend and measured workload-specific throughput. Estimates remain candidate evidence, preserve provenance, freshness, and confidence, and are audited against invocation and spend receipts. Effective cost per token is explanatory; cost per successful workload unit is the decision metric *where outcome evidence exists*, and a typed gap elsewhere — never a synthesized success rate. Economic comparison neither grants route rights nor independently authorizes placement or migration. Supporting constraints: quality, privacy, residency, latency, and availability are admission filters applied before price ranks anything; pricing is an extensible schedule (input/output/cache tokens, GPU-seconds, minimum rentals, commitments, storage, egress, cold starts, headroom), not a token-versus-GPU enum; GPU spend truth lives on the shared capacity pool with route economics as a derived join, because direct route attribution double-counts shared pools and would mint a second spend spine; amortized candidates render as a utilization band with the project's receipt-observed usage placed on it, never a point estimate; missing evidence renders as a typed gap. Effective cost per token is not cross-route comparable as a winner metric because tokenizers differ. **W3.2 obligation, filed by name:** `ProviderTransport` must define and begin collecting the evidence fields this surface consumes — price schedules, full token mix including cache and reasoning tokens, attempts, latency, and outcomes — which is cheap while the transport is being built and expensive to retrofit. |

### Systemic finding filed 2026-08-11 (verifier evidence integrity)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A `CapabilityLease` did not say whose it was**, so a lease could not confer an acting principal and no agent could draw one down. | Field census over ALL 2248 issued lease records (`GET /v1/hypervisor/capability-leases`, 2026-09-04): every record carries the same 14 keys — `allowed_tools`, `authority_provider_ref`, `backing_provider`, `credential_source`, `expires_at`, `grant_ref`, `issued_at`, `lease_id`, `policy_hash`, `receipt_required`, `request_hash`, `resource_refs`, `revocation_ref`, `schema_version` — and NONE carries `principal_ref`, `owner_ref` or `subject`. The chain reaches no principal elsewhere: 2238 leases cite `wallet.network://grant/approval/<sha256>` and the rest `standing-grant://sha256:…`, with zero citing a local `agr_*` record; and the 468 local authority-grants carry `subject` as the literal string `"operator"` 371 times against a `user://` principal exactly twice, while `resolve_workload_broker_identity` requires a `user://` principal plus a tenant-verified `org://`/`project://` owner. | **CLOSED 2026-09-04 by canon change, forward-only.** `ioi.hypervisor.capability-lease.v1` gains `principal_ref` and `owner_ref`, written at issue from the SERVER-RESOLVED caller (`provider_write_caller` → `RequestIdentity`) and never from a request field. Absent rather than defaulted when the issuing route has no resolved caller, so absence stays distinguishable from fabrication. Existing leases are NOT back-filled and refuse `lease_predates_principal_binding` by name — a principal inferred after the fact is one nobody granted. The alternative considered and rejected was resolving the principal from the requesting session, which would have put an acting principal who did not act onto a receipt (`INV-37`) — evidence that reads as true, which is worse than a refusal. Doctrine clause: [`../components/wallet-network/doctrine.md`](../components/wallet-network/doctrine.md) § "A CapabilityLease says whose it is". |
| **No verifier enforces an assertion-count floor**, so a published verifier pass count is a commit-message claim rather than a CI-enforced fact. | Byte-verified census at `36ca2b9a4`: 119 verifier scripts under `apps/hypervisor/scripts/` carrying 3,328 static `ok(` call sites; every one terminates on `process.exit(fails.length ? 1 : 0)` (spot-checked in `verify-hypervisor-launch-chain.mjs`, `-work-cockpit.mjs`, `-home-cockpit.mjs`, `-ontology-journey.mjs`, `-operations-cockpit.mjs`); `verify-hypervisor-operations-cockpit.mjs` carries zero floor-shaped tokens, so the mechanism is absent rather than inconsistent; [`ci.yml`](../../../.github/workflows/ci.yml) invokes all eleven journey verifiers bare, consuming only the exit code. | **Open finding (program-level, not owned by any W3.1 unit).** Deleting assertions is silently green: a cut that removes a gate's load-bearing checks and a cut that fixes the code both exit 0. Same class as the W3.1 gate-hardening rounds recorded in Journey verification V, one level up — there a gate could not fail on its own finding, here a gate can stop containing its finding, and both publish a number that reads as proof. Recommended fix: a floor on the RUNTIME assertion total, never static call sites (they diverge — `check:launch-chain` had 28 static sites while reporting 43 runtime rows), pinned in a tracked verifier-to-minimum map and validated by a `check:verifier-floors` gate matching the `check:shipped-products` count-plus-digest idiom, so counts shrink only through a deliberate reviewed edit. Filed for a future cut. |

> **Superseded 2026-08-20 — read this block as the dated finding it is, not as
> current CI truth.** The recommended fix landed as `check:verifier-floors`
> (#258); see Journey verification VI above. Three of the verifier filenames the
> census cell spot-checks no longer exist: `verify-hypervisor-work-cockpit.mjs`
> was re-aimed and renamed `verify-hypervisor-session-authority.mjs`, and
> `verify-hypervisor-home-cockpit.mjs` and
> `verify-hypervisor-operations-cockpit.mjs` were retired with their surfaces by
> the E7-1 cockpit retirement
> (`apps/hypervisor/reference-seed-adjudications.v1.json#e7-cockpit-retirement`).
> The 119-script/3,328-call-site census remains valid at its pinned commit
> `36ca2b9a4`.

### Journey verification XVI (2026-09-07, bounded-alpha closure program — the blocked qualification's two root causes found and fixed, the authority node replaced by a deployment bring-up with generated keys, the release packaged and updatable/rollbackable through admitted change plans, the session panes bound to daemon truth, and the journey passed 43/43 on the packaged release)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Root cause 1 — the wallet.network fixture's blocks were topology, not load alone:** the fixture's default ordering profile is the four-validator AFT (ML-DSA classic-BFT) cluster of debug binaries, so every setup transaction needed a BFT commit across four starved validators. The alpha names ONE deployment-local node; the journey now starts the fixture in the Solo single-validator profile and it converged (readiness 416 s including a one-time node build). | `check:alpha-journey` fixture mode 25/29 (the reds were root cause 2) | fixed |
| **Root cause 2 — the operator's approved grant could never be consumed:** the local approver minted with no `audience` (wallet.network consumes only when the audience is the consuming signer), and even with the audience the daemon's preflight found no state for the grant because the approval decision was never RECORDED on the node. The execute challenge now carries `approval.audience` (the daemon's capability account) and `approval.target_scope`; the approval act mints one one-use grant and records it on the node (`wallet-network-local-authority record-approval`, or the fixture's command directory) before the execute resumes; an unconfigured recorder refuses the approval. | direct reproduction (record committed, execute proceeded); deployment-mode journey step 6 `done` with an execute receipt binding the lease | fixed (`ced3f1b9c`, `a1bf94cbc`) |

No second authority plane. | standalone drill (v1 → rotate v2 → revoke v3 → resume → rotate v4); journey steps 2b, 2c-rotation (second run executes under the new key), 2c-revocation (third run fails closed) | landed (`15b19290f`; M03.9 record verified) |

### Journey verification XXIV (2026-09-09, standing-lease program 2, leg 3 — R-14 ruled and implemented: the bounded alpha can mint a standing envelope with the key its operator already holds)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The ruling.** `deployment_local_operator` is a recognized custody tier for recording a standing grant, beside `passkey`, under the same rules — one ceremony, receipted before the effect, creating no effect authority, widening nothing (ADR 0052 § 7, owner-reversible). Before it, the alpha's operator held the approver key on the deployment host and the wallet's evidence rule was passkey-only, so the alpha could mint no standing envelope at all and its attach flow refused typed. | [`0052`](../../decisions/0052-hypervisor-bounded-alpha-profile-and-base-platform-acceptance.md) § 7; [`bounded-alpha-profile.md`](../components/hypervisor/bounded-alpha-profile.md) § Nonclaims | ruled (owner-reversible) |
| **Carried by a NEW registered contract, not a mutated one.** `auth-factor-receipt/v2` admits the tier and binds the verification mode to it, so neither tier borrows the other's evidence; v1 stays valid and names v2 as its successor (`compatibility: widening`). The v2 receipt attests CUSTODY — the host, the hash of the key's path, that it is readable by its owner alone, and when the operator acknowledged the ceremony — and never a person. The wallet selects the contract from the receipt's own declared `schema_version` across a CLOSED set, and requires the ceremony's `required_auth_factor_posture_refs` to name the same tier the receipt is. | `auth-factor-receipt.v2.schema.json` + fixtures; `standing_authority.rs` `RECOGNIZED_FACTOR_POSTURE_PREFIXES`; `npm run check:architecture-contracts` | landed |
| **Recording is a control-plane act.** The deployment's authority node gained `record-standing-approval`, `revoke-standing-approval` and `chain-timestamp`; the standing record is signed by the deployment's CONTROL ROOT (the wallet gates the method to `WalletAuthRole::ControlPlane`, exactly as it gates authority registration), while the capability account remains the grant's audience and the signer of every later draw. | `wallet_network_local_authority.rs`; `wallet-authority.mjs` `bindStandingLease` | landed |
| **A deployment that mints standing envelopes runs its authority node on the WALL CLOCK.** An envelope's expiry is a real-time promise, and the daemon checks it against the host clock at every bind and draw; on the deterministic clock the ceremony landed outside its own consent window and then the grant read as already expired. The alpha journey now brings its node up with `--wall-clock`, and the deterministic-clock nonclaim is retired. | the journey's authority bring-up; `deploymentLocalOperatorCeremony` stamping on the node's committed clock | landed |
| **The deployment lane proves the whole consumer loop: `check:alpha-journey` with `IOI_ALPHA_JOURNEY_AUTHORITY=deployment` is 38/38**, including the attach-time mint under the ruled tier, a silent run to done with no approval card, a second run over the 1-usage envelope failing closed on `max_usages` with nothing run, and revocation refusing the next session naming that connection. | [`evidence/m13-alpha-journey-deployment-standing-2026-09-09.v1.json`](./evidence/m13-alpha-journey-deployment-standing-2026-09-09.v1.json) | qualified |
| **Three client-side defects the lane surfaced, fixed rather than tolerated:** the composer's submit escaped its handler on any non-typed error and dropped the socket, so a failure to create a run reached the caller as a transport error rather than an answer (it now always answers, with a typed code); the journey's HTTP client had a SILENT ~300 s transport budget that a real environment start can outrun (now explicit at 900 s); and a pooled keep-alive socket closed during a minutes-long model run made the next submit fail as a socket hang-up, read downstream as "no run" (now retried once on a fresh connection — a status is never retried). | `ioi-api-adapter.mjs`; the journey's `jd` | landed |

### Journey verification XXVI (2026-09-09, standing-lease program 2, leg 3 — typed effect recovery, enforced where the claim is decided)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **M06.2 — the declared recovery class SUBTRACTS, and cannot widen.** `evaluate_claim_transition` now calls `evaluate_claim_transition_substrate` first and returns early on anything but `Grant`, then lets the action's declared class refuse: `non_retryable` at all, `compensatable` before its compensation is recorded, and every ambiguity-blocked class. A class can therefore refuse a claim the two-phase substrate would grant and can **never** grant one it refuses. An admission that declares nothing keeps the substrate's own decision. | [`evidence/m06-2-typed-effect-recovery-2026-09-09.v1.json`](./evidence/m06-2-typed-effect-recovery-2026-09-09.v1.json); `m06-2-typed-effect-recovery` (`verified`); `npm run check:typed-effect-recovery` **7/7, 12 pinned tests** | landed |
| **Ambiguity blocks retry; restore never clears it.** A `reconciliation_required` prior with no reconciliation refuses `checkpointable`, `reconciliation_required` and `non_retryable` by name and sends `compensatable` to compensate first; only `replayable` proceeds, because an exact replay changes nothing whether or not the first attempt landed. `effect_is_reconciled` requires `source == external_system_readback` **and** an outcome of `effect_landed` or `effect_did_not_land`, so a restored environment, a restored backup or a re-read local record leaves a pending reconciliation exactly as pending as it was — which is the claim canon makes about restoration. | `governed_authority.rs` `evaluate_recovery`, `effect_is_reconciled` | landed |
| **Six planted defects, each red, each restored.** The class defaults instead of refusing an undeclared effect; the ambiguity block is disabled; any reconciliation source counts so a restore clears an ambiguity; **restart**, where an orphaned claim from a dead incarnation is re-granted instead of reconciled; the frozen member set is widened on one side only; and the narrowing is removed so the module becomes uncalled. `npm run mutate:typed-effect-recovery` **6/6**. | same evidence | landed |
| **No second spine for a canonical field.** The first cut read a private `recovery_class` key. Canon froze this field as `effect_recovery_class` and the ontology action contract already admits under that name, so the claim path was moved onto the canonical field and its typed set is now checked to be **exactly** the contract's frozen `EFFECT_RECOVERY_CLASSES` — one member set, one owner, read in two places, with a drill that widens one side and requires red. | `EFFECT_RECOVERY_CLASS_FIELD`; `the_typed_class_set_is_exactly_the_frozen_canonical_member_set` | landed |
| **A red THIS PROGRAM left on master, found by running the suite the pin lives in.** `c80621ac2` (XXV) added two MCP-bearing routes and classified both — required for daemon readiness — but a separate pinned count asserted `MCP_ROUTE_CLASSIFICATIONS` held exactly **36** entries. The unit's own verifier was 33/33 green and the alpha journey passed, because neither runs the daemon's whole suite; CI's would have failed. Re-pinned **36 → 38** with both routes named in the comment, and the full suite is now **1148 passed / 0 failed**. Same lesson as leg 1's `tokenMentions` drift: a unit's own green verifier is not evidence the estate is green. | `operability_routes.rs` `every_mcp_route_has_one_startup_classification` | repaired |
| **The check is a CI FLOOR, not a local ritual.** `ci.yml`'s `build_and_test` already runs the daemon's whole suite, which means a deleted or renamed test simply stops running and the suite stays green. `npm run check:typed-effect-recovery` now runs in that job immediately after it, pinning the twelve tests by exact name with `--list --exact` and `passed == pinned`, so removing the proof is red. It is the first of program 2's root-workspace wrappers to be floor-pinned; the rest are still local-only. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` step *Pin the typed effect-recovery population (M06.2)* | landed |
| **OPEN, and not this unit's to close (R-16): one field name carrying two vocabularies.** `runtime_work_lifecycle_log.rs`, added by `704c4d0dd` (M04.6, 2026-08-25), matches `effect_recovery_class` against `none / reversible / compensatable / irreversible / ambiguous` and refuses anything else as `work_lifecycle_cancellation_recovery_class_invalid`. Canon's set for that field is `replayable / checkpointable / compensatable / reconciliation_required / non_retryable`. Only `compensatable` is common, so a child declaring the canonical `reconciliation_required` is refused as invalid by the cancellation planner, and one declaring `irreversible` would be refused by the action contract. Named here rather than changed: it is M04.6's owner's call. **Closure test:** the planner's arms are asserted equal to `EFFECT_RECOVERY_CLASSES`. | `runtime_work_lifecycle_log.rs:1087`; `canonical-enums.md` § External-Effect Recovery Classes | open |

What is enforced is that a **declared** class is typed and honoured; requiring a declaration everywhere would refuse every existing effect path and is a separate, wider cut. Canon's grounding line for the enum now says exactly this. | `canonical-enums.md` | recorded |

| **Migrated, never silently reinterpreted — and never defaulted.** A record written under the retired vocabulary resolves through an explicit table that states what each retired member MEANT, derived from the planner's own action lists rather than name similarity: `none`→`replayable` (no external effect, so an exact replay changes nothing), `reversible`→`checkpointable` (resumes, requires no compensating act — deliberately NOT `compensatable`, which hard-refuses without a compensation policy and would newly refuse work admitted today), `irreversible`→`non_retryable`, `ambiguous`→`reconciliation_required`. `RecoveryClassResolution::Migrated` is a distinct variant so a caller must handle it. **The sharper half:** three projection sites minted the literal `none` for an ABSENT class, and the planner then treated it as the most permissive posture — cancel and drain, no fence, no reconciliation. That is the widening the claim gate refuses, so an undeclared class is now `work_lifecycle_cancellation_recovery_class_undeclared`. | `the_planner_arms_are_exactly_the_canonical_member_set`; `every_retired_member_plans_exactly_what_its_canonical_target_plans`; `an_undeclared_recovery_class_is_refused_rather_than_defaulted` | landed |

|---|---|---|

Canon gained the who-derives paragraph and the one-sentence caretaker definition it lacked. The composer's SystemReader reads the release/constitution/profile set from the daemon's SERVED genesis, and the gate proves the stamped values equal what the daemon answers. |
| **A registered invariant said one thing and tested another.** `collective_resolution_receipt.orchestration.is_a_resolved_owner`'s description claimed membership in `resolved_owner_refs`; its expression was `non_empty` on `orchestration_ref` — an assertion that could not fail the way its sentence claimed. | Fixed to `array_contains_value` over a path-valued needle (the evaluator supports it); a new negative with a root that DOES recompute and the orchestration removed from the owners; the gate posts that record around the composer and the seam refuses it by the rule's name. | The same class as the false-green negatives in the goal-run gate and the `provenBy` harness bug: a check that cannot fail is a claim. Oracle green at 1747 fixtures. |
| **The receipt lacked the seam's binding — R-197's defect, fourth instance — and the lineage had no record at all.** | Optional `system_binding` on the receipt (canon forbids narrowing in place); a new contract `persistent-executable-lineage/v1` carrying exactly what canon's Persistent Artifact Ecology paragraph lists, three positives (one under a System), seven negatives, five rules: root recompute over twenty-two members, typed orphan reason when quarantined, a fork names its parents and a transformation receipt, `active` binds a runtime. | A policy stop is not an orphan, so `stopped` carries no reason and the rule binds `quarantined` — said in the rule, said in canon. |
| **Driven, not read.** `check:collective-artifact-runtime-lifecycle` 22/22, exit 0, drill 25/25, exit 0, package suite 47/47, floor pinned at 22 from its own census, CI matrix leg, ACC-5 clause 10 executes it and N4 is proven inside it. | The gate installs into a REAL automation the daemon admitted through its own routes (project → automation), activates as a kernel-owned delegation of the coordinating thread, binds leases the M04.11 composer issued and revoked, and re-derives every root itself. After `plane.stop()` and a fresh start on the same data dir a client that remembers nothing reads the receipt and both lineages at the same heads and bytes. | What it does NOT claim is stated in the gate's header and the unit's manifest check: ArtifactRef production (no producer anywhere — R-205 asks the owner where artifact identity is minted), TTL elapse by clock, runtime health beyond served-or-not, a participation-backed caretaker. |
| **Five live runs to green, each red a fact the gate had to learn.** Run 1: the composer's System reader silently swallowed a non-200 — now it logs the read, and the assertion compares the daemon-served coordinates to the composed genesis at the daemon's content-pinned profile revision. Run 3: the registered receipt identity is `collective-resolution://crr_<sha256>`, not a slug — the composer refuses a slug by name and exports `collectiveReceiptId`. Run 4: `GET /v1/hypervisor/automation-executions/:id` answers 200 with `ok:false` for an unknown id, so a status-only served probe read a ghost as served; `POST /v1/hypervisor/projects` takes `{repository_url, project_name}` and answers the project-state projection (`selected_project_id`). Run 5: the retirement clause forbade any route literal containing the word lineage and matched `/v1/hypervisor/learning-lineage/impact` — M06.9's plane, not this unit's — so it now names this unit's own vocabulary. | Each run's failing detail is in the gate's own output; every fix landed in the gate or the composer, never in the daemon. | Recorded because the pattern is the lesson: a gate that prints its refusal messages converges in hours; one that prints only booleans does not. |
| **S5-3 is closed: M04.4, M04.11 and M04.12 — the head of the dependency graph's largest chain — in one day, all three as ioi.ai compositions over the seam.** | Blockers 24 → 23. | The pattern held every time it was applied: measure first, canon and contracts before code, cross-record obligations refused by name in the composer, single-record ones by registered invariant, and a driven gate that goes around the composer to prove the seam refuses on its own. |
| **ACC-5 RE-RUN ON THE FINAL TREE (`f18ecdb1f`): PARTIAL, exit 2 — 17 executed clause checks green and exactly ONE typed absence left.** | Clauses 1–8 as before; **9 `check:context-family-composition` 31/31 + drill 34/34; 10 `check:collective-artifact-runtime-lifecycle` 22/22 (1181 s) + drill 25/25 (1187 s)**; N1, N2, N3's native half and N4 PASS by `provenBy` against clauses that actually passed; E session-authority 24/24 + daemon suite 969/0/10. ### Journey verification CX (2026-09-19, MVP finish-line program — R-195 slice S5-3, step 1: the closure M04.4 is accepted on was committed by a root nobody recomputed)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **M04.4's acceptance is "profile resolution closure … committing the admission-time dependency closure", and the receipt that commits it had no invariant at all.** `goal-run-profile-resolution-receipt/v1` carries a `receipt_root` field; its registry row's `cross_field_invariant_refs` was empty and no invariants file existed. Its one positive fixture's root was the placeholder `sha256:1111…`. | Measured while planning the composition: ten of the sixteen goal-run and context contracts carry no invariants, including `goal-run/v2` and `context-cell/v2`. | A root nobody recomputes commits nothing. The rule now exists: `jcs_sha256_equals` over twenty-three material fields — the profile revision and hash, the override set, the constraint envelope, the orchestration policy, every workflow-template resolution and skill binding, the active skill set, every resolved harness-profile, adapter and runtime-tool revision, every requirement left as a LATE BINDING, the resolved component set, and the assurance stage — under the receipt's own identity. |
| **The fixture's root is now real, and two negatives prove the rule bites.** | Oracle green at 339 contracts. `negative-closure-root-does-not-recompute` edits one closure member and leaves the root; `negative-late-binding-added-after-the-root` adds a requirement to the unresolved set after the fact. Both are refused by name. | The second negative is the sharper one: a late binding silently resolved away, or added, after the root was taken is exactly how a resolution closure stops being closed. |
| **Two orphan constants in the neutral kernel module, invisible to the compiler.** `DEFINITION_RESOLUTION_SCHEMA_VERSION` and `PROFILE_RESOLUTION_RECEIPT_SCHEMA_VERSION`, both naming `applications.ioi-ai.*` contracts, lost their only user when `resolve_definitions` left in S5-1. | Each had exactly one reference repo-wide: its own definition. | DELETED. A `pub const` in a library is never dead-code-flagged, so they survived a cut that removed everything around them AND a rename that made the module platform-neutral, still spelling the application's name. Found by grepping for the receipt's producers, not by any gate — which is the lesson: the compiler's dead-code pass does not reach public items, so a deletion slice has to grep for its own residue. |
| **What this step does NOT claim.** No live code writes this receipt — the plane that did was deleted in S5-1 — so the invariant binds a contract with no current producer. | Stated rather than implied. | That is the point of doing it first: the composition S5-3 writes will have to produce a real closure root to be admitted at all, instead of inheriting a field it could fill with anything. The invariant is the precondition, not a repair of a live write path. |

### Journey verification CIX (2026-09-19, MVP finish-line program — R-194 slice S5-2a: the delegation edge ADR 0034 ruled the kernel owns, written for the first time, and the owner it needed that nobody had)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **ADR 0034 was Accepted on 2026-08-07 and never implemented, and its August census still measured true today.** It rules that `runtime_thread_fork_control` is the delegation primitive, that the Subagent API is a surface owning no delegation semantics, that bounds live in the kernel admission and nowhere else, and (sub-ruling 5) that the delegation EDGE is a `child_reference` in the work-owning graph. | Measured: `/v1/threads/:id/fork` registered 0; `max_subagents`, `fanout_cap`, `delegation_bound` all 0 repo-wide; the fork planner reached from exactly ONE place, the harness-session-launch path, and only when the caller passes a `delegation` object; `handle_subagent_spawn` reaching it never. | The canon was already right and needed no edit — `api.md`'s Subagent API section already states the composition, structural inheritance and derived cancellation. Canon said it; the code did not do it. That makes canon the acceptance text and the slice pure implementation. |
| **The edge is now a registered contract, and the bound is checked rather than trusted.** `schema://ioi/foundations/delegation-edge/v1`, the estate's 339th. | `cargo test -p ioi-types --lib golden_fixtures_match_generated_rust_contracts` green; nine fixtures, seven negative, each naming the rule it must trip. | Depth is asserted against the ancestor chain's LENGTH by invariant rather than accepted as a field, because a depth stated independently of the chain is a number a caller can set. `depth <= ceiling` makes ADR 0034 sub-ruling 3's structural inheritance checkable. The resolver revision and content hash are required exactly when the resolver kind is not `none`, so a resolution is a binding rather than a re-aimable pointer. |
| **"Topology-agnostic" was ruled to mean the platform branches on NOTHING, not that it supports everything.** `role_kind` and `topology_kind` are carried and recorded; no match arm, no default, no behaviour anywhere reads either value. The vocabularies stay closed by the CONTRACT, so a nonsense value is refused at admission while the code stays value-blind. | `topology_kind` appears zero times in `crates/` outside generated contract code, so nothing had to be unwound. | The retired kernel emitted ONE hardcoded topology and branched on it. A platform enumerating ten would be that defect at a larger size, because a platform that can name a topology will eventually branch on it. |
| **THE PREREQUISITE THE CODE FOUND BY BEING WRITTEN: there was no owner to bound a delegation against.** `handle_subagent_spawn` took `State`, `Path` and `Json` and no `HeaderMap`. It resolved no principal, and `build_agent_candidate` writes no owner, principal or tenant field. A subagent was spawned by nobody, for nobody. | Its presence in `audit-admission-evidence-provenance.mjs`'s H_BASELINE as a known mutating handler resolving no identity. | That is the DEEPER reason the surface never had a bound: a bound narrows an OWNER's ceiling and there was no owner. The handler now resolves identity as its first act and answers 401 before anything is minted, which removes it from that baseline **by doing the thing the baseline exists to ask for** — the first removal there that is a repair rather than a deletion. |
| **A unit test caught the slice's own worst bug before it shipped: an absent bound became a real inherited ceiling.** The edge must carry an integer ceiling because its contract requires one, so an underivable bound is written there as the depth itself. Writing that same number into the child's POSITION record invented a ceiling out of an absence — the child inherited "ceiling 1" and its own children were refused for exceeding a bound nobody ever set. | `depth_is_the_chain_length_and_a_grandchild_narrows_from_its_parents_record` failed with `DepthCeilingExceeded { depth: 2, ceiling: 1 }`. | Fixed at the propagation: the position carries the ceiling as DERIVED (`None` when underivable), so absence propagates as absence. And the contract gained a required `depth_bound_absent` boolean, because without it a reader of the stored edge alone cannot tell an enforced ceiling from a recorded one — and a record that cannot be told apart from an enforced one is worse than no record. Two more tests pin both halves. |
| **Driven, not read.** | `check:orchestration-composition` 18 → 22 against an isolated daemon and the real wallet fixture, drill 25/25, floor re-pinned. Four new assertions: the edge is admitted on a real spawn with its coordinate composed from the pair it names; the platform records role and topology and interprets neither; the underivable bound is REPORTED absent with the ceiling equal to the depth; and an anonymous spawn is refused before anything is minted. | The gate passed 18/18 with the new code before those assertions existed, which is exactly why they were added: a slice whose behaviour no gate asserts is a slice that is not verified, however green the run. |
| **And run 25 was red for a file that was regenerated but never staged — the sweep validates the TREE, the push ships the INDEX.** The delegation-edge contract gained `depth_bound_absent` after its first generate, which regenerated BOTH projections. The Rust one is under `crates/` and went in with the code commit; the TypeScript one is under `packages/`, which that commit's `git add` did not include. | `Build and Test` and `M06.1` both red on their contract-projection steps; locally `check:architecture-contracts` said "up to date" because on disk it WAS. | Fixed by shipping the projection. The lesson is the seam rather than the file: a generated artifact that is regenerated but unstaged passes every local gate, because those gates read the working tree, and fails the identical gate in CI, because CI reads the commit. `git status` is part of the pre-push habit and was not part of mine. |
| **CI run 24 has ONE red, confirmed to the STEP but NOT to the test names — said plainly because the rule is to confirm every red from its log.** | The job is `Rust library-suite floor (R-21)`; its steps show every crate suite green except exactly one, `Library suite — ioi-networking`, which is R-80's crate. The job's log blob answers `BlobNotFound` and two re-downloads of the run archive omit that job's file, so the three individual test names could not be read this time. They WERE read on runs 20 and 22: `delayed_server_hello_cannot_destroy_current_handshake`, `genuine_proof_evicts_squatter_pending_handshake_state` and `live_outbound_failure_on_unproven_enrollment_reports_loss_and_status_reenrollment_recovers`, 49 passed 3 failed. | CLASSIFIED AS R-80 ON THE STEP, with the gap in the evidence recorded rather than papered over. Proven: the failure is confined to the ioi-networking suite and no other crate. NOT proven: that it was the same three tests. Run 23 passed this same suite entirely, which is what an intermittent looks like. |
| **What this slice does NOT claim.** Fanout is not yet enforced — the edge carries the reservation ref when a caller supplies one and the `concurrent_invocations` claim is not yet made on its behalf. Four of ADR 0031's five primitive refs are null: only `forked_thread_ref` is populated, because the child thread exists; managed session, launch recipe and harness binding do not exist until the child launches. The child mint still does not go through `plan_runtime_thread_fork_control`. | Stated here rather than discovered later. | S5-2b: route the child mint through the fork planner, make the fanout claim, and turn `depth_bound_absent` from a recorded fact into a refusal once every caller supplies a bound. |

### Journey verification CVIII (2026-09-18, MVP finish-line program — R-192 slice S5-1: the owner ruled goal pursuit out of the Hypervisor, so the GoalRun plane, the IOI-Agent launch plane and the runtime goal-run admission kernel are deleted, and the work-lifecycle record chain gets the generic writer its retirement left it without)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|

| **The regression the cut created, found by reading the plane rather than the diff: the work-lifecycle record chain lost its only writer.** Its routes are read-only (records, projection, status, cancellation-plan, compaction) plus `POST reservations`, and `append_gated` — the record-chain writer — had exactly one production caller, the GoalRun admission path. Deleting it would have left a platform plane with a gate, a kernel, five durable families, a CI job and nothing able to write to it. | `grep` for `append_gated` callers at HEAD versus the tree. | CLOSED IN THE SAME CUT. `POST /v1/hypervisor/work-lifecycle/records` is the generic writer: owner-scoped to the authenticated principal BEFORE the stream is read, then the same kernel admission, exact-head CAS, object-scoped idempotency and projection rebuild. A platform plane whose only writer is one application is not a platform plane. |
| **The platform's gate is ONE rule, and it is the one the kernel leaves open.** `append_gated` demands a `LegalEdgeGate` because the shared kernel never acquires a caller's authority table. ### Journey verification CVII (2026-09-18, MVP finish-line program — R-178 slice S4d-3 under R-191: the hypervisor's Missions surface is re-typed onto the planes that still exist, and the Work subject registry's contributed family becomes the orchestration — S4d complete)

It now reads exactly `/v1/goal-orchestration/goal-runs`, `/v1/hypervisor/work-results` and `/v1/hypervisor/operations`, and renders a GoalRun list, an exact `?goal=` selection, a detail pane (status, continuation, admission path, the `orchestration_ref` the composing application stamped, its own results, its blockers) and the unchanged run queue and incident lane. The membership ref is RENDERED, never resolved: the orchestration record is application vocabulary and this surface reaches no application seam. | `check:surface-modules` **69/69**, including a new probe that RECORDS every path the surface requests and asserts the set is exactly those three — "it no longer asks" is a stronger claim than any fixture could carry. | **Closed.** |

| **Records follow the surface; history does not.** The missions entry in `application-operational-depth.json` — workflow, reached states, control-census ids, labels, bindings, implemented census, route list and the `federated room authority` nonclaim — names the GoalRun inspector. The `landing-designations`, `ported-seed-preservation` and `reference-*` records are NOT rewritten: an archive edited to match today stops being a record of what was true when it was written. | `check:architecture-docs` OK; `check:named-gap-truth` **12/12** after atlas decided 224 → **228** (the entry now names the three planes it reads where it named one) and absence labels 120 → **118** (the room-graph absence assertions retired with their planes). | **Closed.** |

It wants a wiring decision with a floor row, which is its own slice, and is recorded rather than quietly deleted. | `git grep` across package scripts, CI and the floors manifest: no reference. | **Open (its own slice).** |
| **Driven on the real wallet fixture.** `check:systems-work-projections` **21/21** (the registry answer now names `goal_run, orchestration`), `check:m3-goalrun-plane` **14/14** and `check:orchestration-records` **21/21** — the two GoalRun-plane gates re-run unchanged, which is the evidence that this slice moved a registry and a surface without moving a runtime path. | The three logs; the projections floor's assertion-NAME hash re-pinned (its count is unchanged at 21 — one assertion's text moved). | **Closed.** |

| **THE DRIVEN CHAIN EARNED ITS PLACE: one surviving line failed five gates, and the static suites all passed.** The first chain over this cut went red on five of seven gates. One cause: the direct GoalRun record still authored `"room_participant_lease_ref": Value::Null`, a member `goal-run/v2` removed under `additionalProperties: false`, so `validate_architecture_contract` answered **422 `goal_run_contract_invalid`** on every creation — goal-profile-contracts failed four assertions and m3-goalrun-plane, m4-goalrun-activation-plane and m4-work-lifecycle-plane CRASHED reading a records directory that was never written, because no GoalRun was ever admitted. No other gate loads a contract by filename — measured across `apps/hypervisor/scripts`, `scripts` and `packages`. | The re-run below; the gate reached 48 passing assertions before these three, which is what localised it. | **Closed.** |

The daemon, moved to the v2 profile, was refused **422 `goal_run_activation_receipt_contract_invalid`** on every activation. The const now names the contract it IS, its five fixtures follow, and their receipt roots are recomputed (the profile ref is inside the hashed material). | `generate` → `check:architecture-contracts` up to date; `cargo test -p ioi-types --lib golden_fixtures_match_generated_rust_contracts` **1 passed** on the 1718-fixture bar; `check:m4-goalrun-activation-plane` **52/52**. | **Closed.** |

Reading one gate's 403 and flipping every assertion to it was wrong and the next chain said so: the composer's own client is local-development (404) while the portal session is identity-enforced (403). | **Two more tenants measured and taken, neither named in the hand-off.** (1) The hypervisor product UI served `/__ioi/goal-space?room=…`, an M4 "Goal Space proof projection" page rendering a room through the deleted routes — a SECOND live consumer R-185's grep had not seen because the path was assembled at run time; R-179 puts no room surface in the hypervisor app, so the page, its renderer and its allowlist entry are deleted (the GoalRun timeline shares its two-minute read bound and keeps it). | **The substrate's room operation family went with its only caller.** `admit_outcome_room_system_operation` and the two `read_outcome_room_system_operation*` readers (plus their test) were called only from the deleted module; R-178 had placed them in S4c-3 with the contract, but dead admitters that name rooms are exactly what R-179 forbids, so they are deleted here and S4c-3 shrinks to the contract, the agentgres refs and ACC-9's re-type. The work-lifecycle status no longer declares a hosted-room owner binding, and its nonclaim says why. The dead room-context authority wrapper is gone; the context variant stays because the genesis test uses it to prove context-distinct policy hashes. | `check:m4-work-lifecycle-plane` pins binding[0] = goal_run and the kernel nonclaim phrase — both hold. | **Deleted with its caller.** |
