### Journey verification CII (2026-09-17, MVP finish-line program — R-185 slice S4c-1: the ioi.ai goal space consumes the composition, the orchestration becomes a record under its System, and the two platform read paths the goal space runs through are re-typed to the primitives that composition drives)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The ioi.ai web application no longer calls a daemon room route.** R-185 measured six hosted-v2 OutcomeRoom routes proxied by `apps/ioi-ai/plugins/web-ui` — the one live consumer the room family had — and ruled that S4c does not delete a route an application still calls. This slice re-points that application at ITS OWN composition: `/api/ioi/orchestrations/*` runs `packages/ioi-ai-orchestration` over the agent SDK's substrate client, built for one request on the daemon authority the BFF's gateway verifies (a new `authority` resolver beside `request`). Composing creates the coordinating thread and admits the orchestration record through the System-record seam; list, open, graph, replay and delegations are reads; GoalRun attach/detach and status are revisions on the exact head the client loaded. The screen, its validators, deep links, CSS and copy speak "orchestration" — the owner's "rename any room-like terminology" applied to the one surface that still said room. | Six proxies (238 lines) replaced by one dispatch into `server/orchestration-routes.ts`; `server/ioi-daemon.ts` gains `authority`; client `goal-space*.ts`, `deep-link.ts`, `shell.css` renamed end to end. Web-ui typecheck (3 tsconfigs) green; `npm test` **544/544** (52 in the goal-space, deep-link and security files, the server-route test now driving compose → attach → stale 409 → already-attached 409 → detach → list 3 revisions → replay → graph against a seam-faithful stub); `npm run smoke:browser` **33 route renders** plus the interaction journey composing, attaching and detaching with **three revisions** on the fake seam and the assertion that no `outcome-rooms` daemon path was called. | **Re-pointed; the room routes have no consumer left and die in S4c-2.** |
| **The orchestration is a record: `applications/ioi-ai/orchestration/v1`, the successor of OutcomeRoom v2.** The S2 handle (`thread_ref`, `owner_ref`, `orchestration_ref`) made durable under the bounded System, with the objective, the mode, the governance refs the application declares, the attached GoalRuns and a three-state status. Two invariants pin it: the composition scope equals the binding's parent scope, and the scope is derived from the id and from nothing else (`field_suffix_equals_prefixed_field`). `outcome-room/v2` now names it as `successor_contract_id` and stays registered until S4c-2 deletes its routes. | Schema + invariants + **6 fixtures** (2 positive, 2 invariant negatives, 2 schema negatives); `generate:architecture-contracts` and `check:architecture-contracts` green; the Rust golden oracle **1 passed** with the bar moved **1686 → 1692**; `check:architecture-docs` 183 files / 11 rules; `check:canon-document-residuals` + its drill green. Canon: `collaborative-pursuit.md` § *OrchestrationEnvelope* (and *OutcomeRoomEnvelope* marked retiring), `orchestration-api.md`, daemon `api.md`. | **Registered as the successor** (R-178's naming). |
| **The composer lives where the owner said compositions live.** `packages/ioi-ai-orchestration/src/orchestrations.ts`: `Orchestrations.compose / list / open / attachGoalRun / detachGoalRun / transition / graph / delegations`, every method one daemon read, one admission or one exact-head revision; the instance remembers nothing. Its own refusals are named (`orchestration_goal_run_already_attached`, `…_not_attached`, `…_closed`, `…_members_over_bound`, `…_expected_head_required`, `…_admission_unreadable`); a seam refusal is passed through untouched — the composer never pre-empts the stream with a read of its own. | Unit tests **10/10** over a seam-faithful fake (exact heads, derived binding, chain, list), including the record's member set pinned against the REGISTERED schema and the registry row's successor pointer; package built and exported (`./orchestrations`). | **Landed** (R-172's home for compositions). |
| **THE GATE, and the two platform findings it made.** `check:orchestration-records` (**19/19** on the real wallet fixture, floor-pinned, CI matrix entry, drill D1–D4) composes, lists, re-attaches, revises, transitions, projects, restarts, and then mints THE SAME portal exchange session the ioi.ai BFF mints and drives the composition under it. Two things the first run found, both platform, both fixed in this cut: (1) **the portal session could not reach the primitives** — it was scoped to `/v1/goal-orchestration/` plus whoami/logout, so the re-pointed BFF would have been 403 on every composition call in production. The session mint now names exact routes (`/v1/threads`, the Systems projection) and per-segment patterns (`/v1/threads/*`, `/v1/threads/*/subagents`, `…/autonomous-systems/*/records`, `…/records/*/*`) through a new `allowed_route_patterns` member of `session_allows_route` — a pattern cannot widen into a prefix, so a System's genesis, activation and transition routes, the work-lifecycle plane, secrets and a thread's turns stay `hypervisor.auth_session_route_scope_denied` (driven). (2) **The Systems projection listed no System for any principal.** Its visibility rule was `proposed_by_ref == user://principal` — unsatisfiable, because the genesis contract admits only `system|wallet|org|project://` proposers — or `owner_ref` in the tenant, a member no genesis record carries; the M08.9 gate had only ever asserted `honest_empty`. The goal space enumerates Systems through that projection, so the gap became reachable and is fenced in the same cut (the no-nonclaim rule): a third clause makes a System visible to a principal that holds System-record seam scopes under it — the seam's own discipline, read once before any row. | Daemon unit tests **8 passed** (route-pattern matcher, the widened portal scope with six allowed and eight denied paths, three projection-visibility tests); the gate's `[portal]` assertion moved from `systems: []` / `honest_empty` to the System listed as `active` under the portal session. | **Fixed in the same cut** (R-186). |
| **One more driven correction, recorded because a fake had lied politely.** An orchestration the seam does not hold answers **403 `request_resource_scope_required`** — the seam discloses neither absence nor existence — not the 404 the composer, the server-route stub and the browser fake all assumed. The composer maps only the seam's own 404 to `orchestration_absent` and passes the scope refusal through; both fakes now answer as the seam does; the gate asserts the 403. | Gate run 1 **16/19** → run 2 **18/19** → run 3 **19/19**; each red read from its detail before the next change. | **Corrected at the depth the assertion lives.** |
| **What is NOT claimed.** The GoalRun side of membership: no reciprocal write lands in a GoalRun and its projection does not yet name its orchestration (S4d composes GoalRun over the orchestration). The ioi.ai web UI has no CI coverage (the guide's open question on whether, and from which root, the ioi.ai web UI ships stays open; its tests, typecheck and smokes ran locally and are recorded here). `smoke:real-daemon` in binary-isolated mode proves reads only — no active System exists there, so it lists no orchestration. | — | **Typed absences.** |
| **Registry and wiring.** | Manifest M12.4 check text names the third gate; `implementation-program --check` 14 modules / 157 units / 21 gates; CI `m4_plane_verifiers` matrix +1 (`orchestration-records`, build daemon + SDK + package, verify, drill); `verifier-floors.v1.json` +1 row at 19 / `276c182a…`; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; blockers **24**, unchanged — this slice is the structural step R-185 inserted before S4c-2, on the chain that closes M04.12. | **Wired.** |

### Journey verification CI (2026-09-17, owner direction R-182 — decentralized.cloud and its sibling specs leave this repository, and the placement path keeps running on the eight provider adapters that were always doing the proposing)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The owner paused the MVP program to defer the candidate engines: "move decentralized cloud into a new repo … and utilize underlying depin + centralized cloud integrations in place of it."** The reasoning is the re-entry test, so it is recorded rather than summarized: fees accrue where AUTHORITY is exercised and where PLACEMENT is decided, so an engine that only proposes was never where the revenue was; `RouteCandidate` and `CandidateEvidence` are the durable half and the producer is swappable by construction, so building the engine first built the replaceable and expensive half; and using DePIN does not imply owning the intelligence layer above it. | [ADR 0054](../../decisions/0054-defer-the-decentralized-candidate-engines-and-place-through-the-underlying-providers.md), superseding [ADR 0051](../../decisions/0051-decentralized-cloud-public-face-job-primitive-and-supply-registry.md). | **Accepted, and 0051 marked superseded rather than deleted** — its boundaries still bind any candidate source. |
| **MEASURING FIRST CHANGED THE WORK, and this is the finding of the cut.** `decentralized_cloud_routes.rs` was not a decentralized.cloud plane. It is the GENERIC cloud-candidate plane behind `/v1/hypervisor/cloud-candidates/*`, enumerating THIRTEEN sources — and `decentralized.cloud` was one of them, answering `candidate_source_unavailable` with reason `network_adapter_absent` on every call, because the engine was never live. Six of its 1,229 lines mentioned the product. The substitution the owner asked for was therefore already the operating reality in code; the work was to make the names and the product match it. | The eight adapters that actually propose are unchanged: Akash (`depin_market`), Vast, RunPod, AWS, GCP, Azure, Lambda, Kubernetes — plus `storage_network`, `customer_inventory` and `direct_provider`. | **Nothing had to be written to replace the product.** |
| **The plane is renamed for what it is.** `decentralized_cloud_routes.rs` → `cloud_candidate_routes.rs` across seven files, the never-live source declaration deleted, and its canonical doctrine owner moved to [`byo-provider-plane.md`](../components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md). The routes are untouched: a rename and the deletion of a dead declaration, not a behaviour change. | `cargo build -p ioi-node --bin hypervisor-daemon` exit 0; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; the candidate-plane gate's external-source assertion drops to the three that remain (`managed_capacity`, `depin_market`, `storage_network`). | **Renamed, not re-pointed.** |
| **141 files left this repository with their history, and nothing was lost to make that true.** The product was extracted with `git filter-repo` into its own local repository at original paths — 576 commits on two branches (`main`, and `target-ux` carrying 449 commits that never merged). The other four decentralized-cloud branches were measured `ahead 0` and carried nothing `main` does not. | Verified before deleting: every one of the 140 tracked paths present in the new repository, zero missing. Uncommitted work preserved — the live branch's 521 dirty files and 234 untracked source, control-plane, script and architecture files (including two ADRs that had never been committed), committed on its branch; two other worktrees' work kept as patches. | **Extracted and verified, then deleted.** |
| **What was deliberately NOT carried, and was not deleted either.** The 2.3 GB target-UX browser evidence tree and the regenerable `dist-*` outputs stay in their original worktree. A third worktree's three uncommitted files were left exactly where they were because they are **Hypervisor daemon** files, not decentralized.cloud files — the kind of thing a path-shaped cleanup quietly takes. | Recorded in the new repository's `preserved-wip/README.md` with the origin worktree named for each item. | **Preserved by not being touched.** |
| **Four archived forwarding records remain in canon, and that is deliberate.** 39 links pointed into the deleted tree — from live canon, and from `_archive` implementation logs. Rewriting an archive to point somewhere else falsifies a record of what was true when it was written, so the links resolve instead to tombstones carrying the decision, the substitution table and the re-entry triggers. | `check:architecture-docs` **183 files, 11 rules OK**; work items 72 records, 425 anchors. | **Forwarded, not rewritten.** |
| **Canon prose was left standing where it was already true.** The estate had written these engines as permissive throughout — Hypervisor "may" or "optionally" uses decentralized.cloud, "or other candidate sources", a "preferred" engine — so those sentences survive the product's departure unchanged. Exactly two active claims were false and were re-typed: `byo-provider-plane.md`'s "is filled by the decentralized.cloud candidate plane", and the vocabulary term, now marked deferred. | 16 decentralized brand tokens had zero consumers once the console left and moved to the new repository as `brand/brand-tokens.css`; the shared palette drops 42 → 26 tokens. | **Re-typed only where it became untrue.** |
| **R-18 changed owner, not subject — because an unowned red is worse than an owned one.** The mutation-event coverage finding on `handle_cloud_job_execute` belonged to the decentralized-cloud program. That handler and `cloud_job_routes.rs` did not leave; the program did. | ACC-1's fence, the CI job's name and the admission-evidence baseline comment now name the MVP finish-line program, and the closure is this program's to schedule. | **Re-owned** (R-182). |
| **The product’s BUILD PROGRAM left with the product, and the sweep is what found it.** The pre-push sweep went 17/19 on `guide structure` and `release-readiness scope`: the private manifest still carried module **M15** (nine units) and journey **ACC-21**, and M15.1's check named an npm script deleted with the app. Calling it “To be authored” would have been false — it was authored, and it left. Retired instead: the module, its nine units and ACC-21 (all `product_track`, none ever a release blocker), with their private planning docs moved to `_archive/` where the structure walker does not scan. | The routes M15 owned did NOT leave: `/v1/hypervisor/cloud-candidates` is re-owned to **M09** (providers and environments substrate), and `MODULE_IDS` drops M15 with the reason recorded at the pin. Guide structure **14 modules, 157 build units, 21 acceptance gates**; `check:release-readiness-scope` **6/6**; blockers **24**, unchanged, over the same **121 mvp_required units and 15 composed journeys** — the MVP accounting never moved because a product track was never counted. | **Retired with its product; the routes stayed and changed owner.** |
| **Re-entry is a measurement, not a plan.** Cloud: when optimized-placement volume makes provider margin exceed the cost of running an engine, or when no provider exposes the custody, failover and restore evidence needed to admit a candidate. Exchange: when wallet-authorized swap flow makes aggregator pricing cost more than running routing, or when aggregators cannot supply the evidence fields policy needs. | Both are visible from the wallet and the daemon. | **Recorded as the trigger.** |
| **One claim outside this repository is now untrue and is named rather than fixed here.** Any published roadmap or ecosystem milestone presenting exchange and trade candidate clients as ACTIVE must move to planned or gated. That data is not in this tree. | — | **Open, owner's to place.** |
| **Registry and wiring.** | Shipped products 7 → **6 lanes** (`check:shipped-products` preflight passed, 104 source-graph files) with its manifest library and test re-typed; the workspace, its lockfile entry, four `.gitignore` rules and the hypervisor live-quotes script removed. Census: modules unmoved at 122 (a rename adds and removes nothing), token mentions 145379 → **145372** (−7, the dead source declaration). `check:named-gap-truth`, `check:env-lease-authority`, `check:ontology-admission-census` **27/27**, `check:admission-evidence` all green. | **Re-pinned with the reason beside the number.** |

### Journey verification XCVII (2026-09-16, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — M11.2 CLOSED (R-175): portable exit as a fact on top of acceptance and federated admission as a declared, co-signed mode, composed over M11.1 and driven 16/16 on the real wallet fixture)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Exit is a third revision, and acceptance is history.** The first cut made exit a lifecycle status (`exited`), which contradicted the registered rule that a present decision sets the status to its verdict; the portable invariant language cannot express the disjunction. | `OrchestrationParticipationRequest` v2 (successor of v1, additive): `exit: { status_at_exit: accepted, bundle_ref, exited_at, reason_code, receipt_ref }` with invariant `exit.only_after_acceptance` (an exit sits on an accepted participation and on nothing else); the status stays `accepted`. `Collaboration.exitParticipation` produces the bundle (`voluntary_retirement`) first and then the exit citing it and the accepted revision's seam receipt; a bundle is never produced twice for a participant that left. | **Registered and composed.** Canon states why the status does not move. |
| **Federated admission is a declared mode whose decision is co-signed.** | v2 `decision.adjudicator_ref` + `decision.federation_signature` (nullable); invariant `decision.by_the_admission_owner` (hosted: the host System's own; federated: the declared policy path's). The adjudicator is a party of the active terms with role `coordinator`, signing `ioi.orchestration-participation-decision-jcs-sha256.v1` with the key it accepted the terms with; `verifyParticipationDecision` verifies offline. The composer refuses a federated decision without the adjudicator, with a non-party adjudicator, with a key that is not the key of record, a hosted decision that carries one, a request whose mode is not the projection's, a hosted owner that is not the host, and a federated owner that is not a policy path. | **Composed as ruled (R-175).** The co-signature requirement is the composer's obligation, gate-proven, because the DSL cannot condition on topology through a nullable object. |
| **Driven proof.** `apps/hypervisor/scripts/verify-hypervisor-aiip-portable-exit.mjs` (`check:aiip-portable-exit`, drill `mutate:…`, CI matrix `aiip-portable-exit`, census id `aiip-portable-exit`, floor row at 16), through the built SDK and package on the isolated daemon and the REAL wallet fixture, the worker and the adjudicator signing from the fixture's known seeds. | **16/16** (run 2 on 2026-09-16; run 1 taught that an exit for a record never submitted is refused by the seam's own scope fence before the composer, which is the more accurate claim). Drill **19/19**. The S3 gate re-certified **20/20** on the extended composer. ACC-13 clause 6 executes the gate, clause 7 is `provenBy` it. M11.2 check text typed; release-readiness blockers **26 → 25**. |
| **Not claimed.** Cross-domain transport (M11.3 bindings) — the requests are built by the party's signer in-process; the exited party's lineage refs are opaque strings whose resolution across a real boundary is M11.3's claim; the crosswalk crossing (ACC-13 clause 4). | Named in the gate's header and the manifest. | **Recorded.** |

|---|---|---|
| **Where the composer lives (R-174).** `apps/ioi-ai` — the one ioi.ai application — is not a workspace member, depends on no `@ioi/*` package and makes no daemon call in its source; its rebind doc promises composition the code does not yet do. An `applications/ioi-ai/` namespace inside the agent SDK would blur the platform/application layer the way R-155 forbids in core. | New workspace package `packages/ioi-ai-orchestration` (`@ioi/ioi-ai-orchestration`) over `@ioi/agent-sdk`, workspace-registered in `package-lock.json` (12 lines; no dev dependencies of its own, the root hoists esbuild and tsc). `src/collaboration.ts`: `Collaboration.proposeTerms / acceptTerms / publishDiscovery / admitParticipation / decideParticipation / produceStateBundle`, the pure `buildParticipationRequest` (the external party's side), `verifyStateBundle` (offline), `deriveTermsActivation`, the three root oracles, and ed25519 signers. Unit test 5/5 pins the root member lists against the registered invariants' material fields and recomputes every positive fixture. | **Composed as ruled, owner-reversible to `apps/ioi-ai` once that app reaches the daemon.** |
| **Four successor contracts, no room token in any of them.** Terms v2 carried no binding and so could not pass the seam; the room-hosted discovery, participation and bundle shapes bound to leases and admission decisions the composition does not mint. | `foundations/objects/collaboration-terms-envelope/v3` (system_binding, `required_party_refs`, append-only signed `acceptances`, DERIVED nullable `activation` with `status_when_activated`); `applications/ioi-ai/orchestration-discovery/v1` (successor of `outcome-room-discovery/v1`; `discovery_id`, `orchestration_ref`, terms ref and root required, `private_orchestration_state` among the excluded classes); `applications/ioi-ai/orchestration-participation-request/v1` (successor of `room-participation-request/v3`; `discovery_ref` always named, `requester_system_ref`, `request_hash`, structured `signature`, nullable `decision` carrying its verdict `status`); `applications/ioi-ai/participant-state-bundle/v4` (successor of v3; `participation_ref`, `hosted_database_access_required` const false, all seven excluded classes pinned exactly). Registry 322 rows; predecessors keep `successor_contract_id`; canon sections added under new headings so the predecessors' anchors survive. Golden oracle green. | **Registered.** The seven remaining application contracts still bound to OutcomeRoom members are S4's re-cut. |
| **What the portable invariant language can and cannot say, written down.** A root must not commit `system_binding` (the seam derives the binding from a payload root that would contain the root — circular). The Rust evaluator reads any object as empty under `non_empty`, and the DSL refuses a path through a nullable object, so "active ⇒ activation present" and "decided ⇒ decision present" are inexpressible as rules. | Encoded as the entailment the language can express: `collaboration_terms.activation.implies_active_status` and `orchestration_participation_request.decision.sets_the_status` (`optional_fields_equal` through the optional object onto a status member inside it), plus exact-root rules and the three JCS root commitments. The forward obligations are the composer's and the gate proves them (a proposal refused for publication, an activation re-derived offline, a decision that cites the receipt). Canon states the boundary rather than implying coverage. | **Recorded in canon and here.** |
| **Driven proof.** `apps/hypervisor/scripts/verify-hypervisor-aiip-collaboration-discovery.mjs` (`check:aiip-collaboration-discovery`, drill `mutate:…`, CI matrix `aiip-collaboration-discovery` building the daemon, the SDK and the package; census id `aiip-collaboration-discovery`; floor row at 20), through the built SDK and package on the isolated daemon and the REAL wallet fixture, the external parties signing from the fixture's known seeds. Terms proposed → discovery refused (`collaboration_terms_not_active`, nothing admitted) → host accepts (not activated) → alloy-lab accepts → ACTIVE with the activation naming the two prior seam receipts and re-deriving offline → unrequired party and repeat acceptance refused → discovery admitted (root recomputed, host signature verified) → same-system request refused (N1, nothing admitted) → unknown-signer, forged-key, tampered-body and stale-root requests refused by name → genuine request admitted as submitted → decided as revision 2 citing the submission's receipt → re-decision refused → bundle least-disclosing (raw-secret and private-state refs dropped, seven classes pinned, verified offline; another key and a smuggled ref refused; a refused participation gets no bundle) → restart with a re-attached composer reading all four chains → structure. ACC-13 runner: clause 1 executes the gate, clauses 2, 3 and N1 are `provenBy` it; clause 4 and M11.2 stay absent by name. | **20/20** (run 3 on 2026-09-16; runs 1–2 found the seam finding below and my own two: a refusal-order mismatch and a key-order comparison). Drill **23/23**. M11.1 check text typed in the manifest; release-readiness blockers **27 → 26**. |
| **Seam finding (S1, this cut).** The chain view served no `receipt_ref` or `operation_ref` per revision, so a decision could not cite the submission's receipt and an activation could not name the acceptance receipts after a restart. | `system_record_routes.rs` `project()` derives both from the projection's coordinates exactly as the admit reply does; the gate asserts the decision's receipt equals the admit reply's. | **FIXED.** |
| **CI run 6 classified, and two reds were this program's own.** R-18 (fenced), goalrun-activation-plane (R-75, the refusal at the named anchor evidenced), Rust library-suite floor (R-80, exactly the three ioi-networking tests, 49/3), census floor (downstream of R-75, one `census_missing`). **Build and Test** failed on the golden and the ajv-differential oracles: the S0 registry row for `outcome-room-discovery/v1` listed participation fixtures as its own, and the terms v2 row listed v1 fixtures that lack `party_roles`. **Product builds and browser smoke** failed on `check:source-neutral` (CI-only, not in the pre-push sweep), which refuses the word "borrowed" in one M09.7 assertion label. | Discovery row re-pointed at its own fixtures; v2 given v2 copies of its fixtures (`party_roles`/`activation` null); the label reworded and the custody-proven floor's names hash re-pinned from a census-emitting 39/39 run. | **FIXED in this cut**, pushed with it. |

### Journey verification XCV (2026-09-16, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — R-172 slice S2: an orchestration is COMPOSED in the application layer from thread orchestration primitives, node-graph style, and driven green through the agent SDK against the real daemon; two reservation-route defects and one SDK type lie found by the drive and fixed in the same cut)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The composition, and where it lives.** ADR 0022 (rooms are not Hypervisor surfaces), ADR 0030 (a room is an application composition instantiated as a bounded System, never a second admission plane), ADR 0031 (execution composes the daemon's primitives) and ADR 0034 (the thread fork is the delegation primitive; subagents are its surface) together say the orchestration belongs to the application over primitives the platform already serves. | `packages/agent-sdk/src/orchestration.ts` — the `Orchestration` composer: `compose()` creates the coordinating thread (`POST /v1/threads`, the root node); `delegate()` spawns a subagent of it (`POST /v1/threads/:id/subagents`); `reserve()` admits an exact-head per-dimension reservation on the work-lifecycle plane, naming the last head the daemon handed back or the genesis head; `record()` admits a typed record under the bounded System through the S1 seam with the application's own `scope_ref` as `parent_scope_ref` and a content-derived idempotency key; `graph()` projects a node graph (system, thread, subagent, reservation, record nodes; coordinates/delegates/reserves/records/scopes edges; every node names the kernel or seam that owns its truth) from the daemon's own reads. Client methods `admitSystemRecord`, `listSystemRecords`, `getSystemRecord`, `admitWorkReservation` on `RuntimeSubstrateClient`; the module is exported from the SDK index and as the `./orchestration` subpath. The composer holds no store and no coordination object; a re-attached composer (`open(handle)`) remembers nothing but its handle. No name on the surface is an application's: the module and its graph speak primitives and graph words only (measured: zero `room`/`participant` tokens). | **Composed as ruled.** Application-layer only; the daemon gained no route, object or roster for it (asserted structurally by the gate against the router source). |
| **Driven proof.** `apps/hypervisor/scripts/verify-hypervisor-orchestration-composition.mjs` (`check:orchestration-composition`, drill `mutate:orchestration-composition`, CI matrix `orchestration-composition`, census id `orchestration-composition`) on the isolated daemon and the REAL wallet.network fixture, through the built SDK: the bounded System activated through its governed genesis; the coordinating thread served back by the kernel; two delegations as subagents bound by `parent_thread_id`, distinct, listed under the thread (both runs completed by the kernel with no model behind the plane); the FIRST reservation admitted against the genesis head; a sibling on genesis refused `work_reservation_head_moved`; the successor on the admitted head admitted; an over-bound claim refused 409 by a `work_reservation_*` code; a typed record admitted with its binding derived (this System, the composition's scope as parent, the payload root re-derived by the SDK and by the S1 gate's independent oracle); replay under the same content-derived key; a revision on the handed-back head; the chain and the list served; the graph rooted at the thread with 1+1+2+2+2 nodes and provenance on every node; restart with a re-attached composer re-deriving the same thread, subagents and records and a successor reservation naming the pre-restart head admitted while an older head is refused; structure. | **18/18** (run 3 on 2026-09-16; runs 1–2 found the defects below and my own two mistakes: a genesis builder typed from memory and a dimension outside the contract's closed vocabulary). SDK unit test `packages/agent-sdk/test/orchestration.test.mjs` 4/4: the SDK's slug and payload-root derivations agree with the S1 gate's independent oracle, the tail mirrors the daemon, the graph is order-independent, and the composer drives exactly the primitives' routes with exactly the bodies their handlers read. |
| **Finding 1 — a first reservation was unreachable through the daemon.** `handle_work_reservation_admit` read `None` as the head of an empty stream; the kernel refuses an empty `expected_ancestor_head` and compared the route's `None` as `""` against the caller's non-empty head, so every first claim was refused as "moved against `<genesis>`". The M04.10 gate (`verify-hypervisor-work-lifecycle-reservations.mjs`) is structural and never drove the route. | `work_lifecycle_routes.rs`: `RESERVATION_GENESIS_HEAD` (`sha256:000…0`, the machine plane's convention, a real value rather than an absence) is what an empty stream reports to the kernel's comparison; the substrate CAS still receives `None`. Unit test `a_first_reservation_names_the_genesis_head_and_its_successor_names_the_admitted_one`. | **FIXED in this cut.** |
| **Finding 2 — the reservation stream tail was never canonical.** The tail was spelled `reservations/<root>`; the slash is outside the substrate's canonical tail alphabet (alphanumerics, `-`, `_`, `.`), so Agentgres refused every claim as `CoordinatesNotCanonical("tail")` AFTER the kernel had planned it. Together with finding 1: no reservation had ever been admitted through the daemon. | `reservation_stream_tail` now spells `reservations.<root>`; the same unit test admits, refuses stale and admits the successor over the real substrate. | **FIXED in this cut.** |
| **Finding 3 — the SDK typed a subagent-list envelope the daemon never served.** `GET /v1/threads/:id/subagents` answers with the bare array of subagent records; the SDK's `RuntimeSubagentListResult` has always been `{object, count, subagents}`, no canon names the envelope, and every listing read as empty through the SDK. | `RuntimeSubstrateClient.listSubagents` normalizes the served array into the published envelope as the SDK's own read model; the wire is untouched (its other consumers were not measured and canon does not require the envelope). Documented in `api.md`. | **FIXED in the SDK; ruled R-173 (owner-reversible: serve the envelope from the daemon instead).** |
| **Vocabulary boundary observed, not crossed.** `attempt/v3` and `finding/v3` still bind to OutcomeRoom members through their invariants (`attempt.room.matches_admission` requires `system_binding.parent_scope_ref == outcome_room_ref`; a direct attempt has a null binding and cannot pass the seam). | The gate speaks only `work-frontier-item/v3`, which the S1 gate proved; the composer's `scope_ref` is opaque to the platform. | **Deferred to S4's vocabulary re-cut**, recorded so the boundary is not mistaken for coverage. |
| **Records.** `api.md`: the reservation route listed (it was absent from the work-lifecycle block) with the genesis head and canonical tail stated; the S1 section names the composer and the bare-array listing. `verifier-floors.v1.json`: `orchestration-composition` pinned at 18. CI: the S1 job's cloned step names corrected to what they run; the S2 job builds the daemon and the SDK. | This entry; private register R-172 (S2 landed) and R-173. | **Recorded.** |

### Journey verification XCIV (2026-09-16, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — R-172: the owner's structural correction lands as a program; M11.1's spine-based slices are discarded, and the first platform slice of the composition — the generic contract-typed application-record seam under a bounded System — is driven green on the real wallet fixture)

It is not a second admission plane"; the current room transition, head-reservation, receipt and root implementation is "migration input, not conforming evidence"), ADR 0031 (execution composes the five daemon primitives; "OutcomeRoom does not absorb the seam"), ADR 0034 (thread fork is THE delegation primitive; subagents are its surface), ADR 0036 (hosted participation native, discovery the cross-domain lane) and R-68 (the owner's earlier statement of the same rule; M04.7/M04.8/M12.4/ACC-9 to product track; M11.1 re-pointed to M04.4). R-171's measurement stands; its provisional design is superseded. | Generator, check and the golden oracle green over 318 registry rows. | Closed. |
| **S1 — the generic contract-typed record seam under a bounded System, with no application vocabulary in it.** `system_record_routes.rs` serves `POST /v1/hypervisor/autonomous-systems/:id/records` (admit), `GET …/records[?contract_id=]` (current heads) and `GET …/records/:contract/:object` (one chain). It validates by registered contract id, derives the `SystemScopedObjectBinding` (this System, the parent scope, the resolved principal, a payload root over the record with the binding absent), refuses by name a caller-authored binding, an identity the record does not carry as its own single `*_id` member, an unregistered contract, a registered contract whose shape carries no binding, an invalid record, a System that is not active, a stale or genesis-over-existing head and a reused idempotency key, recognizes an exact retry before any head is compared, and admits through `admit_owner_scoped_mutation` as `event_stream.system_record_admitted` — the shared owner-scoped write path, the same tenant scope discipline every family uses. The caller is resolved before the body is parsed. Its source names no room, participant, frontier or claim, which the gate asserts structurally. | 3 unit tests; `check:system-record-seam` 17/17 on the real wallet fixture (System genesis and activation through their governed routes; a `work-frontier-item/v3` record admitted, its binding re-derived offline; successor, stale head, replay, seven typed refusals, a second principal refused by scope, restart); drill 3/3; floor row 17. | Closed. |
| **CI run 5 (M09.7's push, 35078472314) classified; one red was mine.** R-18 (decentralized-cloud plane), R-21 (R-80: exactly three ioi-networking tests, 49/3), and "Product builds and browser smoke" — `check:model-route-authority`'s census of every mutation on the model-route surface found my `POST /model-routes/:id/assurance` answering 415 anonymously (the JSON extractor refused an empty body before identity), where every other mutation answers 401. Fixed in this push: the assurance and observation admits and the record seam's admit resolve the caller before parsing the body, and the assurance admit resolves identity before it looks the route up. | `check:model-route-authority` 43/43 locally after the fix. | Fixed; run 6 watches it. |

### Journey verification XCIII (2026-09-16, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — M09.7: a route's assurance class becomes a DERIVED fact over the daemon's own evidence, custody-proven only when a measured node's appraisal, owners, paths and preventable egress all hold, and the node plane's governed routes are driven live for the first time)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Canon owned the doctrine and the coverage shapes; the daemon held every piece of evidence and nothing that derived a class or produced node-profile coverage.** R-169 measured: doctrine.md § Core Doctrine and § Model-Weight Custody, README § 25, hypervisoros.md § Node Enforcement Profile / § EnforcementCoverageDeclaration / § Node Measurement Doctrine ("Measurement proves what was supposed to run. cTEE limits what the node is allowed to see"); no `contractual_privacy`/`workload_isolation`/`custody_proven_no_plaintext` vocabulary anywhere in canon; ACC-11 N3 typed ABSENT. The daemon: route custody (`compose_custody_admission`), the rights contract's provider-use and retention terms (M03.15), M09.8's isolation binding, the node-attestation plane with `derive_node_readiness`, and `enforcement_coverage_routes.rs` consuming node-profile declarations nothing produced (the M01.2 hand-off). | Two registered shapes and one core module, driven 39/39 against the isolated daemon and the real wallet fixture, drill 5/5. | Closed. |
| **Two registered shapes: a claim whose class is derived, and an observation that is observed, never promoted.** `RouteAssuranceClaimEnvelope` (`schema://ioi/components/model-router/route-assurance-claim/v1`, owner doctrine.md § Route Assurance Classes; registry 315; invariants re-derive `content_hash` under `ioi.model-router.route-assurance-claim-content-commitment-jcs-sha256.v1` and refuse a derived class the evidence does not support): per model route a family on the shared chain — `requested_class`, `effective_class` ∈ {`contractual_privacy`, `workload_isolation`, `confidential_compute_declared`, `custody_proven_no_plaintext`} or `unevidenced`, a typed `downgrade_reason`, the `declared_posture` recorded VERBATIM as the model-route plane serves it (the kernel serves six postures where the vocabulary names four — recorded, never promoted; the reconciliation is queued to the plane's owner), evidence by class (contractual: revision ref, terms hash, retention posture; isolation: binding ref and hash; custody: the committed receipt root, epoch, posture, the appraisal under separated roles with the relying party resolved, the owners, the observable-path table, the egress coverage ref and `egress_preventable`), `refusals`, `expires_at` = the appraisal's expiry. `NodeEnforcementProfileObservationEnvelope` (`…/daemon-runtime/node-enforcement-profile-observation/v1`, owner hypervisoros.md; registry 316): the physical enforcement owner's admitted observation per mechanism (mode, action classes, verification evidence, receipt contracts, privilege) with the measured-boot summary SERVER-RESOLVED from the node plane. | 13 golden fixtures; generator, check and the golden oracle green. | Closed. |
| **One core module composes the estate's machinery: two families, a producer, a pure appraisal, a derivation by rank, and twenty-two typed refusal codes.** `route_assurance_routes.rs`: observations admit as revisions bound to the resolved principal (an authored measured-boot summary, a mode outside the vocabulary, an active mechanism without verification evidence, an anonymous caller each refused by name); the PRODUCER derives one `EnforcementCoverageDeclaration` per class an observed mechanism names and admits them through the enforcement-coverage registry — `mediated`/`preventable`/`receipted` only under an active, verified, receipted mechanism, an audit-only mechanism observing and attributing only, a measured-boot receipt contributing `discovered`/`attributable` and naming the committed receipt `receipt://hypervisoros/boot-receipt/<root>`; `GET …/coverage?profile_ref=` serves what the registry resolves. `derive()` resolves the contract through the rights plane (unresolvable, route mismatch, not live, terms insufficient), the isolation binding through the WorkRun, and custody through `appraise_receipt` — a pure function over the committed receipt and the resolved inputs: roles missing or confused (attester, verifier, appraiser, relying party), nonce missing or replayed, appraisal indeterminate or failed, stale or unexpiring, endorsement withdrawn or missing, posture insufficient, measurement substituted (verified against a root the estate does not declare), node not measured, route custody remote, owners unnamed, egress not preventable; `route_custody_remote` and `owners_required` are the route's and the request's and are reported with or without a receipt; a route whose custody members are null is `route_custody_undeclared` before any admission. The class is the strongest the evidence supports; `expires_at` follows the appraisal; the daemon's clock refuses and never extends. | 6 unit tests over synthetic receipts; every refusal code observed LIVE in the gate. | Closed. |
| **THE FINDINGS: the node plane's governed routes had never been driven live, and two shared assumptions made them undrivable.** (1) The temporal profile was governed under a hard-coded `wallet://hypervisor/local-estate-owner` — a ref the wallet.network refuses as a principal by grammar — so no request could ever have admitted one; ruled: the estate owner is deployment configuration (`IOI_HYPERVISOR_ESTATE_OWNER_REF`, default retained, shared by the node and environment planes; canon says no request chooses it), the gate names a distinct estate operator and proves the node owner's grant refused with the challenge naming the configured owner. (2) The shared evidence builder re-derived every governed decision's policy and request hashes under the SYSTEM GENESIS context from `/system_id`, which neither a node-profile declaration nor an estate-governed environment effect carries (500 `system_lifecycle_artifact_invalid`): the node plane's two sites and the environment plane's one authorize under their own contexts and were never admissible live; ruled: `prepare_node_evidence_under(AuthorityDecisionBinding{policy_context, subject_ref})` owns the discipline, the old name is the system-genesis wrapper and its twenty callers are untouched. (3) The node plane checks neither the appraisal's expiry nor the evaluation's horizon against any clock — the derivation is the first consumer of `appraisal_expires_at`. (4) A route registered without custody serves null custody members: the plane's admission composes defaults it never writes back. Queued, not this unit's: a caller's invalid temporal profile answered 500; the posture vocabulary; ad78c3b5c naming M09.7's plane in the route-rights population ahead of its landing (CI run 4's Build and Test red is this program's own and resolves with this push). | Both plane fixes unit-tested and driven; the fixture (Rust + JS) gained the estate operator and the node plane's scope family. | Closed. |

Drill 5/5. | Run of record 39/39; drill 5/5. | Closed. |
| **Pins moved by EXPERIMENT; bindings; nonclaims.** Pins moved by experiment: distinct paths 940 → 945 (946 occurrences; five registrations, the observation and per-route assurance paths each carrying GET and POST in one registration), absence-worded assertions 116 → 117 (probe-resolving 17 held), handlers 1209 → 1216; ontology tokens 159912 → 161210 (+1298, split by EXPERIMENT: the entry with the new module +1286, the shared builder's split +11, the node plane +6, the environment plane −5), modules 126 → 127, opaque-initialiser 3640 → 3706 and foreign-qualified 5118 → 5146 (all the new module's), judged positions 282 and every writer bucket HELD on all five runs; residuals 135 held; admission-evidence H baseline 188 held. Floor row `custody-proven-private-routes` 39 (names sha `2dfea71819c02a62553aed21131fb36d80bbf72f2c56ca62dfb3994c6b3f077e`); ACC-11 N3 re-typed from ABSENT to the gate and its drill; CI M04 matrix entry `custody-proven-private-routes`. ### Journey verification XCII (2026-09-16, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — M03.16: an external-account connection becomes a registered single-use ceremony and a versioned binding that grants reachability and nothing else, fenced at the one gateway every brokered use crosses)

### Journey verification XC (2026-09-15, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — M10.2: Search, Judgment and Authority become principals bound per campaign, the substrate learns to delegate a read, and the archive keeps what Authority rejected)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The three trust rings existed as a table and a ladder, the daemon recorded the ladder's tier and enforced nothing, and Authority was nobody.** R-163 measured: canon names Search, Judgment and Authority as logical trust functions with a May/Must-not table (bounded-recursive-improvement.md § Search, Judgment, And Authority) and a cumulative `improvement_assurance_profile` ladder whose tiers are "checkable, not descriptive" (`local_lightweight`: separately identifiable under one accountable principal; `independent_review`: judgment and promotion under distinct principals); M10.1 registered the profile on the campaign contract (canonical-enums.md still called it "no substrate on current master" — corrected) and the daemon copied it nowhere and checked it against nothing; `POST …/improvement-proposals/:id/{approve,reject,apply}` took NO caller at all; a nomination disclosed no selection policy and no selector and its evidence refs were free strings; M10.4's `submitter_role` was a body claim, so `role_separation_violated` refused only a caller who SAID it was search; `GET …/candidates` was planned and asserted absent. | `internal register R-163`; `git show HEAD:crates/node/src/bin/hypervisor_daemon_routes/ioi_intelligence_routes.rs` (the three handlers with `State` and `AxumPath` only) | measured |
| **One registered binding makes the functions separately identifiable, and independence follows the declared tier checkably.** `ImprovementRoleBindingEnvelope` (`schema://ioi/foundations/objects/improvement-role-binding/v1`, owner bounded-improvement.md; invariants re-derive its `content_hash` and its family) binds, per campaign, which admitted deployment principals hold each function (`user://{principal_id}`; a label or an unadmitted principal is `principal_unresolvable`), COPIES the campaign's profile and DERIVES `independence`: overlap permitted at `local_lightweight` (`separately_identifiable`), `judgment ∩ authority = ∅` and `search ∩ judgment = ∅` required at `independent_review` (`distinct_principals`, else `role_independence_violated`), every higher tier failing closed (`assurance_profile_not_evidenced` — canon's own sentence, owners M10.8/M12.5). A campaign starts, pauses or stops only once bound (`role_bindings_required`) and only by Authority; the epoch is created, frozen, activated, challenged, closed, invalidated and its ledger moved only by Judgment; runs and results are admitted only by Judgment (a `target_owner` submitter must also hold Authority, which `independent_review` forbids in one principal); a nomination is Search's; approve, reject and apply of a campaign-bound proposal are Authority's and every reviewer is a resolved principal (an anonymous review is 401 before any record is read). The refusal is canon's word, `role_separation_violated`, now meaning the RESOLVED principal's binding. A nomination discloses `selection_policy_ref` (one of the contract's declared archive policies, else `selection_policy_undeclared`) and its accountable selector as the resolved caller (a body naming one is refused by name), and cites at least one evaluation result or exposure SPEND resolved under the active epoch (`nomination_evidence_required` / `nomination_evidence_unresolvable`) — a promotion attempted without the ledger's records is refused. | `improvement_campaign_routes.rs` § role bindings (`ROLE_BINDING`, `require_function`, `handle_role_binding_admit`); `evaluation_routes.rs` (the judgment checks); `ioi_intelligence_routes.rs` (`require_reviewer`); improvement-governance-gates.md § Search, judgment and authority separation (the seven codes beside, not inside, M10.1's entailed family) | landed |
| **THE SUBSTRATE FINDING, and the ruling it forced (R-164): request scopes are principal-bound, so no second principal could ever act on a campaign — the binding now DELEGATES reads.** The first driven run refused Judgment and Authority `request_resource_scope_required` on everything the campaign's creator held, because `authorize_request_resource_scope` keys on `scope.principal_ref` and M05.8's gate asserts exactly that isolation ("a SECOND REAL PRINCIPAL in the same tenant cannot read this view family"). A role binding without a way to read what it binds is prose. Ruled: `delegate_request_resource_scope` is a successor operation on the scope's own stream (`event_stream.request_resource_scope_delegated`, exact-head, idempotent union), admitted only by the scope's principal, honoured by `authorize_request_resource_scope`, `authorized_request_resource_refs` and a read-side `validate_scope_fields_for_read` — and NEVER by the write path, whose `validate_scope_fields` still keys on the principal alone. The binding admission is the delegating act (the campaign and the binding to every bound principal), and every epoch, ledger, run and result created under a bound campaign is delegated at creation. M05.8's isolation holds unchanged for everything nothing delegated. The second finding: a REFUSED genesis still pinned the epoch family's scope to the refused principal before the role check ran, so Search could squat a name Judgment then could not create — the role check now runs before any genesis bind at the epoch, run, result and binding seams. | `substrate_store.rs` (`RequestResourceScope::delegate_principal_refs`, `readable_by`, `delegate_request_resource_scope`); `mutation_event_foundation.rs` (`validate_scope_fields_for_read`; test `a_delegate_reads_and_never_writes`); the driven run's second and third logs | landed (R-164) |
| **The gate's actors are real, and the refusal is keyed on the binding rather than the operation.** `check:improvement-role-separation` mints three principals through the daemon's own routes in one organization — before any binding Search cannot even READ the campaign Authority created; after it, Search and Judgment read the campaign, the binding, the epoch and the ledger identically to Authority. Each planted mutation is refused and then DONE by the principal the binding names: Search creating, freezing, activating or challenging the epoch or reserving exposure (Judgment does it); Authority activating the epoch, reserving exposure or admitting a run (Judgment does it); Judgment nominating, approving, rejecting or applying (Search nominates, Authority decides). The same overlapping binding is refused at `independent_review` and accepted at `local_lightweight`; `protected_build` fails closed. A second nomination is REJECTED by Authority and stays in the derived archive beside the applied one with its selector, policy and spend entry, and no rank, order or promote member exists anywhere in the projection (asserted over every key of every entry). A restart reproduces the binding, the archive and the delegation, and Search still cannot close the epoch. Writing it found, beyond the two substrate findings: the proposal creator dropped the binding's new members; the campaign's lifecycle refusal must answer before the function refusal (a proposed campaign has no epochs, whoever asks); the fixtures a campaign resolves through their owners must be the creating principal's. | `check:improvement-role-separation` 69/69 (three real principals) + `mutate:` 4/4; the six runs of this leg (28 → 28 → 11 → 62 → 69 of 69) | landed |

### Journey verification LXXXIX (2026-09-15, MVP finish-line program, the 33→0 leg — M10.4: Evaluations becomes a governed plane whose evidence is real model invocations, whose verdicts carry a derived floor, and whose continuity report is derived with the incumbent route disabled in registry truth)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |

| **What writing the verifier found before commit, in order.** The harness smoke found two parser gaps: strace splits a non-blocking connect across an `<unfinished ...>` line carrying the address and a `resumed` line carrying the errno, so the first parse read every refused reach as `unfinished`; and the host resolver appends its search domains, so one lookup read as two names — both fixed (the resumed result attached to its pending attempt; variants folded onto the shortest name). The first drills run then found a REAL DEFECT in the read model: the model-route registry materialises the env-configured local route lazily from its own read handlers, so a fresh daemon's registry dir was empty and `external_model_provider` reported "0 routes read" — the seeded route that IS the deployment's model dependency was invisible to the projection that exists to name it. The fix composes the registry's own seed. The same run found the runner's synthetic-ledger expectations miscounting the resolver's own loopback connect. The first COMPLETE run then found the lane's own sequencing defect: the fault block sat after the journey's ordinary 120 s park loop, a WARM model completed the whole run inside that loop, the bridge was severed after completion, and the lane read a legitimately completed run (`done`, `ALPHA_JOURNEY.md` written, a success receipt) as if it were a fabricated success — the verifier's oracle was right to go red, and the lane was wrong about when "mid-run" is. Fixed by moving the lane before the park loop and triggering only while the bridge's connection count has grown past its pre-run baseline with a stream still OPEN; the runner now also requires at least one in-flight stream to have been destroyed. The second lane run severed mid-stream (one in-flight connection destroyed) and the deployment failed closed in one second — and two oracles were still wrong about the SHAPE of honesty: the trigger had fired on the daemon's own 300 ms reachability probe (a connection that is briefly active and carries no bytes), so the daemon refused at its execute gate (`no_model_route`) rather than the harness dying mid-request; and the refusal receipt reads `exit_status: failure` beside `exit_code: 0` and a typed error, which the fabricated-success filter had counted as a success because of the zero. Fixed: the trigger fires only once REQUEST BYTES have flowed past the pre-run baseline on an open connection (a probe carries none); a success claim is `exit_status: success` or a written file, never a zero exit code on a refusal; and post-sever readiness is read from the daemon's OWN route probe (`POST …/model-routes/mrt_local_default/probe` → `unreachable`) rather than from an environment read that carries a different readiness shape. The third lane run then found the harness's own blind spot: the bridge persisted its statistics only at connection open and close, so while the model stream was OPEN the byte counter on disk was the one from before the request — the byte-based trigger could never fire, and a warm model completed the run in sixty seconds. The bridge now persists on every data event, and the fourth run severed the request in flight. The lane's last red was the RUNNER's mirror of the fabricated-success rule, still keyed on a zero exit code; aligned with the journey's (a success claim is `exit_status: success` or a written file, never a zero exit on a refusal), and the mutation drill now plants a written file and a success receipt beside the done-with-no-error case. Four iterations of one negative lane, each red for a different reason, none of them the product's: that is what "write the driven verifier before believing it" costs and buys. | Runs recorded in `.artifacts/mvp-finish-line/standalone-conformance-*.json`; the drills 15/15 after the fixes; `mutate:standalone-conformance` 5/5 planted defects red. | **fixed before commit** |
