### Journey verification LXXXVI (2026-09-14/15, MVP finish-line program, the 36→0 leg — M04.11: the context objects become contracts and a lifecycle, and the four privacy dimensions the unit asks for turn out to belong to someone else)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The unit's check text names "purpose, audience, data class, redaction" on the lease, and four of those must NOT be built — the canon owner of the labels says so in as many words.** M04.11 asks for GoalRun-owned ContextCell/ContextLease/ContextHandoff revisions binding "subject, purpose, audience, data class, redaction, budget, expiry, revocation, predecessor/successor and receipt roots". Measured before writing: `execution-context-and-step-resolution.md`, the canonical owner of `InformationFlowLabel` and `DeclassificationApproval`, states that "`ContextCell`, memory, connector/tool, and model owners carry refs to these labels rather than **defining parallel taint or privacy objects**", and the label's registered v1 shape already owns "destination/data-class egress policy, purpose, retention". Beneath it, `policy_bound_data_view_revision_routes.rs` (v2, registered, BUILT) carries `purpose` + `purpose_binding_ref`, `data_classes`, `privacy_class`, `redaction`, `retention_and_hold`, `destination_and_egress` and `allowed_uses` as MATERIAL content-committed fields folded by SUBTRACTION (`fold_purpose` denies whatever the bound purpose does not support). So the lease binds exact `view://<family>/revision/<n>` refs and INHERITS those denials at resolution through that plane's own published reader, which already refuses a family head, the predecessor's `policy-bound-data-view://` spelling and a cross-tenant read BEFORE returning bytes. A lease that restated its own data class could claim one WIDER than the view it leases — the hole the unit exists to close — so restating them would have built the defect the unit is meant to prevent. Only `permitted_recipient_roles` is the lease's own (which cell ROLE may hold it, distinct from the view's `destination_and_egress`, which governs where DATA may go). Canon's shapes also turned out to live in `domains/ioi-ai/goal-run-execution.md`, not the `foundations/objects/*` the leg's own prompt assumed — and that location CONFIRMS the layer law, because these are the ioi.ai application's objects and the document's front matter declares itself their owner. | Canon extended in the owning document: `leased_refs` (exact revisions BESIDE the pattern fence, because a pattern is not a ref and a lease resolving through a moving head cannot reproduce a least-context result after restart), `information_flow_label_refs`, `permitted_recipient_roles`, `successor_of`/`predecessor_remains_valid`, `receipt_root`, the handoff's `non_grants` block in the domain's own idiom, and prose stating what is inherited and why. Three contracts registered under the estate's `applications/ioi-ai/` prefix (294 → 297; 22 fixtures; corpus 1437 → 1459). The ContextCell positive IS the producer's own output byte-for-byte from `build_implementer_context_cell`, so the contract is proven against what the daemon writes rather than a hand-made guess. | **Ruled and built (R-152).** The first draft of this slice minted `data_class`, `redaction_policy_ref` and a purpose vocabulary on the lease; the canon owner's front matter caught it, not a test. Recorded because the same trap is one grep away for anyone extending a lease-like object. |
| **A defect the contracts made visible the moment they existed: the daemon had been issuing context leases to a reusable HarnessProfile.** The activation lane wrote its lease as an inline literal on the goal-run record with `"issued_to": profile_ref`. Canon's rule sits in the paragraph directly beneath the shape — "A reusable HarnessProfile, AgentHarnessAdapter, Worker definition, or service definition is never a lease subject; `issued_to_ref` names only a concrete ContextCell or HarnessInvocation." Nothing could refuse it because nothing validated it: the exact drift an unregistered inline literal hides. Validating today's literal against the new contract enumerated the rest mechanically — ten members missing (`schema_version`, `work_subject_ref`, `issued_to_ref`, `authority_scope_refs`, `leased_refs`, `information_flow_label_refs`, `permitted_recipient_roles`, `successor_of`, `predecessor_remains_valid`, `receipt_root`) and two misnamed (`goal_ref`, `issued_to`). | `goal_run_context_routes.rs`: two families on seven routes under `/v1/goal-orchestration/goal-runs/:id/…`, every record validated against its REGISTERED contract before the write. Narrowing is SUBTRACTION and each widening refuses by its own name — `leased_refs`, `allowed_ref_patterns`, `authority_scope_refs`, `information_flow_label_refs` and `permitted_recipient_roles` may only shrink, denials may only GROW (a successor that forgets a denial widens reach by removal), and a ttl may be shortened but never extended or unbounded. A lease DECLARING an inherited dimension is refused by the request-field fence. The handoff's `non_grants` is constant by construction, re-asserted on the decision successor because that is the moment the claim matters most; acceptance is a CANDIDATE under the receiver's policy that copies no lease. Then the producer: the activation lane now calls `admit_activation_context_lease`, binding the implementer's own CELL, under a DERIVED idempotency key so a replayed creation replays the same lease — and if the lease does not admit, GoalRun creation FAILS, because a run reporting success while its context lease is absent claims a bounded context it never obtained. 8 module tests, each pinning a refusal; `check:m4-goalrun-activation-plane` 52/52 on an isolated daemon including its restart-replay clause. | **Fixed at the producer, not only refused at the new routes.** Composed rather than rebuilt: the shared owner-scoped mutation spine owns genesis-versus-successor and the CAS (`expected_head: None` admits, `Some(head)` succeeds it), which is why EVERY ontology writer bucket held; `sealed()` was promoted to `pub(crate)` rather than copied so `receipt_root` keeps one implementation. |
| **`check:architecture-contracts` is DRIFT-ONLY, and 22 negative fixtures would have been decorative without noticing.** The estate's recorded trap says the golden fixture bar is generated from the registry and therefore moves with your own additions. Tested directly: a defect was planted that made `negative-unknown-field.json` VALIDATE, the projections were regenerated around it, and the JS gate reported "projections are up to date" at exit 0. The real oracle is the GENERATED RUST TEST `golden_fixtures_match_generated_rust_contracts`, which asserts each fixture against the registry's own positive/negative classification rather than re-running ajv — it failed BY NAME (`expected_schema_accept=false schema_result=Ok(())`). A third oracle nobody had pointed at also covers this work: `live_ajv_differential_corpus_matches_rust_validator_and_deserializer`, so the schemas are proven to behave identically in ajv and in the generated Rust validator. | Restored by copy (git checkout restores nothing for an untracked path), digest re-verified, regenerated; `cargo test -p ioi-types --lib` 502/502. | **Recorded as a method, not just a result:** after adding fixtures, a green `check:architecture-contracts` is NOT evidence — run the cargo test. |
| **Pins moved by EXPERIMENT rather than estimate, twice.** Routes 879 → 886 and registered handlers 1138 → 1145 (seven each — for once they move by the same amount, because every one of the seven paths carries exactly one method; workspace 40 and candidates 47 held). Ontology: modules 121 → 122, tokens 152781 → 153327 → 153360, opaque-initialiser 2843 → 2854, foreign-qualified 4708 → 4739. The token splits were MEASURED: removing the module declaration and re-running the census read 121 modules / 152788 tokens with both buckets exactly at their pins, so +539 tokens were the module, +7 the route registrations, and both bucket deltas entirely the module's; then for the activation-lane slice, reverting `goalrun_routes.rs` alone read 153376, so the new admission function is +49 and the lane itself is −16 — the inline literal removed was LARGER than the call that replaced it, which a net +33 would have understated on both sides. | Both census verifiers green after each re-pin (12/12 with 3/3 mutations, 19/19, 27/27); the entry point and the activation lane were each restored by copy and digest-verified. The pre-push sweep caught the second token move that the module tests and the activation gate could not: 17/19 (the census and its battery, the battery red only because the census was), 19/19 after the re-pin. | **Re-pinned in the same commits as the code.** |
| **The verifier closed the unit, and driving a real daemon found three defects eight module tests could not.** `check:context-lease-handoff-lifecycle` drives an ISOLATED daemon through create → narrow → revoke → resolve → hand off → accept → reject → restart, with the receipt_root RE-DERIVED in JavaScript as canon defines it rather than read back from the record that carries it. Writing it exposed three bugs in the slices above, every one of them invisible to the module's own unit tests because those tests never called the shared mutation spine: `genesis=true` was passed for EVERY successor, so the spine refused every narrow, revoke, accept and reject outright (`mutation_genesis_expected_head_present`); the narrow loop copied the spine's own body fields (`owner_ref`, `idempotency_key`) into the record, which then violated its own closed contract; and both history readers took the FIRST matching entry rather than the maximum-`seq` one, so once a narrowing existed every later successor compared against a stale head and lost the CAS (`event_stream_expected_head_conflict`). That is ledger LXXXIV's lesson in its own terms: a unit test that seeds proves nothing about the producer. | 43/43 on the gate, 4/4 on its drill (the receipt-root oracle rejects a CHANGED and a REMOVED field; the byte-identity comparison goes red when a real record is written, so its green is not two empty sets agreeing; a narrowing that SHRINKS is still admitted, so the widening refusals are not a blanket refusal of every successor). Floor-pinned at 43 with its names digest and CI-bound in the `m4_plane_verifiers` matrix beside the activation gate that MINTS a lease — the unit's check text runs both, because a unit whose check only drove its own routes would prove nothing about its producer. The MCP half of the unit's matrix is a TYPED ABSENCE the gate ASSERTS rather than omits: no MCP tool reaches the context families, so the native refusals are the whole enforced surface and the matrix arrives with M01.10/M01.11. | **M04.11 CLOSED: check text set, `--release-readiness` 35 → 34.** ACC-5 clause 9 now composes the gate and N3 is proven by it; the remaining nonclaims (handoffs still inline, expiry by status rather than by a sweep, the label plane's own axes evaluated by their owner) are on the work item rather than implied away. |

### Journey verification LXXXV (2026-09-14, MVP finish-line program, leg 1 of the 36→0 prompt — ACC-R's orchestrator authored FIRST so every closed unit is composed as it lands, and authoring it made the manifest's check texts executable, which is how two commands the release would have skipped were found)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The release rehearsal is an orchestrator over the program's own accounting, not a fifteenth clause table, and it was built at the START of the finish line rather than the end.** `scripts/check-release-rehearsal.mjs` derives all 152 legs from the private manifest through `scripts/lib/release-plan.mjs` — the SAME parser `scripts/implementation-program.mjs --check` now enforces on every check text — so the release set cannot drift from the set the tool counts, and a `preflight:plan-agreement` leg asserts precisely that (the plan's UNRESOLVED legs are the tool's blocker list, or the run is red). What it adds beyond the journeys it runs: composition under ONE basis re-read after every leg; independent accounting (121 mvp_required unit checks, 4 declared scheduled checks and the 15 journey gates ACC-R depends on, in dependency order, each NAMED even when a journey runs the same command); the verifier-family floor ONCE over the UNION of every leg's runtime census (R-145) with per-verifier contributors recorded and a source-digest disagreement reported as a CONFLICT rather than averaged; the five lifecycle legs this gate's acceptance names; and a plan digest re-derived from the manifest at the end, so an evidence bundle cannot claim a plan the tree no longer holds. Evidence is written after EVERY leg (`complete: false` until the verdict), so an interrupted multi-hour rehearsal still leaves the record of what it had proven. | Self-drill 11/11 (`--mutation`, plants nothing): a planted red leg FAILs; a child's exit 2 is NOT-YET, neither a pass nor a failure; an unresolved, owed or skipped leg holds the verdict at PARTIAL; only an all-green set reads PASS; no duplicate leg id; every leg follows its dependencies; a manifest edited mid-run moves the plan digest; two censuses for one verifier at different source digests are a conflict; a census with no floor row is named as outside the closed world. The executor itself proven on REAL legs rather than only in drill: `unit:M01.1` green in 11 s, `unit:M03.1` green in 20 s (23/23 mutation drills), `unit:M03.2` SHARED against M03.1's identical command, `unit:M08.2` deferred and then resolved against the union floors leg, and the floors gate reading the union directory this run built. `--phase preflight` on a dirty tree FAILs on `preflight:basis` with the two offending paths named (exit 1). | **Authored — and PARTIAL by construction.** ACC-R's check text is set, so `--release-readiness` reports **36 → 35**: 34 unit checks + the M09.10 scheduled qualification. Every one of them is printed UNRESOLVED by name on every run, and the rehearsal cannot read green while one remains. Its first PASS is possible only when the last of them lands. |
| **Making the check texts executable found two commands the release would have reported as executed without executing: the manifest's prose was carrying part of the bar.** Of the 102 authored rows in ACC-R's subject set, 93 already separated the exact command from its prose with an em dash; eleven ran prose straight on from the command — and inside that prose **M09.4 named `npm run check:backup-restore --workspace=@ioi/hypervisor-app` ("alongside … for the managed lane end to end, 76 assertions, CI-gated") and M09.5 named the same command ("beside … for the managed lane")**. Any extractor that stopped at the first command would have dropped them silently while still reporting each unit as executed. **M13.9** likewise carried `IOI_ALPHA_MODEL_ROUTE=remote` only in prose — an environment its command cannot run without. Ruled (R-150): the grammar is `<command>[ — <prose>]`, a bare token is an argument only as the value of the valueless flag before it (which is what separates `--profile procedural` from `proves the SEAM`), and the accounting tool REFUSES a row it cannot parse rather than guessing. The eleven rows were normalized, M09.4/M09.5 gained their second command with `&&`, M13.9 gained its env prefix. **M09.3 was deliberately left alone**: its own prose says `check:env-lease-authority` "stays the cross-consumer port preview fence's subcheck and is not restated here", so promoting it would have widened the unit's bar by misreading it. | `node scripts/implementation-program.mjs --check` green with the shared parser wired in (15 modules, 166 build units, 22 gates); 0 malformed rows across ALL 166 units, measured before and after; `M15.1`'s space-form `--workspace @ioi/hypervisor-app` parses (the grammar admits `@`-led workspace values). | **Enforced, not just fixed.** The grammar lives in one module used by both the accounting tool and the orchestrator, so a tolerant second extractor cannot reappear in the runner — the failure mode this finding is an instance of. |
| **Where the orchestrator lives was an open question the previous leg deferred, and the estate's own `.gitignore` had already answered it.** The 2026-09-12 leg-5 row read: "the tool and manifest are gitignored, so the orchestrator lives beside them (private) and a tracked npm script cannot point at it; the ruling on where it lives is leg 5's first act". Measured: `.gitignore` already carries `/scripts/check-release-readiness-scope.mjs` with the reason — "Its verifier reads the same private guide and asserts over nothing else, so it travels with it rather than shipping a tracked check that is BLOCKED on every clone" — that script has no npm script anywhere, and `M06.6`'s own check text is already `node scripts/implementation-program.mjs --check`. RULED (R-150): the orchestrator and its plan module are PRIVATE, travel with the guide, and are invoked with `node`; the three npm scripts added earlier in the leg were reverted. The distinction that decides it: the 13 tracked ACC journey runners work on a clean clone because their clause tables live in their own source, while this orchestrator READS the private manifest as its subject and cannot. With the manifest absent it refuses typed (`BLOCKED`, exit 2) — an orchestrator that derived an empty plan would read PASS over nothing. | The only TRACKED change in this leg is the `.gitignore` rule and its reason. `check:work-items` 64 records / 339 anchors green, unchanged: no work-item record was added, because rule 12 requires every `code_anchors[]` entry to name a file that EXISTS, and an anchor into a gitignored path is green locally and red in CI — the private register (R-150), this ledger row and the journey document are this leg's record. | **Ruled, owner-reversible.** Recorded here rather than left as a private-only decision, because it fixes where every future private program gate lives. |
| **The residuals gate counted a REGISTERED route as an unserved documented endpoint, and the same defect has been over-counting by three since the pin was taken.** CI 34907977656 (Build and Test) read 169 documented daemon endpoints against the exact pin of 168, and the 169th was `GET /v1/hypervisor/work-projection?view=…[&q=…]` — documented by M08.9 in `api.md` the same day, and a route `hypervisor-daemon.rs` registers. `check:canon-document-residuals` captures a fenced path with `[^\s#]+` and compares it LITERALLY, query string included, to the router's registered paths, so a served endpoint documented with its query reads as unannotated. Measured over all tracked canon in the two policed namespaces: exactly FOUR fenced endpoint lines carry a query string and every base path is registered — api.md:218 work-projection, api.md:1073 `core-taxonomy?schema=…`, api.md:1404 `preferences?org_ref=…`, api.md:3083 `autonomous-systems?system_id=…` — so the pin's true population is **165** and the other three were inside the 168 since it was taken. | `e8233b109` fixes the red by the doc-side alternative (the fence now names the bare registered path; the view/q parameters are described in prose), returning the measured count to 168 with the gate and its planted-defect step green. The extractor defect itself is RECORDED, not fixed: its direction is fail-safe (it over-counts violations, so it can only be stricter than its claim, never permit an unserved documented endpoint), and a second edit to the same gate on top of that commit is a collision, not a repair. | **Fixed for the red; the defect recorded for `M08.6` with its one-line repair and the three citations that must move with it** (`ENDPOINT_RATCHET`, the work-item record's ratchet paragraph, and the M08.6 ledger row) — the pin moves 168 → 165 in the same commit as the fix, which is the closure test that gate already states about itself. Two sessions were working this checkout; the diagnosis was made independently on both sides and the committed fix verified rather than taken on report. |

### Journey verification LXXXIV (2026-09-14, MVP finish-line program, leg 3 — a regression M07.5 made reachable: every transport charge had been landing as a typed gap since c89159437, and CI found it on the pushed commits)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Order, second lesson: the ledger reads the invocation through the transport's own reader, and the transport wrote that projection AFTER the join.** M07.5 made the usage append derive its metering dimensions by reading the cited `model-invocation://` receipt through `provider_transport::admitted_invocation` (the plane's own reader — correct), and its tests seeded that projection before appending (also correct, and exactly why they were green). In the transport's real `handle_invoke`, the join ran after `admit_owner_scoped_write` but BEFORE `persist_record` wrote the projection the reader reads, so every transport-originated charge since c89159437 refused `economics_receipt_unresolved` and landed as the typed gap `economics_join_refused` — the invocation record said so, the ledger had no row, and nothing was red locally because M07.5's population gate never drives the transport. CI run 34893989439 (the M08.10 D / R-144 / M07.5 push) read it on the durable ledger: `check:model-router-decisions` "the LEDGER ROW carries the accumulated quantity … row undefined vs 9410" (37/38); reproduced locally 37/38 before the fix. | Fixed by order, not by a pin or a nonclaim: the projection is written first with a truthful `economics_join_pending` marker (a crash between the two writes leaves a record that SAYS the join had not run, never a silent absence), the join runs, and its outcome replaces the marker in the same request; a failed rewrite refuses by name. `cargo test … economics_tests` 13/13, `provider_transport` 1/1 (the transport's pure test) — the order itself has no unit harness, which is the remainder below; `check:model-router-decisions` 38/38 after the fix (37/38 before, locally and in CI); `check:provider-transport` 31/31. | **Fixed before this push.** No nonclaim was written for it (the rule: a regression your cut makes reachable is fenced in the same program, never carried). The fence that caught it is the CI-bound router verifier; M07.5's own bar still does not drive the transport, and that is recorded on the work item as the remainder it is. |
| **Pins.** Ontology tokens 152770 → 152781 (+11, all provider_transport.rs 638 → 649: the pending marker's four members and the second persistence refusal's code and message); foreign-qualified 4707 → 4708 (one more `StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` in provider_transport.rs, 3 → 4, the second persistence refusal); opaque-initialiser 2843 held; every writer bucket held: the projection is written twice through ONE `persist_record` call site (a closure invoked before and after the join); a second literal call site had moved the non-ODK literal writer bucket 254 → 255 on the first cut of the fix and was collapsed rather than re-pinned. Route and handler counts unchanged (879 / 1138). | `check:ontology-admission-census` 27/27 after the re-pin; `check:named-gap-truth` 12/12; environment-owner-source green. | **Re-pinned in the same commit as the fix.** |

One recorded divergence: the Session plane's admitted identity is `session:<id>` where canon names `session://`; the row carries the owner's identity verbatim and the contract accepts both until the plane converges (canon note). | `check:systems-work-projections` 21/21 against an isolated daemon + serve lane: the two routes exist; an empty Work projection is typed (zero rows, six zero counts, three typed-absence families, migration and rebuild stated); the Work subject registry is said on every answer (projected exactly `session` + `automation_run`, contributed exactly `goal_run` + `outcome_room` with their seams, readers exactly the two, no `/work/goals` or `/work/rooms` anywhere in the body); one admitted Session becomes exactly one registered row (owner identity verbatim, deep link `/work/sessions/{id}`, facets derived from `lifecycle_state`); counts and recents do not move under search; a governance approval becomes one review FACET that points; unknown view / non-canonical subject / kind disagreement refuse by name; the owners' record directories are byte-identical across every read; an anonymous caller sees none of the principal's rows; the Systems projection answers under policy with its registered row contract and `honest_empty`; a daemon restart re-derives the same rows; canonical `/work`, `/work/sessions`, `/work/reviews` and `/systems` render the projections under their ownership headers. `cargo test -p ioi-node --bin hypervisor-daemon work_projection` 5/5. Two earlier runs were red on the gate's own construction and are recorded here: run 1, the App's `/__ioi/work-projection/<view>` handler read a block-scoped URL and 500ed, so the gate timed out on `/work`; run 2, the nonmutation fingerprint was taken before the gate's own approval write and read that write as a mutation. Neither touched the daemon. Run 3 was 20/20 over the first cut. Runs 4 and 5 over the registry cut were 20/21: the new registry assertion failed on its own wording (its regex demanded the literal "application-contributed view" while the daemon said "contributed Work / Goals view"; the daemon's text was aligned to canon's phrase and the regex to canon's exact wording). Run 6 is 21/21 and is the census the floor row pins (names sha256 `950ea2a9…`). | **Built.** Floor row `systems-work-projections` (21, names sha256 `950ea2a9…`, 40 verifiers) and a ci.yml step in the product_surfaces job beside packages-journey; `check:verifier-floors` over the emitted census: the new row clean, the other 39 `census_missing` as R-145 expects. ACC-10 clause 4 composes the gate (self-drill green); the absence is gone. Ledger rows 91 and 92 move from "not started" / "compatibility only" to **partial** with the exact remainder named; `api.md` documents the route. |
| **The owner asked why Hypervisor Work enumerated goal runs and rooms, and canon had already answered: the first cut over-reached at the seam, and the Work subject registry now closes ADR 0022's open untangling.** term-boundaries.md § *Which layer owns which*: "Goal runs and rooms are not Hypervisor surfaces… the substrate's own surfaces speak session vocabulary" and ioi.ai "dogfoods Hypervisor rather than receiving privileged substrate semantics"; ADR 0022 Decision 1: Work "may include application-contributed subjects" (contributed, not enumerated); the bounded-alpha profile: "no step here is a room, a goal object, or an ioi.ai account"; and ADR 0022 § Named Untanglings still carried "Work workspace subject registry — which subjects are core vs application-contributed" as an open working_ruling owned by core-clients-surfaces.md. The first cut had core reading goal-run and room records through two readers published for the purpose and minting `/work/goals/…` and `/work/rooms/…` detail routes — privileged substrate semantics for one application. ### Journey verification LXXIV (2026-09-14, MVP finish-line program, leg 3 — M09.11: the conformance gate, and the non-deterministic head it found on its first run)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The gate drives a REAL daemon over its real route with two asymmetric reference backends.** Eleven contracts registered before any surface; the sixteen-verb vocabulary closed and agreeing between contract and kernel; the portable subset running on the hosted reference; an extension cell running only where declared while the other refuses with the declaration's own `reason_code`; a drifted declaration refusing before effect; a stale head and a replayed key refusing rather than taking effect twice; an unconfirmable completion recorded as ambiguous advancing neither generation; and a non-simulated backend admitted but NOT executed. | `check:machine-lifecycle-backend-conformance` 20/20 | authored; blockers 57 -> 56 |
| **It found a real defect on its first run, and the defect was not the one the failures pointed at.** The run came back 17/20 and all three failures were the GATE's own bug — it tracked the head itself with `.at(-1)` over a record listing. Chasing that found the daemon doing the same thing: `observed_head_for` took `.last()` over `read_record_dir`, which iterates `std::fs::read_dir` with **no ordering**, so the workload head was whichever record the filesystem happened to return last. **A caller could be refused as stale for quoting the head it had just been given.** | private register R-130 | found by the gate before the gate was registered |
| **The fix is the estate's own fork rule rather than a sort or a counter.** The head is the hash NO successor cites — the same derivation `route_binding_head` already uses — which is deterministic without a clock, a sequence field or a sorted read. Two uncited heads is a FORK, refused by name (`machine_head_forked`) rather than resolved by picking a branch, because picking one would be choosing which history is real. | a route test constructs the two-record chain and then the fork | fixed, with the rule named |
| **The gate now asks the daemon for the head instead of keeping its own copy.** A gate that keeps its own bookkeeping is asserting its own bookkeeping. | `currentHead()` walks the same fork rule over the route's own projection | corrected |
| **What the gate says it does NOT claim, on every run.** Attachments, console scope, crash/restart reconstruction and cleanup are ACC-20 clauses 6 and 7 and have no surfaces yet — there is one route here, not a machine plane. And profile qualification is a separate claim this evidence may never make: both reference backends declare `evidence_mode: simulated`, and the executor refuses to answer for anything else. | the gate's closing lines | honest partial |

### Journey verification LXXIII (2026-09-14, MVP finish-line program, leg 3 — M09.11: the kernel was reading a capability declaration that does not exist)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|

| **The fix validates the declaration against its registered contract BEFORE reading a field from it.** A shape that drifts away from the contract now refuses rather than silently reading nothing, which is the property the first cut lacked entirely. | `a_declaration_that_is_not_a_valid_capability_declaration_is_not_resolved` pins the exact defect | fenced |
| **The drift check got better by using the estate's own field.** The operation's pinned `capability_declaration_hash` is compared to the declaration's own `declaration_hash`, not to a hash this kernel recomputes — a republished declaration carries a new one by construction, so drift is detected by the declaration's own accounting rather than by a convention invented here. | `a_declaration_that_drifted_under_the_operation_refuses_before_effect` now republishes with a new hash | corrected |
| **The registered shape is plainly better than the invented one.** `unsupported_operations[].reason_code` is exactly canon's "exact typed reason from the current capability declaration"; my `unsupported_reason` was a worse copy of a field that already existed. And a verb named in NEITHER list still refuses, now with `operation_absent_from_declaration` so the two cases are distinguishable. | the unsupported test asserts both the declared reason and the absent-from-declaration reason | built |
| **The lesson, from the direction it actually bit.** I applied "grep before minting a field name" rigorously to the nine contracts I AUTHORED, and then invented a shape for the tenth thing I CONSUMED. Consuming a shape is minting one if you do not check first. | this row | recorded |

### Journey verification LXXII (2026-09-14, MVP finish-line program, leg 3 — M09.11: the machine-operation admission kernel, and a gate deliberately not authored)

|---|---|---|
| **M09.11's runtime is greenfield, measured before writing any of it.** Nothing in the daemon serves `VirtualMachineWorkload` or the virtual-machine state payload — not a route, not a handler, not a backend. ### Journey verification LXXI (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 3 — M09.11: the nine unspecified machine contracts are ruled into canon, then registered)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The nine were ruled into canon BEFORE registration, which is what made registering them legitimate.** Canon gained a subsection specifying each member's fields. Not one field is a free choice: every one answers a clause of the governed-machine journey, and where a clause does not reach, nothing was invented — `durability_boundary` and `observation_boundary` stayed refs on the operation for exactly that reason. | `check:architecture-contracts` green; all 11 in the registry | canon first, then registration |
| **Four obligations recur because the journey states them once and means them everywhere.** Desired and observed never collapse — a host carries declared AND observed capacity, a snapshot records both generations, because one taken mid-transition is honest only if it says so. Capability truth is current and explicit — ref AND hash wherever a backend decides possibility, since a ref alone cannot detect the drifted cell that must refuse before effect. Bounded things bind five axes: machine, principal, environment, scope, epoch. And backend-native identifiers are evidence, never identity. | the specification subsection states each obligation once, then applies it | derived, not invented |
| **Three rulings worth naming.** A device assignment carries its HOST, because passthrough supply is finite and physical and an assignment knowing only its machine cannot answer whether a sibling may hold the same device — which is the sibling-machine access clause 6 refuses. A console session carries a mandatory expiry and is held as a capability lease, because a session that ends only when someone closes it is ambient access with a polite name. A maintenance plan NAMES its affected machines rather than deriving them when work begins, because the derived set is not the set the operator approved. | private register R-124 | ruled, owner-reversible |
| **The seven typed-outcome invariants are proven wired by construction, not by a second mutation run.** Each `negative-untyped-outcome` fixture is SCHEMA-VALID — the schemas admit a null reason — so it can only be rejected by its invariant. The oracle went green at 1396 fixtures with all seven declared `expected_failure: invariant`, which is only possible if all seven rules loaded and fired. | fixture oracle 14/14 at the 1396-fixture bar | verified without redundant cost |
| **The generator refused two defects on the way in, and both were mine.** Integer members must declare a bounded domain, so six capacity fields with only a minimum were rejected until they carried a maximum. And a regex over-escape had turned `\\S` into a literal backslash in nine schemas — caught by testing the compiled pattern against a real ref and a value containing a space, not by reading it. | `generate:architecture-contracts` fails closed on both | fixed before landing |
| **Registration is not implementation, and canon now says so in its own voice.** Nothing serves these contracts, no backend declares against them, and the VM state payload and capability declaration remain prerequisites rather than evidence of lifecycle conformance. What registration buys is the thing ACC-20 clause 1 asks for first — contracts before surfaces — and what it does not buy is any claim over the surfaces. | the status paragraph rewritten in the same cut | claimed exactly |

### Journey verification LXX (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 3 — M09.11 opens: canon specifies two of eleven machine contracts, and only those two are registered)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **M09.11 is not "register eleven specified contracts", and measuring that first changed the unit's shape.** Canon's machine-control family names eleven, and nine of them — Host, Image, VolumeAttachment, NetworkAttachment, DeviceAssignment, ConsoleSession, Snapshot, MigrationPlan, HostMaintenancePlan — appear **exactly once in all of `docs/architecture`**, in the list itself, with no fields anywhere. Only `HypervisorMachineOperation` and its receipt carry prose bindings exact enough to author from. Canon also states the family's own status outright: "target canon and is not yet registered or implemented as one complete production path". So the nine need their fields ruled into canon BEFORE registration — a schema whose fields exist in no canon makes the registry the specification, which inverts this estate's ordering. | private register R-121, R-122 | ruled; two registered, nine deferred behind canon |
| **The operation verb is a closed enum because canon says a backend alias is never a canonical verb.** Sixteen members, exactly canon's minimum lifecycle vocabulary. A backend that calls reboot `restart` widens nothing; extension is expressed by the capability matrix declaring a cell supported. The negative fixture is precisely that: `operation: "restart"`. | `check:architecture-contracts` green; 6 positive and 6 negative fixtures | built from canon |
| **The capability declaration is bound by ref AND hash, because "current" is not checkable from a ref.** ACC-20 clause 4 requires unsupported, stale, unknown and drifted cells to refuse BEFORE effect, and a drifted cell is exactly what a ref alone cannot detect — the declaration can move under the operation between admission and effect. | the negative fixture removes the hash and must be refused | fenced |
| **The receipt's result vocabulary has THREE members, and that is the design.** `ambiguous` sits beside `succeeded` and `refused` because an external completion the daemon could not confirm is neither, and forcing it into either would invent the answer — the same thing the estate's reconciler doctrine says from the other side. Four generations, not two, because desired and observed never collapse: the gap between `observed_generation_after` and `desired_generation_after` is how a caller learns an operation is admitted and has not yet landed. | 4 positive fixtures including admitted-not-yet-landed and refused-before-effect | built |
| **One portable invariant keeps the three-member vocabulary honest, and it is MUTATION-VERIFIED rather than trusted.** Without it `ambiguous` degrades into "something happened" with no obligation to say what — which is worse than the two-member vocabulary it replaced, because it looks like more information while carrying less. The corpus count moved to the generated 1364-fixture bar, which proves nothing on its own: **that bar is regenerated from the registry and moved with my own additions.** So the defect was planted — a fixture declared negative was given a reason, satisfying its invariant — and the oracle went red naming it exactly. This estate has already shipped a fixture oracle where two of eleven operators were wrong and both failed GREEN. | private register R-123 | the rule is proven to run |
| **Two conventions caught by grepping before minting, both of which I had already got wrong in the draft.** Invariants do not live inline in the schema: they live in `schemas/invariants/*.invariants.json`, are referenced from the registry's `cross_field_invariant_refs`, and use `$.`-prefixed paths with the parameter names `path`/`when_path`/`values` — not the `field`/`selector_field`/`selector_values` I had invented from the operator's name. And the estate stores `idempotency_key_hash`, never the raw key, exactly as `connector-mapping.v2` already does. | the engine's own reader in `generate-architecture-contracts.mjs`, and an existing invariants file | corrected before landing |

### Journey verification LXIX (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 4 — M13.10 slice C: the harness runs in the guest, and the guest's work comes back through quarantine)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A LANE, not a flag, and the measurement is what decided that.** `run_host_spawn_lane` is interactive — it pipes stdin and feeds the harness one task per line while streaming stdout back. The guest agent's `E` verb is one command in, one result out, with no streaming direction and no second write, and that agent is pinned and hash-verified. Pointing the interactive lane at a VM would have meant extending a pinned protocol or quietly dropping the streaming half. The guest lane runs one-shot and says so in its own name. | private register R-115; `check:microvm-model-broker` 56/56 | built as a lane |
| **A microvm venue with no live guest FAILS; it does not fall back to the host.** The venue is resolved from the environment's own substrate, so a run that silently moved to the host would make the receipt's venue field a lie in the one direction that matters — claiming isolation that did not happen. The gate asserts both the failure path and the ABSENCE of a fallback. | `guest_lane_no_live_microvm` | fail-closed |
| **The guest's work returns through QUARANTINE, and this is the half a lane swap would have missed.** The session's diff reads the HOST workspace, which a guest run never touches — so a naive swap produces a green run truthfully reporting zero changed files, a partial reading as a pass in the most literal way available. The export is validated for path, type, member count and total size before anything lands, lands in quarantine, and is copied in only after a run that SUCCEEDED, so a failed harness never writes a half-finished tree over the operator's. | private register R-120; live probe writes and edits files in a real guest and reads them back from quarantine | built |
| **The quarantine walk does not follow symlinks.** `untar_into` refuses them in the archive; following one a step later would undo that refusal from the other side. | the gate asserts the `is_symlink` skip | fenced in the same cut |
| **The merged guest stream is labelled `combined`, not `stdout`.** The guest agent returns ONE merged stream; calling half of it stdout would invent a distinction the wire never made. Small, and exactly the kind of thing that becomes a false record later. | the gate asserts the label and the absence of the other one | named honestly |
| **What is left is one live run, and it is a credential away rather than a build away.** The alpha journey under `IOI_ALPHA_EXECUTION_VENUE=microvm` needs a model endpoint and a wallet fixture. Every mechanism it exercises is now proven on live boots: the channel carries bytes verbatim with zero network devices, and the workspace round-trips through quarantine. A missing credential blocks that RUN, not this unit. | the gate's closing line states exactly this, conditional on what it ran | scheduled-outstanding, named |

### Journey verification LXVIII (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 4 — M13.10: the live boot answered the last unknown, and found a defect while doing it)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The guest-initiated direction is PROVEN, not deferred.** Slices A and B both wrote that cloud-hypervisor's `<sock_path>_<port>` convention "cannot be proven without a live boot" and scheduled it as outstanding. It has now been proven: three consecutive green runs on a real cloud-hypervisor/KVM guest, 1.21s each. The probe sends a byte string with NO protocol at all and requires it back verbatim on the host, so a handshake, framing or error page inserted by any party would fail the comparison and print what arrived. Nothing was inserted. | `check:microvm-model-broker` 48/48, the last of which boots a guest | measured — the unknown is closed |
| **The live run immediately found a defect that no reading would have: the guest's loopback interface is DOWN.** The first probe failed `nc: can't connect to remote host (127.0.0.1): Network is unreachable` while the diagnostic reported `proxy=up` — the proxy had BOUND 127.0.0.1 and the guest still could not reach it. The initramfs `/init` mounts, insmods the vsock chain and execs the agent; it never raises `lo`, and never had to, because every earlier channel in this estate is vsock and needs no interface. **Had this stayed scheduled, the defect would have shipped inside a gate that was green at 47/47, and the first operator to select the venue would have met a model that silently could not be reached.** | private register R-119 | fixed in staging, not in the boot image |
| **Raising `lo` does not weaken the profile, and the claim is measured rather than asserted.** `lo` is a kernel pseudo-device present in every network namespace, attached by no monitor, reaching nothing outside the guest; `network_device_count` counts virtual NICs the monitor attaches. The same live run that carried the bytes also measured the guest's non-loopback link count at ZERO. And it is raised in STAGING, so a VM that declared no model channel keeps a guest whose loopback is down exactly as before. | the live probe asserts the link count beside the bytes | narrower than it looks, and checked |
| **A test that hangs on the failure it exists to detect is worse than no test.** The probe's first cut joined the stand-in endpoint's thread unconditionally; when the guest never dialled, that thread sat in `accept()` forever and the whole test HUNG — seventeen minutes, no result, and I removed its live socket while diagnosing because I misread a `pgrep` loop as having exited. Every wait in the probe is now bounded, and every failure prints the guest's own view plus the tail of its serial log. | the failing run now returns in 180s with `accepted: 0` and the guest's exact error | fixed |
| **The gate stopped printing an outstanding item that had since been measured.** It now RUNS the live probe where `/dev/kvm` and a provisioned toolchain exist, and reports SKIP by name where they do not — a skip is never counted as a pass. Leaving the old line in place would have been its own false record, pointed the other way from the one R-107 warned about. | the gate's closing line is now conditional on what it actually did | corrected |

### Journey verification LXVII (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 4 — M13.10 slice B: the channel is armed where it is declared, and staged where it cannot be exported)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The arming seam already existed and is called `connectivity_profile`.** A brokered model channel IS an egress statement, so the declaration rides the recipe's `egress_policy` — `brokered_model_only` against a default of `default_deny_external` — rather than minting a second field, and a second place where connectivity gets decided. Grepped before minting, as the rule says; the field, its default and its owner were all already there. | `check:microvm-model-broker` 47/47 | declared, not invented |
| **Armed per environment, never by being a microVM.** An environment that says nothing gets no host listener and no staged binary. This is the same reasoning that keeps the proxy out of the initramfs: a VM that declared no model channel must not carry the binary that opens one, and must not have a host end listening for it either. | two Rust tests, one of which walks four silent recipe shapes | built |
| **The host end comes up BEFORE the VM, and that ordering is asserted rather than incidental.** The per-port socket is what cloud-hypervisor connects to when the guest dials; a window where the guest can dial an absent listener is a window where the harness reads a closed connection as a dead model. The binding is likewise set on the spec before the declaration is minted, so the record describes the VM that is about to exist rather than the one that did. | the gate asserts both orderings by source position | ordered |
| **The staged binary is moved out of `/workspace` as part of staging, not as cleanup.** `handle_export` tars `/workspace` wholesale and the host writes it back — so a binary left there lands in the operator's checkout and breaks **the very clause M13.10 exists to satisfy**, a byte-exact host tree before and after. It goes to `/tmp`, which the agent already uses for its own transfer scratch and never exports. Cleanup-on-success would have been a promise; a move before any task runs is a property. | `guest_model_proxy_left_in_exported_workspace` is checked in-guest after the move | fixed before it could ship |
| **Two things are PROVEN in-guest rather than assumed from a zero exit.** `&` reports that the shell forked, not that the program survived its first instruction — a proxy that died on a bad argument would leave the exec green and the channel dead — so the pid is read back from `/proc`. And the workspace is re-checked for the staged name rather than trusted to the `mv`. The detached streams go to `/dev/null` for a reason the agent's own code dictates: `run()` reads the child's pipe to EOF, so a background process still holding it would hang the exec instead of returning. | `guest_model_proxy_not_running_after_start` | asserted, not assumed |
| **The pin is re-hashed at use, not trusted from the manifest.** The proxy's `binary_sha256` is checked against the bytes every time it is about to cross into a guest, the same fail-closed discipline `resolve_toolchain` applies to the monitor, kernel and initramfs. | `guest_model_proxy_hash_mismatch` | supply-pinned |
| **The census held its writer buckets for the second consecutive cut on this unit.** Tokens, opaque initialisers, foreign-qualified names and filesystem calls all moved — the three new filesystem calls being `create_dir_all`, `copy` and `remove_dir_all` on a HOST temp directory, none naming an ontology family — while family 57, non-ODK 252 and runtime-parameter 311 did not move at all. That is the census saying, in the only way it can, that this unit added a channel and not an admission path. | private register R-116; the repin documents the delta and its baseline | repinned with reasoning |
| **Still outstanding, and still said out loud.** The guest-initiated direction end to end needs a live boot, and the harness-in-guest lane is a LANE and not a flag: `run_host_spawn_lane` feeds the harness one task per stdin line and streams output, while the guest's `E` verb is one command in, one result out, with the agent pinned. The unit does not close here. | private register R-115; the gate prints its outstanding item every run | honest partial |

### Journey verification LXVI (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 4 — M13.10 slice A: a second channel that has to be declared before it may exist)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The channel the hostile-guest profile had was the only one it was allowed to have, and canon said so in one sentence.** "The only guest transport is the host-initiated, length-bounded vsock workspace protocol" is not decoration — it is the profile's claim, and a brokered model channel makes it false the moment one exists. ADR 0053 § 2 had already anticipated this and named the answer: the brokered channel is "a different profile from the hostile-guest one, exactly as the provider's own rule states". So the sentence is SCOPED rather than deleted, and a second profile is named beside it: `trusted_host_hostile_guest/no-nic-brokered-model-v1`. | `docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md` | canon amended in the same cut as the channel |
| **The declaration is a v2 successor, because the registry said it had to be.** `wire_mutation_policy: forbidden`, `additionalProperties: false`, and committed fixtures — three facts that together make naming a second channel a SUCCESSOR rather than an edit. v2 carries every v1 field byte-identical and adds exactly one: `broker_channel`, either the named channel or null, and **REQUIRED in both cases, because an absent field is not a claim.** `guest_channel` is untouched: the control channel really is still host-initiated and bounded, and the broker sits beside it rather than replacing it. | `check:microvm-model-broker` 37/37; the gate asserts the succession is recorded in BOTH directions | successor minted, projections regenerated |
| **A refusal AND a name, not one or the other.** I first ruled that the declaration needed no successor at all — that the narrow profile could simply refuse to be emitted beside a broker. That ruling rested on a grep piped through `head -20`, and the twentieth line was not the last: the venue path itself emits the declaration. The refusal alone would not have separated two profiles, it would have made the venue unable to provision. What ships is both: v2 NAMES the channel, and `workload_bound_enforcement_declaration` still refuses the binding outright, so the C8 effect boundary cannot acquire a second channel by arming a field. | private register R-111 (withdrawn, recorded), R-113 | the reversal stands in the register as it happened |
| **A tunnel carries a host and a port, NOT a path — so the destination is admitted, not configured.** This is the constraint that actually shapes the unit. Whatever listens at the destination is reachable in full by whatever the guest writes, so two refusals are typed and tested: a non-loopback destination (the channel terminates on the host; a remote address would restore the raw egress the absent NIC exists to deny) and **the daemon's own address** — which is not a hypothetical, because `executable_route_endpoint` resolves a remote route to exactly that, and the daemon's posture is local-trust: **51 `authorize(...)` call sites against 1117 handler functions.** | private register R-112; `ModelBrokerBinding` is constructed only inside the admitting function | refused by name, proven by test |
| **The residual is stated rather than fenced, because the transport cannot fence it.** At an admitted model endpoint the guest can also reach that server's non-chat API (Ollama's pull and delete) — a disk and availability exposure, not an authority crossing. Path scoping is impossible in a tunnel by construction, and a fence that checked only a first request line is one that keep-alive walks straight past. Canon and the gate both say so; the fence, when built, is a host-side model-only reverse proxy. | the gate's closing line names what it does not claim | accepted, named, not hidden |
| **No provider key enters the guest on any path, and the reason is better than the one I first wrote.** I had claimed the host attaches the credential when it connects onward — true of a design that does not exist. What is actually true: M13.9 already put the remote lane behind the DAEMON, which attaches the sealed bearer itself, so the guest never had a key to hold. The proxy's own header comment said the reassuring thing and now says the exact one. | `scripts/phase1/guest-model-proxy.c`; private register R-112 | corrected to what ships |
| **The guest binary is supply-pinned like the agent and deliberately NOT in the boot image.** The toolchain provisioner builds it and records both source and binary sha256 beside the agent's. It is staged per run over the bounded import path instead of riding in the initramfs, because **a VM with no model channel must not carry the binary that opens one.** | `scripts/phase1/provision-vm-toolchain.sh`; the gate asserts it is absent from the initramfs build | pinned, not shipped in the image |
| **The host end is proven to carry bytes, and proven to stay silent when it cannot.** Six Rust tests drive the real listener: a UNIX-socket client plays cloud-hypervisor's role, writes a request and reads it back from a loopback TCP server byte-for-byte. An unreachable destination closes without writing — and that assertion is pinned to `accepted_connections() == 1`, because an empty read also describes a broker that never accepted anything. | `cargo test -p ioi-node --bin hypervisor-daemon` 8 passed | built |
| **My own mutation-restore failed silently and left both planted defects in the tree.** `git checkout -- "$SCHEMA" "$MICROVM"` where `$SCHEMA` was a NEW untracked file: git matched no pathspec, errored, and restored NOTHING — including the tracked file beside it. I had also piped the run through a FAIL filter, discarding git's error. Only re-running the gate (35/37) found it. The gate caught both mutations, which is what it is for; the restore is what failed. | private register R-114 | both defects removed, gate re-verified 37/37 |
| **What this slice does NOT claim.** The guest-initiated direction end to end — that cloud-hypervisor surfaces a guest connection on `<sock_path>_<port>` with no handshake bytes of its own — cannot be proven without a live boot, and is named as outstanding in the gate's own closing line rather than implied to be covered. The unit stays `specified`: arming the broker in `provision_microvm`, staging the proxy over the `I` verb, and the harness-in-guest journey are slice B. | the gate prints its outstanding item on every run | honest partial; the unit does not close here |

### Journey verification LXV (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 2 — M09.4: a restore that proved its bytes came back and nothing else)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **`post_restore_validation` had no arm in the stage compiler at all.** It has been in the closed change-plan stage ladder since the environment plane was built, and it fell through the catch-all — discharging on evidence-ref presence alone. The stage beside it, `restore_apply`, checks every manifest digest, which is a complete proof of blob presence and no proof of ACC-8 clause 5: **a byte-faithful restore that also resurrects a record deleted before the backup satisfies every one of those digests.** | `check:canonical-environment-backup` constructs exactly that case and shows the manifest digests IDENTICAL across the break the family heads catch | built — the stage now decides continuity |
| **The witness was already in the contract and always written empty.** `source_object_head_refs` has been on `HypervisorEnvironmentBackup` since v1, and `compile_backup_record` emitted `[]` for every backup the plane ever took. No successor was needed; the field's authors had put it there for exactly this, and this is the first unit in this leg where the honest answer was NOT a new contract version. | private register R-90 | filled, not succeeded |
| **Why family roots decide what digests cannot.** Each root is derived from the RECORDS present at capture — never from stored bytes, and never from a head field a restore could itself write back. A deletion before the backup is expressed as an absence inside the root, so a restore that brings the record back produces a different root while every byte it wrote verifies. One commitment answers both halves of the clause, which is why there is no separate tombstone census: a census of what was deleted would have to be complete to be worth anything, and an absence is already total. | seven drills, including set semantics — reordering a family's roots changes nothing, because a family is a set and the kernel sorts | the two questions are separable, and now separated |
| **A design flaw the daemon's own ladder test found, not review.** The first cut committed and compared all four families. It can never pass: taking a backup appends to `backups` and running a restore appends to `change_plans`, so the plane at validation differs from the plane at capture BY CONSTRUCTION — which would have made continuity unachievable rather than strict. **You cannot restore the act of restoring.** `RESTORE_SUBJECT_FAMILIES` names what a restore is answerable for, read by both the commitment and the check so the two cannot drift. | private register R-90; the gate asserts both sides read the one constant | fixed before it shipped |
| **The legacy lane's disposition is a name in the bytes.** ACC-8 clause 6 asks whether every backup byte has an owner and is reachable; the managed lane captures a workspace tarball and has none of the environment plane's families. It commits `managed_workspace` — its own subject family, which the environment plane never rebuilds — so a restore claiming environment continuity from a managed backup is refused BY NAME rather than by having nothing to compare. I first committed it under `cleanup_obligations` because that name was already in the subject set; that was a convenient lie about what the lane captures, and it is not what shipped. | the gate asserts the live capture's head is under `managed_workspace` | dispositioned |
| **The credential-bound half is proven offline and the gate says which half is which.** The change-plan ladder crosses wallet-network authority, so the admission transaction is proven by 21 kernel tests — one of which drives the whole ladder and asserts `restore_apply` PASSES on a byte-faithful restore while `post_restore_validation` refuses it. A missing credential blocks a live RUN, never a unit. | private register R-91 | scheduled remainder is the live wallet crossing only |
| **The live lane earns its keep by being a differential, not a re-read.** The gate recomputes the committed head ref independently in JavaScript — canonical JSON, SHA-256, the same domain string — and compares it to what the Rust kernel minted on a real capture, so a change both sides made together still fails. A verifier that only read back the daemon's own number would be asking the thing under test to grade itself. | `check:canonical-environment-backup` 25/25, wired into CI and the pre-push sweep | authored; the unit closes, 61 → 60 blockers |
| **A verifier defect caught on the same run.** One assertion used `heads.every(...)` over an array that was empty, and `[].every()` is TRUE — an assertion that could not fail, passing while the daemon had committed nothing. It was visible only because an unrelated failure on the same run made the empty list obvious. A partial reading as a pass, wearing a verifier's clothes. | the length guard now sits in front of it, with the reason written beside it | fixed |

### Journey verification LXIV (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 2 — M09.2: a plan that could only be inspected after the thing it plans had started)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **`HypervisorEnvironmentStartupPlan` did not exist.** Canon specifies a 46-field immutable bridge from a resolved recipe to one concrete startup attempt — what will start, where, which gates must become ready, which evidence the lifecycle must emit. The only occurrence of the name anywhere outside canon was `environment_startup_plan_ref`, listed in `project_discovery_routes.rs` among the fields a discovery proposal may NOT carry: the plane next door correctly refusing to mint something that had no owner. | the registered `hypervisor-environment-startup-plan/v1` with 8 fixtures, two of them invariant negatives | built |
| **A plan could only be inspected AFTER the thing it plans had started.** `resolve_recipe` is the only producer of a resolution, and both its callers are inside environment CREATION — which resolves, runs the recipe's tasks and starts its services in one pass. ACC-11 clause 3 says the startup plan is inspectable BEFORE it runs; a plan obtainable only afterwards does not satisfy a weaker version of that clause, it inverts it. Found while writing the gate, not while reading the unit. | private register R-87; `POST /v1/hypervisor/environment-recipes/:id/resolutions` | fixed — resolution is a pure derivation, so the lane adds no capability, only the ability to exercise it without the side effects |
| **The module's three `validate_architecture_contract` calls were ALL inside `#[cfg(test)]`, so production validated nothing.** A registered family whose production writers never check is the M10.5 pattern, and here it was wearing a healthy grep count: `grep -c` returned 3 and the number was true; every one of them was unreachable from a running daemon. **The lesson is the measurement: a count of call sites is not a count of CALLS.** What caught it was reading the line numbers against the `#[cfg(test)]` boundary. | private register R-86, corrected within the hour on my own ruling | fixed — both `persist_record` calls are contract-checked before the write |
| **The bare-name rename lands where canon puts it, and only there.** The resolution plane stores `recipe_ab12`, `reso_cd34`, `gate_ef56` and a plain-string `environment_ref` — the unqualified naming canon calls a defect, since `DataRecipe`, `HypervisorSessionLaunchRecipe`, `WorkflowTemplate` and `GoalRunProfile` would all answer to a generic `recipe_…`. Canon's instruction is exact: the historical spellings are READ-ONLY v1 compatibility aliases, and canonical state emits the owner-qualified names. So stored records keep their spellings (rewriting them would break every persisted resolution for a naming change) and the plan — a new contract with no legacy data — requires the qualified form from its first byte. The bare id survives as the final path segment, so the mapping is reversible by inspection and no identity is invented. | M05.7 had already qualified the ROUTE; this ledger recorded the residue as *"M09.2 still owns the `DevelopmentEnvironmentRecipe` OBJECT rename behind the now-qualified route"* | that residue is discharged |
| **Three defects the tests found rather than confirmed, each invisible by reading.** The revision counter filtered stored plans on the BARE environment id while plans are stored qualified, so the filter matched nothing, every plan minted as `revision/1`, and each successor silently overwrote its predecessor — patch-in-place arriving through a comparison rather than through an update. The required-edge check had the same shape in the other direction: comparing a resolution's `db`/`5432` to a plan's `service://…/db`/`port://…/5432` by equality refuses every plan, and the natural fix of comparing two non-empty lists accepts every plan — **both look like a working check from outside**. And my own test fixture was refused by the contract I had just wired into production, which is that validation doing its job on first contact with a hand-written record. | private register R-88; the comparison is now by whole final path segment, with a drill declaring the wrong port number | all three fixed |
| **Mutable recipe aliases are refused by content, not by rule.** The gate re-admits a recipe under the SAME id with different bytes and proves the admitted plan still commits the bytes it froze. A plan tracking its recipe by name alone would silently start something other than what was inspected, which is exactly why canon pairs the ref with a content hash — and why the admission computes that hash from the STORED recipe and refuses a caller-asserted one (INV-37). | `check:environment-startup-plan` 20/20, wired into CI and the pre-push sweep | authored; the unit closes, 62 → 61 blockers |
| **The run ends by counting.** Exactly two plans exist after fourteen refusals, so every refusal admitted nothing. A gate that checks status codes without checking what was written cannot tell a refusal from a refusal-then-write. | the final assertion | the count is the claim |

### Journey verification LXIII (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 1 — M08.8: a compiler whose header named five projections and served three)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Two of the five projections ACC-10 clause 2 requires did not exist.** The product-surface compiler's own header claimed to feed "nav / catalog / palette / launch state from registration records" and returned workspaces and applications only. | **Membership of a rendered product lane came from a screenshot comparison.** Fourteen ported tool surfaces appeared in the product because `shell_pixel_certified` was true for them in the harvest parity matrix. Canon gives parity evidence "zero authority over registration class, catalog membership, owner, capability, or maturity", and the band had even been LABELLED `catalog_authority: false` while its membership stayed parity-derived — **a label is not a boundary**. A verifier downstream had hardened the arrangement into an acceptance test reading *"catalog membership equals certified surfaces plus exact contract-evidence admissions"*, which is this unit's scope bullet about parity artifacts behaving like acceptance tests, in the form of a passing assertion. | private register R-82; `buildAppCatalog` deleted rather than corrected | fixed — all fourteen register as `surface_class: tool_surface` over eleven axes; the matrix now decides membership in nothing |
| **Registering them surfaced an axis conflation and a route collision that clause 1 forbids.** The surface registry's `operational_state` field carries `browse \| act \| workflow_complete` — canon's **capability-depth** vocabulary, living under the operational-state axis's name, which is exactly the inference between independent axes Non-Negotiable 36 forbids. And several tool surfaces carry a `canonical_route` that is their FAMILY's landing (`monitors` carries `/automations`, which the `automations` owner application already claims), so registering them as written would have put two registrations on one click target while proving clause 1. | the gate asserts every click target resolves to exactly one designated surface, with a collision drill | both fixed at registration time |
| **The 401 extractor-ordering defect recurred, and the fence written for it turned out to be redundant.** The new gate's first live run found the projection answering an anonymous caller **415** rather than 401 — `Json<Value>` runs axum's body extractor before the handler is entered, so a caller who sent nothing is refused for its content type instead of for being nobody. **Identical defect and identical fix to M07.4 six hours earlier in this same leg**: it is a property of the handler signature, so every new handler taking `Json<Value>` before an identity check has it. | **"All eleven axes non-null" was the wrong bar and would have made the daemon fabricate.** It failed on `embodied-systems`, registered `surface_availability: planned` — no admitted release, no installation, no serving binding, so seven axes are null **because those records do not exist**. The bar is two-tier: the four axes fixed at registration are always non-null, and the seven join-derived ones may be null only where the row carries a typed reason code. All eleven KEYS are present either way, because "registered as none" and "not registered" are different answers. | private register R-84; three drills cover the three ways it goes wrong | the honest bar is stricter than the naive one, not looser |
| **The drills run against predicates rather than against the tree.** Every assertion is a pure function of a projection object, so a drill feeds it a mutated copy and proves the predicate refuses — nothing is planted in a tracked file, so nothing can be left planted. The structural half is proved by unreadability instead: no membership path reads the parity matrix or `shell_pixel_certified` at all, and an unreadable input cannot be mutated into an influence. | `check:product-surface-compiler` 38/38 with eleven drills, wired into CI and the pre-push sweep | authored; the unit closes, 63 → 62 blockers |
| **The gate's first assertion caught a stale binary before anything downstream could lie about it.** It reads `target/debug/hypervisor-daemon` and does not build it, so it refuses outright when the binary is older than the sources it claims to measure — the precondition written after a stale-binary reading cost a full false measurement earlier in this program, firing correctly on its first real opportunity. | the first run's `STALE — rebuild before trusting anything below` | the guard earns its place |

### Journey verification LXII (2026-09-13, MVP finish-line program, leg 1 — M10.5: a plane that wore canon's names while sharing one field with them)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Two live routes served canon's `FoundrySpec` and `FoundryRunPlan` NAMES while sharing exactly ONE property with canon's shape — `status`.** Measured field by field before building, because the standing ruling framed the work as "wire them to a registry validation call" and that wiring was not available. Fifteen fields are canon-only (`base_model_refs`, `training_mode`, `dataset_snapshot_refs`, `packaging_targets`, `budget_policy_ref`, `objective`, `task_family`, `version`, …) and fourteen daemon-only (`kind`, `model_route_refs`, `provider_refs`, `backend_refs`, `endpoint_refs`, `inputs`, …). Canon specifies a model-TRAINING pipeline; the daemon serves an eval and route-comparison draft. The module claimed the names explicitly in its own header, so this was not two families that happened to collide. | private register R-76 | measured before building on the estimate |
| **The code's name moved, not canon's shape, and the choice was ruled on the unit's acceptance rather than on convenience.** ACC-12's clause is a NEGATIVE — no Foundry surface claims a family whose wire contract is unregistered — which both convergence and renaming satisfy. The scope then settled it: registration is "the ruled precondition for ANY Foundry surface claim", so a registered family awaiting its producer is the INTENDED state. Both paths were costed first: convergence touches six daemon modules plus the approvals surface and invalidates every record ever written, where renaming touches two constants and a header. That the smaller change and the more honest one agreed is the reason to trust it. | private register R-77; the 22/22 foundry suite passing through the rename | ruled and built |
| **Every malformed body was a 201.** These routes called `validate_architecture_contract` ZERO times against 299 such call sites elsewhere in the estate, and minted a `schema_version` present in no registry. And `spec_content_hash` is required, so a later spec edit surfaces as drift rather than silently re-aiming a plan. `inputs` and `steps` stay unconstrained as a stated nonclaim: nothing dispatches them, and constraining their shape would assert a contract over something no runtime reads. | the registered draft contracts and their fixtures | built |
| **A performance claim now carries the fingerprint set canon asks for, and three of its six elements had no field anywhere before this unit.** `model_fingerprint`, `recipe_fingerprint` and `time_to_quality` were zero-hit repo-wide. The split is not cosmetic: one composite cannot answer WHICH axis changed between two measurements, so two runs differing only in `software_fingerprint` are a software regression while the same two differing only in `hardware_fingerprint` are not a regression at all. The topology axis also stops being degenerate — v1 pinned its scope to a single daemon CPU process, so the axis was present in name and impossible in fact. | `foundry-qualified-measurement/v2`, registered as a SUCCESSOR because v1 forbids wire mutation and carries records | built |

Deriving the list externally is what makes it a check rather than a tautology. | `check:foundry-spec-contracts` 20/20 with four mutation drills | authored; the unit closes, 64 → 63 blockers |
